# Non-spec changes: Session suspend, idle suspension, and resume driver

## Design (implementation-facing)

This proposal lands after proposal 0091. CODE-4 deletes the `ErrHeld` hold in `holdOrFailOnResumeError` that 0091 CODE-2 adds, and CODE-5 edits the `Sweep` loop that 0091 CODE-5 rewrites. Each edit is located by function and quoted text against the 0091 version of the code.

Each code deliverable states the invariants its code holds, the files it touches (its `Targets:` line), and the tests that pin each invariant. The SPEC item a deliverable cites is the normative statement of the behavior, and the deliverable does not restate it. Where the spec leaves a function-level choice open, the deliverable marks it **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE** and states its bound. Rationale and history are in the review log entry `[restructure.non-spec-to-constraints]`.

The components:

- **Watchdog** (`pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog`) lists rows and calls optional hooks that the session server implements: `ResumeDriver` (CODE-4), and `SuspendedPodReleaser`, `IdleSuspender`, and `BacklogResumer` (CODE-6). It gains `sweepSuspended` (CODE-6 item 2) and measures session age as active time (CODE-1).
- **Session server** (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver`) implements the hooks.
- **Binder** (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession`) gains the post-claim callback (CODE-3) and `ReleaseUncommitted` (CODE-4 item 5.3).
- **Coordination Sweeper** (`pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination`) widens its adoption predicate, holds no lease for a podless `suspended` session, calls the `HeldDeliverer` hook after a takeover publish, and hands each terminal row this replica binds to the `TerminalReleaser` hook (CODE-5, D6).
- **Gateway leader** (`cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go`) runs the podless `suspended` backlog re-check (CODE-6 item 16).
- **Runtime adapter** (`pkg/adapter`) records each session's last activity, including a runtime's `busy` report, and seals a session against `message` delivery after an idle window without activity (CODE-6 items 4 and 18). The runtime SDKs (`sdks/runtime/{go,python,typescript}`) report `busy` (CODE-6 item 18.4).

**Design lease gate.** (D1) The resume driver, the suspension release, the idle suspension, the backlog resume of a row that names a pod, and every held-pod resume act only when the session's `REG-COORDLEASE` names this replica. They act on every session when no lease store or no pod binder is wired. A lease read that fails counts as "not the holder". Each caller states its outcome on a refusal. Pinned by intents C4.23, C6.10, C6.17, and C6.44.

**Design binding check.** (D2) When a pod binder is wired and this replica's pod registry holds no binding for the session, the suspension release, the idle suspension, and the backlog resume return before any lease read. Pinned by intent C6.21.

**Per-session delivery lock.** (D3) `Server` gains `deliveryLocks`, a second instance of the per-session lock type in `slot_bind.go`, separate from `slotAccountLocks`. On one replica it makes each of the following one critical section against the delivery of held messages:

- the REST and MCP message paths, from the classification read through the hold or the send (taken honoring the request context);
- `completeResume`, from before its commit until item 5 step 6; `resumeHeldAndDrain` and `handleStart`, from before their `running` write until `deliverBuffered` returns; the takeover delivery (CODE-4 item 26), from before its `ReadPrimary` until `deliverBuffered` returns;
- the `lenny/delegate_task` held delivery.

Never take `slotAccountLocks` while holding the delivery lock, and never take the delivery lock while holding `slotAccountLocks`. The lock is not reentrant, so never call `ResumeSuspended` while holding it. `ReleaseSuspendedPod`, `SuspendIdle`, `enterResumePending`, and `BacklogRechecker.Pass` take no delivery lock. The lock orders nothing across replicas. Across replicas, the guarded row writes (D4) order the state changes, and the adapter's session seal (CODE-6 item 4 **Session seal.**) orders every delivery against an idle suspension. Cite `// spec: §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6)`. Pinned by intents C6.33 and T1.22.

**Session-row read authority.** (D4) `Get`, `GetByID`, `List`, and `ListByRoot` read the §12.3 read pool, which lags the primary.

- A read-pool row may select work and serve a listing, a client-facing status, or a request's entry precondition check (`loadMessageTarget`, the MCP target lookup, `handleInterrupt`, `handleTransition`, `handleDelete`), and it may stop a held-message delivery when it reads terminal (CODE-4 item 6 **Terminal stop.**). It never decides a write. Every session-row write in this proposal re-evaluates, inside its `Update` mutator on the locked row, the state and every field its selection used: `UpdatedAt` for a window, `CoordinationGeneration`, `PodAssignment`, `SuspendedAt`, or `LastAgentActivityAt`. An active age past its cap is recomputed on the locked row (CODE-1 item 4). Work that writes no session row, such as a checkpoint or a `HeldLen` count, may act on the listed row.
- A one-shot decision that has no write of its own reads the primary through `sessionstore.ReadPrimary(ctx context.Context, st Store, timeout time.Duration, tenantID, id string) (Session, error)`, new in `sessionstore.go`. Such a decision is a route, a skip, a nil return, or the expected value of a compare-and-swap. `ReadPrimary` writes nothing: it advances no `UpdatedAt`, accrues no session age (CODE-1 item 3), and returns `ErrNotFound` and every other error unchanged. It runs under `context.WithTimeout(ctx, timeout)`, and a `timeout` at or below zero uses the exported `DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout` (5 s), so no call is unbounded. On expiry it returns an error that wraps `context.DeadlineExceeded`. Session-server sites call one helper, `s.readPrimary(ctx, tenantID, id)`, which passes the `primaryReadTimeout` field. That field is set from `Options.PrimaryReadTimeout` and defaulted beside `streamFailureReportTimeout` in `sessionserver.go`. Every `mcptools` read passes `deps.PrimaryReadTimeout`, which `cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go` sets from the same flag: the MCP `lenny/send_message` classification read and its re-read after a seal refusal (CODE-6 item 7 **MCP**, in `mcptools_register.go`), the `lenny/delegate_task` read, and the `lenny/request_elicitation` read. The flag is `--session-primary-read-timeout` / `LENNY_SESSION_PRIMARY_READ_TIMEOUT`, with default `sessionstore.DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout`. Its help states that the value is operator-tunable and bounds each primary read a one-shot decision makes, and startup exits with an error on a non-positive value. The 5 s default matches the bound the activity stamp already puts on its primary write (`sessionidle.go`, `persist`). An expired deadline is a primary error: on the message paths it takes the **Primary error** column of the **Message-path read failures.** table, and at every other site it takes the path that site's read error takes. No spec or docs page states the value, as for `--stream-failure-report-timeout`. Its sites are `holdMessage` (CODE-4 item 7), `handleResume` (CODE-4 item 8), `attemptResume` step 1 (CODE-4 item 3.1), the row `ReportSessionFailure` routes (`failure.go`), `ReportAttachStreamFailure` (CODE-4 item 16), `persistDeriveFailure` (CODE-4 item 17), the `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 3 re-read (CODE-6 item 4), the `lenny/request_elicitation` terminal-state check (CODE-6 item 19.4), and the message-path reads in the table below.
- A mutator that decides not to write returns a package sentinel and never nil. The caller treats `errors.Is(err, <sentinel>)` as no change: it counts nothing, notifies nothing, runs no terminal hook, and continues. `Update` returns a zero `Session` with any error, so a caller that needs the row its guard read copies that row inside the mutator.
- **Guarded-write repeat rule.** A guarded `Update` whose outcome its caller classifies follows one rule. Its mutator copies the locked row it saw into a caller variable before it tests the guard, because `Update` returns a zero `Session` with any error. On a guard miss it changes nothing and returns the caller's not-applied sentinel. A store error does not say whether the write applied, so the caller repeats the same `Update`, with the same inputs, until it returns nil, the sentinel, or `sessionstore.ErrNotFound`, or the context ends. Each caller states how it classifies the outcome: `classifyCommit` reads the last try's row and whether any try returned a store error (CODE-4 item 5 step 3), and `classifyIdleWrite` runs inside the mutator, so a repeat classifies a row its own landed write left (CODE-6 item 4 step 3).

**IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** how `ReadPrimary` reaches the primary, for example an `Update` whose mutator copies the locked row and returns an unexported sentinel. Bound: it writes nothing and returns within its deadline. When the deadline expires during a primary partition, a row lock the read took stays on the primary until the primary ends the abandoned connection, as for any guarded write abandoned on the same path.

The watchdog's pre-running timeout sweep and `sweepAwaitingClientAction` keep their nil-returning mutators, and BUILD-GAPS F-10.1.34 and F-11.3.40 track them under P3. Cite `// spec: §12.3 (Postgres HA Requirements)` on `ReadPrimary`. Pinned by intents C4.3, C4.4, C4.5, C6.39, and T1.31.

**Message-path read failures.** (D5) Two invariants govern the reads on the REST and MCP message paths. (I1) A new message is never handed to the runtime ahead of a held entry that the delivering replica can read (SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.**). An entry that `Peek` or `PeekOldest` cannot read is outside I1. (I2) No session-row read on these paths refuses or stalls a message to a session whose entry row reads `running` during a §12.3 primary failover or partition, unless an idle suspension has sealed the session. The REST tenant-suspension and injection-support gates in `loadMessageTarget` read the primary and are outside I2. The entry row is the read-pool row that `loadMessageTarget` or the MCP target lookup read before the delivery lock. The classification read falls back to that row. A stale fallback row cannot end a turn, because the adapter of a session an idle suspension sealed refuses the delivery (CODE-6 item 4 **Session seal.**). No other read falls back to the read pool. The held branch is CODE-6 item 7's, run on the classification row with `State` set to `suspended`. Pinned by intents C6.39, C6.31, C6.38, T1.30, T1.31, and T1.32.

| Read | `ErrNotFound` | Primary error, read-pool row readable | Both unreadable |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| Classification read, under the delivery lock (`ReadPrimary`) | REST answers 404 `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` as `loadMessageTarget` does, and MCP answers `errSessionLookup`. Nothing is held or sent. | Classify the entry row, and log at WARN with the session ID. | Does not arise, because the entry read succeeded before the lock. |
| Re-read after a seal refusal (`executor.ErrSessionSealed`) on the `ActionDeliver` branch (`ReadPrimary`) | Held branch. | Held branch, with no read-pool fallback: a lagging row reads `running` and routes back to path 2. | Held branch. |
| `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` re-read (`ReadPrimary`) | Held branch. | Held branch, with no read-pool fallback. The inbox is not a valid destination when the row cannot rule out a recovering state. | Held branch. |
| `SessionDelivery.DeliverHeld` | Reads no row to classify. It delivers on the `running` row its caller passes. `deliverBuffered`'s **Terminal stop.** reads the read pool, never the primary. | As the `ErrNotFound` column. | As the `ErrNotFound` column. |
| `ResumeSuspended` | Reads no row. It branches on the row its caller passes, and every write it makes is guarded on the locked row. | Reads no row. | Reads no row. |
| delegate_task child read after the task input, under the delivery lock (`ReadPrimary`) | No delivery. | No delivery, with no read-pool fallback: the task-input turn can move the child out of `running`, and a lagging row cannot show it. | No delivery. |

The other reads on these paths state their own failure outcomes: `holdMessage`'s terminal re-read in CODE-4 item 7, `deliverBuffered`'s `Peek` and `PeekOldest` in CODE-4 item 6, and `HeldLen` at its callers in CODE-6 item 2, item 4 step 3a, and item 16.3.

Cite `// spec: §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §12.3 (Postgres HA Requirements)`.

**Terminal binding reclamation.** (D6) Invariant: a binding in a replica's pod registry whose session row is terminal is released, or evicted when a peer took the session over, within one Sweeper interval of the later of the first completed `List` that returns the terminal row and the end of the last terminal claim this replica holds for the session (D7), whichever replica wrote the terminal state and whichever publish placed the binding.

- **Why the post-terminal hook is not enough.** The post-terminal hook (D7) releases only this replica's binding (`releaseExecutor`, which `PodExecutor.Release` no-ops without a binding). It therefore misses a publish that follows the terminal write, and every binding held on a replica other than the writer's. The watchdog runs on every replica, so the second case is routine.
- **Publish sites.** Each site publishes after a row read or a guarded row write, and a terminal write can land between that read or write and the `Put`:
  - `completeResume`, the item 5 Publish row, on both branches, after the commit;
  - the Sweeper readopt (CODE-5 item 2), after `RecordHandoff` and during the dial and fence;
  - `handleStart` through `registerBinding`, while the row is `ready`, before the item 21 guarded `transitionStart`;
  - `handleCreateAndStart` through `publishBinding`, after `store.Create`;
  - `MaterializeDelegatedChild` through `publishBinding`, after its item 21 guarded running-commit, where a parent's `cascadeToChildren` can cancel the child.
  `resumeHeldPod`, `ResumeSuspended`, `SuspendIdle`, `ReleaseSuspendedPod`, and tree recovery publish nothing.
- **Trigger.** In `Sweep`'s terminal branch, the Sweeper calls `TerminalReleaser.ReleaseTerminalBinding(row)` when this replica binds the row (CODE-5 item 2d). The item 5 terminal re-check row calls the same method as a fast path.
- **Decision.** `(s *Server) ReleaseTerminalBinding` (CODE-4 item 27) returns at once. It runs one goroutine per session on the server-lifetime context, at most one in flight per session on this replica, and records it in the terminal claim record (D7). **Deferral.** This is the one statement of when it defers. It does nothing on a non-terminal row, and nothing while this replica holds an open terminal claim for the session (D7 **Claim.**) or runs a D6 goroutine for it. A writer opens its claim before its terminal `Update` and ends it after its post-terminal hook returns, so on the writer's replica a Sweeper-triggered release never precedes the hook's seal-and-export, its drain, or its terminal signals, which §7.1 steps 20 to 24 and §29.4 steps 11 to 17 order before the credential-lease and pod release. The claim opens before the commit because every listing that can return the terminal row is then taken while the claim is open or after the hook returned; a claim taken after the commit would leave the interval between the commit and the claim open. A deferred call is not queued: the Sweeper calls again on its next listing of the bound terminal row. The goroutine takes `slotAccountLocks` for the session, honoring that context, so a release follows a failure funnel that is still accounting the slot it moved to `failed` (item 22). It then reads the current binding and does one of the following:
  - when the registry has none, it does nothing;
  - when the binding's `CoordinationGeneration` is 0 or `row.CoordinationGeneration` exceeds it, it calls `evictStaleBinding`, with no binder call, because the replica that advanced the generation owns the pod's release, and an unstamped binding fails closed as `reportSupersededByPeer` treats it;
  - otherwise it calls `releaseReportedBinding(ctx, row.ID, bind, dispositionForState(row.State))`, whose identity check releases only the binding it read.
  The release returns that binding's leases, as **Credential lease return.** below states. A lease above the stamp belongs to the attempt that minted it, and that attempt returns it through `ReleaseUncommitted` or `failResume`, unless its reclaim left the pod unconfirmed (CODE-4 item 5.3). The goroutine takes no delivery lock. Each reclaim is logged at INFO with the session ID, state, and action.
- **Credential lease return.** This is the one statement of which component returns a session's credential leases. A lease is returned only by the call that releases the binding the lease belongs to, by the attempt that minted it, by identifier, while that attempt's binding is unpublished, or by the renewal or rotation that replaces it, as the sub-bullets below state. No component returns a lease by session ID for a binding it does not hold, except the departure stated below. A second return of a lease already returned finds no record and changes nothing, so the rule limits which component may return a lease rather than how often one is returned.
  - A published binding's leases are its `MintedLeaseIDs` and every lease of the session whose recorded generation is at or below the binding's stamp, which `releasePodCredentials` returns (CODE-4 item 24, SPEC-1g). Only `Binder.Release` and `Binder.ReleaseSlot` call it, on the replica whose registry holds the binding. Their callers are the hook's release half (D7 **Hook.** step 6, through `releaseExecutor`), D6, the failure funnel's `releaseReportedBinding` (CODE-4 item 22), the suspension release (CODE-6 item 4 step 4), `releaseEarlierBinding`, `rollbackBinding`, and the executor's `Close` and `ReleaseSession`.
  - A pre-running claim binding is a `created`, `finalizing`, or `ready` row whose `PodAssignment` names a claim that no registry holds. It carries only the leases that `/finalize`'s `Prepare` minted, because no other path mints a lease for a session row that names a pod before a registry binding exists. `ReclaimClaimed` and `failPhase` return them with `ReleaseSession`. Their callers release that claim: the hook's release half through `terminalReclaimPreRunning`, the created-expiry sweeper, the create rollback, the finalize handler after a failure write that returned a store error, and `Prepare` and `Launch` on a failed reconnect or step. When a `/start` on another replica publishes a registry binding over the same claim, both releases reach the same leases, and the later one finds no record.
  - An attempt whose binding is never published returns, by identifier, the leases it minted: `Bind` and `BindSlot` through `releaseAttemptCredentials`, `Binder.Resume` through `failResume` (CODE-4 item 20.4), and the resume driver through `ReleaseUncommitted` (CODE-4 item 5.3), except after an unconfirmed reclaim. A minting call that records a lease and then returns an error returns that lease itself (CODE-4 item 24.7).
  - `revokeFinalizeLease` (`finalize.go`) returns the session's leases by session ID after a terminal writer overtook `/finalize`, for a binding it does not hold. This is a recorded departure from the rule: BUILD-GAPS F-4.9.35, owned by P1.
  - The §11.4 `full_revoke` step and the §4.9 credential revocation remove lease records for a security reason. This rule does not govern them.
  - A renewal or rotation returns, by identifier, the lease it replaces or the replacement it minted and could not hand over: `credRenewalWiring.Renew` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go`, CODE-4 item 23 step 2) and the Token Service's `RotateCredentials` (`pkg/tokenservice/grpc.go`).
  - A lease whose binding no releaser releases is returned only by a later pod release of the session whose binding stamp is at or above the lease's generation, or at its expiry, as Edge cases **Credential leases with no binding releaser.** states.
  Pinned by intents C4.43, C4.53, C4.64, C4.65, C6.20, T1.14, and X2.
- **Read authority.** It acts on the listed read-pool row (D4) and writes no session row. A terminal state is absorbing, and `RecordHandoff` refuses a terminal row. The §7.2 snapshot-close bump is made inside the terminal write (D7 **Mark.**), so no generation write follows a terminal write and a listed terminal row carries its final generation.
- **Cost.** The Sweeper already lists every terminal row of every tenant (RECORDS-1 item 7(l)). D6 adds one registry `Get` per terminal row and one goroutine per bound terminal row.
- **Accepted gaps.** A sweep that returns early on a `List` or lease `Get` error defers the release to the next completed listing. A release error after the unbind leaves the pod to the claim-orphan GC. A row hard-deleted while bound is never listed, so the claim-orphan GC drains its pod, the registry entry lasts until the process restarts, and its credential leases follow Edge cases **Credential leases with no binding releaser.** On a replica other than the writer's, the release is not ordered after the writer's hook steps 4 and 5. It can return the leases and release the pod before the drain, the terminal signals, and the `cascadeOnFailure` evaluation, which §29.4 orders first. A `full_revoke` write opens no claim. A D6 release on the handling replica can therefore close a binding's connection while the §11.4 fan-out's `Shutdown` is in flight, and the fan-out then lists that session under `podTerminationFailures`. Owners: P1, and P5 for `full_revoke`.
- **Why this proposal owes it.** CODE-5 item 1 adds held-pod `suspended` to adoption (`input_required` is already adopted). The terminal exits of a `suspended` row (the CODE-6 item 2 lifetime expiry and a DELETE) run on any replica, so without D6 an adopted `suspended` binding is never released. D6 also closes the same gap for `running` rows.
- **Rejected alternatives.** D3 and `slotAccountLocks` order nothing across replicas. A per-site terminal re-read leaves the cross-replica case open. A by-name reclaim in the funnel bypasses item 24.

Cite `// spec: §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination), §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior)`. Pinned by intents C5.9, C4.53, C4.35, C4.49, the publish-site intents C4.55, C4.56, and C5.10, T1.14, and T1.25.

**Terminal transition.** (D7) A terminal write is an `Update` whose mutator sets `State` to `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, or `expired`, or a `Create` of a row in one of those states. The **Terminal writer inventory.** table below holds the one list of terminal writers and the path each takes, and intent C4.49 pins it. No other part of this proposal lists them.

**Terminal writer inventory.** Each row gives the writer, its path, the function that takes the claim and calls the hook, and the reason for a `none` row. CODE-6 adds the `sweepSuspended` row in S15.

| Writer | Path | Claim and hook, or reason |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `session_transitions.go` `handleDelete` | `funnel` | `handleDelete`, `recordSessionCompleted` |
| `session_transitions.go` `transitionTerminate` (marked in the `handleTransition` mutator) | `funnel` | `handleTransition`, `recordSessionCompleted` |
| `failure.go` `transitionToFailed` | `funnel` | `transitionToFailed`, `recordSessionCompleted` |
| `session_terminal.go` `expireSession` | `hook` | `expireSession`, `AfterTerminalWrite` |
| `session_terminal.go` `failSession` | `hook` | `failSession`, `afterFailed` |
| `session_terminal.go` `failFinalizing` | `hook` | `failFinalizing`, `afterFailed` |
| `usage.go` `cascadeToChildren` | `hook` | `cascadeToChildren`, `AfterTerminalWrite` on a goroutine |
| `watchdog.go` `Tick` (pre-running timeout), `sweepAwaitingClientAction`, `sweepIdle`, `sweepMaxAge`, and `sweepSuspended` (CODE-6) | `funnel` | the same function, `recordCompleted` |
| `orphansession.go` `failOrphan` | `funnel` | `failOrphan`, `OnSessionTerminal` |
| `orphancleanup.go` `sweepTenant` | `funnel` | `sweepTenant`, `OnSessionTerminal` |
| `cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go` `ForceTerminate` | `funnel` | `ForceTerminate`, `onTerminal` |
| `cmd/lenny-gateway/reconciler_workers.go` `startReconcilerWorkers` (the deadlock fail closure) | `funnel` | the closure, `OnSessionTerminal` |
| `cmd/lenny-gateway/session_budget.go` `terminate` | `funnel` | `terminate`, `onTerminal` |
| `mcptools_register.go` `registerSessionLifecycleTools` (the `lenny/cancel_session` handler) | `hook` | the handler, `Deps.Terminal` |
| `mcptools.go` `cancelSubtree` | `hook` | `cancelSubtree`, its `Terminal` parameter on a goroutine |
| `admin/users.go` `terminateUserSessions` | `none` | `handleInvalidateUser` sends `Shutdown` to the pod of each cancelled session that a registry binds right after the write, so a seal would race the shutdown, and a seal failure would relabel a committed `cancelled` session `failed`. `terminateUserSessions` cancels a same-replica resume attempt and drains the inbox and DLQ right after each write (CODE-4 item 2a), and `full_revoke` dismisses the user's pending elicitations. |
| `translator/openai_chat.go` `handleCreateCompletion` and `open_responses.go` `handleCreate` | `none` | Single-shot session: the handler's deferred `releaseSingleShot` releases the executor, and the write follows a successful `Send` in the same request. A `Send` whose stream failure the funnel reports during the request leaves the row in `resume_pending` or `failed`, and the translator writes nothing. The driver can then restore the session for no client, after which it is idle-suspended and later expires. A completion `Update` that fails leaves a `running` row with no binding. `SuspendIdle` skips that row (D2), and `sweepMaxAge` ends it. P2 makes the translators ordinary terminal writers (BUILD-GAPS F-15.1.46 and F-15.1.47). |
| `derive.go` `persistDeriveFailure` | `none` | It creates a derive-failure audit row directly in `failed`; no pod, binding, or resume attempt ever existed for it. |
| `seal.go` `failWorkspaceSeal` | `none` | It is hook step 3, and it relabels a row that is already terminal. |

- **Mark.** `sessionstore.MarkTerminated(r *Session, from session.State, now time.Time)` is new in `sessionstore.go`. `from` is the locked row's state before the mutator set the terminal state; a `Create` passes the empty state.
  - Every terminal mutator calls it after it sets the terminal state, so the Terminated fact (§7.2, §8.8) lands in the terminal write itself. `handleTransition`'s mutator calls it after `transition(row)`, and `persistDeriveFailure` calls it on its row before `Create`.
  - On a terminal `r`, it sets `TerminatedAt = now` when `TerminatedAt` is zero, and sets `TerminatedReason` from `r.State` (`Completed`, `Failed`, `Cancelled`, or `Expired`). On any other `r`, it changes nothing.
  - When `from` is `resuming`, whatever the terminal state, it increments `r.CoordinationGeneration` by one and leaves `RecoveryGeneration` unchanged, so the §7.2 snapshot-close bump (step 4) is in the terminal write's own transaction. No other edge bumps. Delete `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose` (`session_generation.go`) and its calls in `handleDelete` and `handleTransition` (`session_transitions.go`); CODE-4 removes its calls in `failure.go` (item 3.7) and `resume.go` (item 8).
  - `failWorkspaceSeal`'s relabel mutator also calls it, so a relabel to `failed` keeps the first `TerminatedAt` and rewrites the reason.
  - Delete `stampTerminatedCondition`, its call in `recordSessionCompleted`, and `terminatedReasonForState` (`usage.go`).
  - Cite `// spec: §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §8.8 (TaskRecord and TaskResult Schema)`.
- **Claim.** `(s *Server) BeginTerminal(sessionID string) (end func())` registers a writer claim for the session in the terminal claim record `terminalClaims`. It never blocks.
  - Every writer whose path is `funnel` or `hook` calls it before its terminal `Update`; `handleTransition` calls it for the terminate endpoint only. The writer calls `end` on every path after the `Update` returns: after the hook when the write applied, and at once on an error, on its not-applied sentinel, or when its guard found the row already terminal. `end` is idempotent.
  - D6 **Deferral.** states how an open claim defers `ReleaseTerminalBinding` and why the claim precedes the commit.
  - The failure funnel takes its claim under `slotAccountLocks` and runs the hook after its `unlock` (CODE-4 item 22), so the hook never waits while holding that lock.
- **Hook.** `(s *Server) AfterTerminalWrite(ctx context.Context, fromState session.State, sess sessionstore.Session, signals func(context.Context, sessionstore.Session)) sessionstore.Session`, new in `terminal_hook.go`, is the post-terminal hook and its one entry point. It fixes the order §7.1 steps 20 to 24 and §29.4 steps 11 to 17 state from the seal to the pod release (§29.1 **Step numbering.**).
  - The writer whose write applied calls it once, after its `Update` returns nil and before `end`.
  - `fromState` is the pre-terminal state the writer read, taken from the locked row where the writer captures it there. **Paths.** states the exceptions, for `finalizePrepareFailed` and an unprepared `finalizeReadyWriteFailed`.
  - `signals` is the writer's terminal-signal tail (**Paths.**). A nil `signals` runs none.
  - Steps, in this order:
    1. `s.CancelResumeAttempt(sess.ID)` (CODE-4 item 2a), so the attempt's restoration RPCs stop before the hook touches the pod.
    2. Wait, honoring `ctx`, for a D6 goroutine that holds the session in `terminalClaims` to exit. Such a goroutine exists only when the writer wrote a row that was already terminal. When `ctx` ends first, the hook skips steps 3 and 6, D6 owns the binding, and steps 4, 5, and 7 still run, as the funnel's terminal signals run today after a context end.
    3. Seal half (§7.1 step 20, §29.4 step 11). When `s.sealer` is set, call `sealWorkspace`, and on its error call `failWorkspaceSeal`, whose returned row replaces `sess`. With no local binding the Sealer returns nil. For a `resuming` row this is the §7.2 snapshot-close step 2 skip, because no binding names an uncommitted pod (CODE-4 item 4). For any other row the live workspace is on a binding this replica does not hold or has not yet published, and **When the hook does not run.** states the outcome.
    4. `s.drainMessagingOnTerminal(ctx, sess)` (§7.3 **DLQ drain on terminal transition:**, SPEC-5z item 1, §29.4 step 15). Delete its call in `emitTerminalLifecycle` (`lifecycle.go`) and retarget `TestEmitTerminalLifecycleDrainsDLQ_spec_7_3_12` to the hook.
    5. `signals(ctx, sess)`: the terminal event and the `cascadeOnFailure` evaluation of §29.4 step 15. Steps 4 and 5 are parts of one §29.4 step, whose parts the spec does not order.
    6. Release half (§7.1 steps 23 and 24, §29.4 steps 16 and 17). Call `terminalReclaimPreRunning(ctx, fromState, sess)`. When it reports false and `s.executor` is set, call `releaseExecutor(ctx, s.executor, sess.ID, dispositionForState(sess.State))`, logging an error. `Binder.Release` orders the lease release, `Shutdown`, and the claim change as RECORDS-1 item 7(u) records.
    7. `s.expireRaisedElicitations(ctx, sess)` (CODE-6 item 19), last, after the pod release, as §11.4 orders the elicitation dismissal after the lease revocation. An elicitation whose raise passed its terminal-state check before the terminal write and is recorded after this step stays pending, as SPEC-5p2 states for a missed expiry. CODE-6 adds this step in S15; before S15 the hook ends at step 6.
    8. Return `sess`.
  - Every step is best-effort, and none fails the terminal write. The hook writes no row except `failWorkspaceSeal`'s relabel.
- **Paths.** Each inventory row names one path.
  - `funnel`: the writer calls `recordSessionCompleted`, directly or through a `TerminalHook.OnSessionTerminal` or `onTerminal` seam. `recordSessionCompleted` calls `AfterTerminalWrite` as its first statement and passes `terminalSignals`, a new method that runs, on the row the hook passes it, the operational event, the callback, `emitTerminalLifecycle`, and the archive, child-failed, watermark, and cascade steps, in today's order. After the hook returns, on the row it returned, `recordSessionCompleted` runs the session-log, input-wait forget, budget forget, quota checkpoint, and billing steps. The seal precedes every terminal signal, and the release follows the operational event, the callback, and `emitTerminalLifecycle`, as today. The archive, child-failed, watermark, and cascade steps move ahead of the release, which today precedes them, because §29.4 step 15 places the `cascadeOnFailure` evaluation before steps 16 and 17.
  - `hook`: the writer calls `AfterTerminalWrite` with its own `signals`. `expireSession` and `afterFailed` (`failSession`, `failFinalizing`) pass a `signals` that runs `archiveSettledChild` and then `emitTerminalLifecycle`; `cascadeToChildren` passes one that runs `archiveSettledChild`; the `lenny/cancel_session` handler passes nil, because its write emits no terminal signal today. It runs the hook on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` and builds its result from the row the hook returns, so a seal relabel is answered `failed`. `cancelSubtree` passes a `signals` that archives the node from the row it is passed. `archiveCancelled` takes that row and archives its state with the code `CHILD_` plus the upper-cased state, as `mcptools.go` builds a child's code elsewhere. The `lenny/cancel_child` handler drops its own `archiveCancelled` call, and `signals` is nil when `deps.TreeArchive` is nil; rewrite `afterFailed`'s doc comment, which says the seal and executor release stay in `recordSessionCompleted`. Every `hook` writer captures `fromState` from the locked row in its mutator, and `afterFailed` gains a `fromState` parameter that it passes to the hook. A state read before the mutator, from the multi-session writers' pre-loop listing or the `lenny/cancel_session` handler's replica `Get`, can be pre-running for a session that a running-commit on another replica already moved to `running`, and would then send hook step 6 to a by-name `ReclaimClaimed` of a running session's claim, which bypasses the §6.2 recycle and the adapter scrub that the binding's replica runs. A `failFinalizing` that applied has `fromState` `finalizing`, which is pre-running, so hook step 6 reclaims the pod by name. `finalizePlanParseFailed`, and `finalizeReadyWriteFailed` when `prepared` is true (`finalize.go`), therefore drop their own `reclaimFinalizedPod` call after a `failFinalizing` that returned nil, and keep it after a store error, where no hook ran. `finalizePrepareFailed`, whose failing step already reclaimed the pod, calls `failFinalizing` with a `podReclaimed` flag, and `failFinalizing` then passes `session.StateFailed` as `fromState`, so step 6 does not delete the claim by name a second time; `podclaim.DeleteClaim` has no owner check, and a second by-name delete can remove a successor session's claim on the pod. `finalizeReadyWriteFailed` passes the same flag when `prepared` is false. An unprepared finalize is a service-mode session or a concurrent-workspace slot reservation (§5.2), and today it reclaims nothing there. A slot session still carries the shared pod in `PodAssignment` with no registry binding, so a by-name delete would remove the per-pod claim under its sibling slots, which the slot release through `ReleaseSlotReservation` avoids. A writer that terminates several sessions in one call (`cascadeToChildren` and `cancelSubtree`) runs each session's hook and `end` on a goroutine it starts and does not wait for, on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)`, so one session's seal window does not delay the next. `cascadeToChildren` drops its own `releaseExecutor` call, which step 6 replaces.
  - `none`: the mutator calls `MarkTerminated`; the writer takes no claim and runs no hook. The inventory row gives the reason. D6 releases any registry binding the write leaves, and Edge cases **Pre-running claim at a `none` terminal write.** states the outcome for a pre-running claim.
- **Seams.**
  - `BeginTerminal` joins `OnSessionTerminal` on the `TerminalHook` interfaces of `watchdog`, `orphansession`, and `orphancleanup`, and on the `cmd/lenny-gateway` adapters for `ForceTerminate` (`session_admin.go`) and the budget terminator (`session_budget.go`). The deadlock detector (`reconciler_workers.go`) calls the session server directly.
  - `mcptools.Deps` gains `Terminal`, a consumer interface with `BeginTerminal` and `AfterTerminalWrite`, which `cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go` sets to the session server. `cancelSubtree` takes it as a parameter.
  - `admin.Router` gains `revoked` (CODE-4 item 2a). `podTerminateFanOut` gains no field. When `terminateUserSessions` returns an error, `handleInvalidateUser` (`admin/users.go`) calls `runFullRevokeFanOut` with the IDs it returned before it answers the error. Cite `// spec: §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §11.4 (User Invalidation)`.
  - A writer whose seam is nil calls `MarkTerminated` alone.
- **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the terminal claim record. Bound: it is per replica; it counts open writer claims per session and holds at most one D6 goroutine per session; a goroutine starts only as D6 **Deferral.** allows; an entry with no claim and no goroutine is removed.
- **When the hook does not run.**
  - A terminal write that applied and then returned an error runs no hook on its replica. D6 releases this replica's binding, unsealed, within one Sweeper interval; the attempt sees the terminal row at its next guarded write (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**); the session's pending elicitations stay pending (SPEC-5p2); and its inbox and DLQ entries stay undrained, with no `message_expired` sent.
  - A process exit between the write and the hook leaves the pod to the claim-orphan GC and peer bindings to D6. When the exiting replica held the binding, its credential leases follow Edge cases **Credential leases with no binding releaser.**
  - A terminal write on a replica that holds no binding for the session runs the hook there. The seal and the release find nothing, and the binding's own replica releases it through D6, unsealed. This predates the proposal, because the funnel seals only a local binding. The same holds when a binding is published after the hook's seal step. The `completeResume` publish (CODE-4 item 5) and the Sweeper readopt publish (CODE-5 item 2) each follow their row write, and a terminal write in between finds no binding, on the writer's replica or another. D6 releases that binding unsealed after the writer's claim ends. The session's final workspace is its latest checkpoint snapshot. BUILD-GAPS F-7.1.22 (TT-3, P2) tracks a seal that finds no binding.
- Cite on `AfterTerminalWrite`: `// spec: §7.1 (Normal Flow), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §7.3 (Resume driver), §9.2 (Elicitation Chain), §29.4 (Interrupt, terminate, and delete)`.
- Pinned by intents C4.48, C4.49, C4.50, C4.51, C4.52, C4.53, C4.54, and T1.25.

## Staged code changes

### CODE-1 · Active-time session age in the store funnel

Targets: `migrations/0182_sessions_accumulated_session_age.up.sql` and `.down.sql` (the next number after 0181); `pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go`; `pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go`; `pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/memstore/memstore.go`; `pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go`; `cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go`; `pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/runtimestore.go`; `pkg/api/v1/session/retry_policy.go`; `pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json` and the generated `pkg/ops/mcp/generated_tools.go` and `pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/generated_schemas.go`.

1. **Migration.** `ALTER TABLE sessions ADD COLUMN accumulated_session_age_seconds double precision NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (accumulated_session_age_seconds >= 0)`. The down migration drops the column.
2. **Field and helpers** in `sessionstore.go`: the field `AccumulatedSessionAgeSeconds float64` on `Session`, and two exported helpers.
   - `AccrueSessionAge(prev session.State, prevUpdatedAt time.Time, row *Session, now time.Time)`: when `prev` is `starting`, `running`, or `input_required`, it adds `max(0, now - prevUpdatedAt)` in seconds to `row.AccumulatedSessionAgeSeconds`.
   - `ActiveAge(row Session, now time.Time) time.Duration`: the accumulated value, plus `now - row.UpdatedAt` when `row.State` is one of those counting states.
3. **The funnel.** In both stores' `Update`, after the mutator returns and before the `UpdatedAt` stamp, call `AccrueSessionAge(prev.State, prev.UpdatedAt, &row, now)` with the same `now` the stamp uses. Add the column to `Create`, to the `UPDATE` statement, to the select list, and to the scan in `pgstore.go`. `Store.Update` is the only writer of the sessions row; grep for any other `UPDATE sessions` statement and route it through the helper. A write that does not change the state still accrues, because the row stays in the counting state across it.
4. **Sweeps.** `sweepMaxAge` and `sweepExpiryWarning` compare `sessionstore.ActiveAge(row, now)` with `effectiveAgeCap` in place of `now.Sub(row.CreatedAt)`. Rewrite the doc comments of `sweepMaxAge`, `sweepExpiryWarning`, `DefaultMaxSessionAgeSeconds`, `Config.MaxSessionAgeSeconds`, the `resume_pending` and idle-clock comments, the `--max-session-age-seconds` help in `cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go`, the `Limits` and `SessionPolicy` `MaxSessionAgeSeconds` comments in `runtimestore.go`, the `RetryPolicy.MaxSessionAgeSeconds` comment in `retry_policy.go`, and the `retryPolicy` and `sessionPolicy` `maxSessionAgeSeconds` descriptions in `openapi.json`, which describe age as wall-clock or total lifetime, as the SPEC-4a active-time cap; then run `make generate` and `go generate ./pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/...` to regenerate `generated_tools.go` and `generated_schemas.go`. `sweepMaxAge` lists no `suspended` row, because SPEC-5g lets no age cap expire a `suspended` session. Its mutator recomputes `sessionstore.ActiveAge` on the locked row with the sweep's `now`. It returns a new package sentinel `errMaxAgeSuperseded`, in place of nil, when the locked row is terminal, is `suspended`, or is at or under the cap, and the sweep runs no expiry side effect on that sentinel (Design **Session-row read authority.**). Cite `// spec: §6.2 (maxSessionAge timer behavior across states)`.
5. **Evaluation on entry to `running`.** A paused session gains no age, so an over-cap row reaches `running` only when it left a counting state over the cap before a sweep saw it. `DriveResume` starts no attempt for an over-cap row (CODE-4 item 2), which covers the watchdog and tree recovery. No other write into `running` checks the cap. `resumeHeldPod` admits an over-cap `suspended` row, because `sweepMaxAge` does not expire a `suspended` row (SPEC-5g), so a refusal would refuse every later message to that session. `sweepMaxAge` expires such a row from `running` on its next tick (Edge cases **Over-cap row entering `running`.**).
   **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** how `DriveResume` obtains the effective cap. The constraint is that it returns the value `effectiveAgeCap` returns for the same row, from one shared resolver rather than a second copy of the min-wins logic.

### CODE-2 · Retry accounting

Targets: `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_generation.go` and `usage.go`; `pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/runtimestore.go`; `pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go`; `pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics.go`; `pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/runtimes.go`; `pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json`; the generated `pkg/ops/mcp/generated_tools.go`; `pkg/session/state/state.go`.

1. In `bumpRecoveryGeneration`, delete `row.RetryCount++` and the call `s.recordSessionRetry(ctx, updated)`. Rewrite its doc comment so it describes only the `recovery_generation` bump and the `PodAssignment` write, with `// spec: §4.2 (Session Manager)` for `recovery_generation`. The retry metric and audit fire only on the spend edges: `transitionToResumePending` in `failure.go`, and `sweepResuming` through `notifyRetryAttempt` in the watchdog. SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** is the rule. Rewrite the comments that credit `bumpRecoveryGeneration` with retry accounting so that each names the spend edges (`transitionToResumePending` and the watchdog's `notifyRetryAttempt`). The comments are on `RetryAttemptNotifier` and `notifyRetryAttempt` in `watchdog.go`, `OnSessionRetryAttempt` in `usage.go`, `IncSessionRetry` in `gatewaymetrics.go`, and the comment above `TestRecordSessionRetryWithoutHooksIsNoOp_F_7_3_10_F_7_3_18` in `retry_metric_internal_test.go`.
2. Delete `SessionPolicy.MaxSessionRetries` and its copy in `runtimestore.go`, and its validation in `admin/runtimes.go`.
3. Delete `sessionPolicy.maxSessionRetries` from `openapi.json`, then regenerate `generated_tools.go` with the existing generator so the drift test stays green. No file under `schemas/` names the field.
4. The admin decoder does not reject unknown fields. A runtime create or update that still carries `maxSessionRetries` is accepted and the key is not persisted. Do not add strict decoding.
5. In `ValidTransitions` (`pkg/session/state/state.go`), add `{Running, AwaitingClientAction}` to the running exits, `{InputRequired, ResumePending}` and `{InputRequired, AwaitingClientAction}` to the input_required exits, `{Suspended, AwaitingClientAction}` to the suspended exits, and `{Resuming, ResumePending}` to the resume path, so the table holds every edge of the §7.2 **Session state machine:** block, including the line SPEC-1e item 5 adds. No production code reads `IsValid` or `ValidTransitions` from this package, so no runtime behavior changes. Cite `// spec: §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §6.2 (Pod State Machine)`.

### CODE-3 · Binder post-claim callback

Targets: `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go`, `bindrequest.go`, `binder.go`, `claimconnect.go`, `bindclaim.go`, `bindprepare.go`, `bindlaunch.go`, `reclaim.go` (item 3 **Detached release.**), and `slotbinder.go` (the slot-bind request, `connectSlot`); `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/pod_launch.go`, `slot_bind.go`, `resume_rebind.go`, and `checkpointResumeRequest`.

1. Add `OnClaimed func(context.Context) error` to `podsession.ResumeRequest`, `BindRequest`, and `SlotBindRequest`. A nil hook is a no-op, which covers create and start.
2. Split `connect` (`claimconnect.go`) into `claimPod`, which runs the claim and the fallback claim and returns the claimed sandbox name, and `handshake`, which runs `resolveSandbox`, `DialAdapter`, `NegotiateVersion`, and the `ClaimAccepted` record and closes any client it opened on its own failure. Invoke the hook once per binder call, after the claim and after the slot reservation where there is one, and before `handshake`:
   - `Binder.Resume`: `claimPod`, `reserveResumeSlot` on the claimed sandbox name, hook, `handshake`, `AssignCredentials` (CODE-4 item 20), `cl.Resume`. A `reserveResumeSlot` failure precedes the hook and releases nothing, as today.
   - `Binder.Claim`, and therefore `Binder.Bind`: `claimPod`, hook, `handshake`.
   - `Binder.BindSlot`: in `connectSlot`, after `SlotClaimer.ClaimSlot` and before `resolveSandbox`. `ClaimSlot` (create) reaches the same `connectSlot` with a nil hook.
3. **Release after the claim.** A hook error, and a resolve, dial, or handshake failure after `claimPod` or `SlotClaimer.ClaimSlot`, releases the claim, with or without a hook. No compensation RPC is sent, because no entry-touching RPC has run.
   - `Claim` and `Bind`: `podclaim.DeleteClaim` on the claimed sandbox, with no credential release. Today `connect` returns a handshake failure with the claim in place, so the create-time `Claim` leaks the pod until the orphan-claim GC. This item closes that leak on create too. The create handler rolls back no failed `Claim`, because `claimAtCreate` returns no `ClaimResult`, so the release runs once.
   - `Resume`: `releaseResumeSlot(leaked=false)`. Change `releaseResumeSlot` so that an empty slot ID (an exclusive pool) deletes the claim with `podclaim.DeleteClaim` instead of returning nil. Rewrite its doc comment, and the `failResume` sentence "On an exclusive pool the slot identifier is empty and no accounting runs", to match. This also changes `failResume`: a failed adapter `Resume` on an exclusive pool now deletes the claim after the compensating Shutdown, where today the claim is left to the orphan-claim GC. On a slot pool, the release frees the reservation alone, and `SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot` deletes the claim only at occupancy zero. Do not call `failResume` for these failures. Failures from the credential assignment onward follow CODE-4 item 20.4.
   - `BindSlot`: on a hook error, `connectSlot` releases the reservation with `ReleaseSlotReservation(leaked=false)` and returns the hook error, never inside a `*SlotBindError`. `applySlotRetryPolicy` therefore neither releases it a second time nor retries it. A handshake failure after the hook keeps its `*SlotBindError`, which `applySlotRetryPolicy` releases and retries on a fresh slot.
   - `Bind` after the hook, in `Prepare` and `Launch`. Add `PrepareResult.MintedLeaseIDs []string`, which `Prepare` fills with the lease IDs its `assignCredentials` call returns on success; today `Prepare` discards them on success (`bindprepare.go:167`). `Bind` sets a new unexported `BindRequest` field `attemptScoped`, and it threads `PrepareResult.MintedLeaseIDs` into the `Launch` request. On an attempt-scoped request:
     - the `Prepare` reconnect failure calls `podclaim.DeleteClaim` in place of `ReclaimClaimed`;
     - the `Launch` reconnect failure calls `releaseAttemptCredentials` on those IDs and then `podclaim.DeleteClaim`;
     - the `Launch` reclaim closure calls `reclaimOnFailure` with `leaseAssigned` false, and first calls `releaseAttemptCredentials` on those IDs when the cause is not a slot-bind refusal.

     `ReclaimClaimed` and `failPhase` with `leaseAssigned` true call `ReleaseSession`, which revokes a lease another attempt minted for the session. The decomposed `/finalize` and `/start` path never sets `attemptScoped` and keeps its session-wide release. Where `Bind` serves a session whose only leases are the attempt's, the two releases are equal.
   - **Detached release.** Every claim delete and reservation release this item states, the exclusive-pool claim delete in `releaseResumeSlot`, and the `reclaimOnFailure` drain and `ReclaimClaimed` delete (`reclaim.go`) reached from `Bind` run on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` bounded by `slotCleanupFloor`, as `ReleaseSlotReservation` does. A context that the attempt bound (CODE-4 item 3.2) or a terminal writer's `CancelResumeAttempt` (CODE-4 item 2a) cancelled therefore still releases the claim. On the caller's context, the delete fails and the claim waits for the orphan-claim GC.
4. Return the hook error as the hook returned it. It is never wrapped in a `*SlotBindError` and never matches an exhaustion sentinel. The existing loops stop on it unchanged: `runWithQueue` re-enters only on `isExhaustion`, and `applySlotRetryPolicy` retries only a `*SlotBindError`. No sentinel is added. The driver tells a hook failure from a later failure through its own hook closure (CODE-4 item 3.6).
5. Thread the hook from `resumeOnPod` (new parameter `onClaimed`) into `checkpointResumeRequest`, and through `startOnPod` into both `bindReq` and `slotReq`, so a snapshotless rebuild on a pool with `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` reaches it through `bindConcurrentSlot`.
6. The coordination-lease acquire is not part of the hook. CODE-4 acquires the lease once, before `resumeOnPod`.
7. **Retry accounting at the claim boundary** (SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.**).
   - A failure before the hook writes nothing and spends no retry (CODE-4 item 3.5).
   - A hook error reports no failure; CODE-4 item 3.6 states its outcomes.
   - After a hook that wrote `resuming`, every failure is reported once as CODE-4 item 3.7 states; SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** states which reports spend a retry.
   - On a slot pool, the `applySlotRetryPolicy` retries of one attempt run inside that attempt and spend no `retryCount`. The per-attempt flag of CODE-4 item 3.4 makes the second hook call a no-op. Only the attempt's final error reaches the driver.

1. **Watchdog hook.** Add `ResumeDriver` beside `RetryAttemptNotifier`, with one method `DriveResume(ctx context.Context, row sessionstore.Session)`, satisfied by the wired `TerminalHook`. Invariants:
   - `sweepResumePending` calls `DriveResume` for each listed row inside its window. The active-age check is in `DriveResume` (item 2), so the watchdog and `ReattachNode` (item 11) apply one check. `sweepMaxAge`'s expiry of an over-cap `resume_pending` row is pre-existing behavior that BUILD-GAPS F-6.2.28 tracks under P3.

2. **`DriveResume`.** Invariants:

The received row comes from a read-pool listing and can show an earlier `resume_pending` incarnation, so the check never uses it. Item 3 step 1 returns with no write on an over-cap row. The entry write (item 3 step 4) is guarded on the step-1 generation, and a `resume_pending` row accrues no age within one incarnation, so the checked value equals the locked row's.
   - Otherwise it starts one `attemptResume` asynchronously, on a context derived from the server-lifetime context, which `Server` cancels on shutdown. The session's entry in the attempt record clears when the attempt exits.
   - With no pod binder wired, the attempt runs `completeResume` (item 5) in place of `attemptResume`:
     - step 2 is guarded on `State == resume_pending` and sets no `PodAssignment`;
     - item 5.3 calls `classifyCommit` with the binderless baseline, whose `recoveryGen` is the `RecoveryGeneration` of the row `DriveResume` received;
     - the item 5 **Post-commit tail.** table states which rows apply.

Bound: they are per-replica, at most one attempt per session runs on a replica at a time, and the attempt record holds the attempt's cancel function (item 2a). The attempt record is distinct from the terminal claim record (D7). Cite `// spec: §7.3 (Resume driver)`. Pinned by intents C4.7, C4.8, and C4.22.
2a. **Same-replica cancel of a resume attempt** (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**, §7.2 snapshot-close step 1). Invariant: once a terminal write for a session returns on a replica, every pre-commit restoration RPC of that replica's attempt for the session runs on a cancelled context.

The item 3.2 and 3.4 attempt bounds cancel the same context. The attempt removes its own entry when it exits.

After the hook, the binder's RPC returns a context error, and the binder releases the claim on a detached context (CODE-3 item 3 **Detached release.**, and `compensateFailedSlotBind` for a slot compensation). Item 3.7 then reports at `G`, and its mutator finds the incarnation ended and writes nothing. `completeResume` runs on the server-lifetime context (item 5), so a cancel there leaves the outcome to the commit guard and the tail's terminal re-check row (item 5). 3. **`attemptResume`**, in this order:

Call `releaseThenResumeOnPod(ctx, row, onClaimed)`, which gains `onClaimed` and passes it to `resumeOnPod` after `releaseEarlierBinding`. `releaseEarlierBinding` waits for `slotAccountLocks` on `ctx` and then calls `releaseExecutor` on the server-lifetime context, as D6's release does. `CancelResumeAttempt` and the attempt bound therefore cannot cut the release short after the executor's unbind. The hook:
      - is one store `Update`. When the locked row is `resume_pending` at the step-1 generation, it writes `resuming` and increments `CoordinationGeneration` by one (SPEC-1b). Otherwise it returns `errResumeSuperseded`.
      - The attempt keeps the written generation as `G`.
      - A second invocation in the same attempt, such as a slot retry (CODE-3 item 7), is a no-op that returns nil.

      - On a store error, the write may have applied. Record the next-attempt time as step 5 does. `sweepResuming` ends any such incarnation after `MaxResumingSeconds` and spends one retry.
      - Neither case is counted (item 25).

Otherwise they take the existing `errReportConflict` no-op, and a zero field never matches.

5. **`completeResume`** runs on the server-lifetime context, so the attempt bound does not reach it. A context end in the step 1 lock wait or during the step 2 repeat, before the commit's outcome is known, takes the **Context ended** column of the **Post-commit tail.** table below. It returns the `resumeExit` that the item 25 table assigns to its exit. In this order:
   1. Take the delivery lock (D3). A wait that the context ends stops with nothing written.
   2. Commit with one `Update` guarded on `State == resuming && CoordinationGeneration == G`. It writes `running` through `transitionResume`, increments `RecoveryGeneration` on both branches, and sets `PodAssignment` to the result's sandbox. On a miss the mutator returns the package sentinel `errResumeCommitMissed` and changes nothing. The write follows the Design **Guarded-write repeat rule.**, with `seen` as its copied row and `errResumeCommitMissed` as its sentinel. A row hard-deleted by `DeleteByUser` or `DeleteByTenant` returns `sessionstore.ErrNotFound`, which ends the repeat.
   3. Exit. When the step 2 loop ends other than by a context end, call the pure function `classifyCommit(o commitObservation, b commitBaseline) commitOutcome` in `resume_driver.go`.
      - `o` records whether the last `Update` returned nil, whether any try returned an error other than `errResumeCommitMissed` and `sessionstore.ErrNotFound` (a store error), whether any try returned `sessionstore.ErrNotFound`, and `R`. `R` is the row the `Update` returned on nil, or otherwise the last `seen`.
      - `b` is `{gen: G, sandbox: <the result's SandboxName>, recoveryGen: <the RecoveryGeneration of the item 3.1 row>}` with a pod binder, or the binderless baseline of item 2.
      - The outcome selects the column of the **Post-commit tail.** table. `commitLanded` selects **Landed**, `commitAboveG` selects **Above `G`**, and `commitNotLanded` selects **Not landed**. **Relinquished** comes only from the fence row (step 4).

      | Path | Observation | Outcome |
      |:--|:--|:--|
      | Either | The last `Update` returned nil, after a store error or not | `commitLanded` |
      | Either | A try returned `sessionstore.ErrNotFound` | `commitNotLanded` |
      | Either | Guard miss with no earlier store error. A concurrent pool's `PodAssignment` names the shared pod, so only the error history separates this attempt's write from a later incarnation's. | `commitNotLanded` |
      | Pod binder | Miss after a store error. `R.PodAssignment` is the result's sandbox, `R.RecoveryGeneration` is above `b.recoveryGen`, `podHeld(R)` (CODE-5 item 1) holds or `R` is terminal, and `R.CoordinationGeneration == G`. Only this attempt's commit writes this sandbox at `G` and advances `RecoveryGeneration`. A terminal `R` was ended after the commit, and the Landed re-check row hands the pod to D6, which defers to the writer's open claim. | `commitLanded` |
      | Pod binder | As above, with `R.CoordinationGeneration > G`. An adopter's `RecordHandoff` advanced the landed row, and the adopter owns the pod whether or not it published (Edge cases **Terminal row with no binding at its generation.**). | `commitAboveG` |
      | Pod binder | Miss after a store error, with any other `R`. The commit may have landed and the pod may have passed to another owner. | `commitNotLanded` |
      | Binderless | Miss after a store error, with `R.RecoveryGeneration` above the baseline, whatever `R`'s state, `PodAssignment`, or `CoordinationGeneration`. A Sweeper `RecordHandoff` advances `CoordinationGeneration` with no binder wired, so the binderless path never reads it. | `commitLanded` |
      | Binderless | Miss after a store error, with `R.RecoveryGeneration` at or below the baseline | `commitNotLanded` |

      Invariant, which follows from the table:
      - With a pod binder, the attempt releases exactly its pod and the leases it minted, publishes nothing, and writes nothing, unless `R` shows its commit landed at `G` (this commit owns the pod) or above `G` (an adopter owns it).
      - A commit that applied, returned an error, and whose row a later non-terminal write moved off `podHeld` or onto another sandbox before the repeat read it takes `commitNotLanded`. `ReleaseUncommitted` then releases the claim, the slot, and the minted leases only on a `RECLAIMED` reply. That reply shows that the adapter entry this attempt created is still in place, so no other releaser has acted on the pod. The write that moved `R` emitted its own `status_change`.
      - On the binderless path, `commitLanded` means a commit landed since the row `DriveResume` received. With every binderless replica acting under D1, that commit can be another replica's, and its tail rows then run twice. This is accepted, because the binderless wiring is the single-gateway Compose deployment.

        - the connection close;
        - the reservation release through `releaseResumeSlot`, with `leaked` taken from that reply, or on an exclusive pool the claim delete (CODE-3 item 3);
        - `releaseAttemptCredentials(result.MintedLeaseIDs)`.

        `completeResume` passes `requireReclaimed` true when any commit try returned a store error. With it set, a reply other than `RECLAIMED`, or a reclaim error, releases no claim, slot, or lease, and is logged at WARN with the session ID and the outcome. `BindResult` gains `BindAttempt`, `Pool`, and `MaxConcurrentSessions`. `Binder.Resume`, `Bind` (from the token `Prepare` mints), and `materializeSlot` set them. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the factoring. Bound: `failResume`'s behavior is unchanged, and no teardown sends a second `Shutdown`.
      - `BindResult.MintedLeaseIDs` is filled by `Bind` (through `PrepareResult.MintedLeaseIDs`, CODE-3 item 3), by `materializeSlot`, and by `Binder.Resume` (item 20).
      - Log and swallow its error. It never releases leases by session: do not use `rollbackBinding`, `Release`, `ReleaseSlot`, or `ReclaimClaimed` here.
   4. On **Landed**, run the table's fence row. A relinquish moves the exit to **Relinquished**. `fenceResumedPod` gains a `generation int64` parameter, which both branches pass as `G`. It calls the new `(*coordfence.Fencer).FenceAt(ctx, adapter, tenantID, sessionID, generation)`. `FenceAt` runs `fence`'s loop at `generation` and, on a stale rejection, relinquishes without retrying at a re-read value. The binding's stamp is therefore the generation the pod accepted, and a takeover that raced the commit keeps the pod (**Relinquished**). `Fence`, which the readopt calls, is unchanged. A best-effort fence failure is logged, and the exit stays **Landed**. Remove the stamps that `resumeOnPod` applies today in `resume_rebind.go`, on both branches. `publishBinding` on the rebuild branch makes no second lease acquire.

Delete `clearInboxOnResume` (`messaging.go`), so that no resume clears an inbox, and keep `Coordinator.ClearInboxOnAcquire`. RECORDS-1 item 7(h) files the §7.2 `inbox_cleared` emission.    **Post-commit tail.** This table is the only statement of which effect each exit runs. A step or an exit bullet names a column and never an effect. Rows run top to bottom.

   | Effect | Landed | Relinquished | Above `G` | Not landed | Context ended |

The adopter fenced at its `RecordHandoff` generation (CODE-5 item 2). | Skipped. The pod is released. | Skipped. The adopter of a landed row fences (CODE-5 item 2). Otherwise `sweepResuming` and the claim-orphan GC reclaim the pod (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**). |

A read error is logged and the column continues, and D6's Sweeper trigger covers the binding. | Runs. On a terminal row, skip every remaining row except the connection close. This replica published nothing, so it calls no `ReleaseTerminalBinding`. A read error is logged and the column continues. | As **Relinquished**. | Skipped. Nothing is published. | Skipped, as in the fence row. |

   | `ReleaseUncommitted` | Skipped. The binding's release (item 24) applies. | Skipped. The adopter's binding owns the pod. | Skipped. The adopter's binding owns the pod. | Runs. | Skipped, as in the fence row. |

   The resumer itself runs the bus, audit, store, and tree-recovery rows that the table assigns to the landed columns, because only the resumer knows that its commit landed. None of those rows sends an RPC to the pod, so a relinquished or superseded resumer may run them: §10.1.5 forbids RPCs and cached state, and every transition writer emits its own `status_change`, as item 9 does. Item 25's table states the `resumeExit` of each exit. On the binderless path (item 2), the first five rows do not apply, and **Relinquished** and **Above `G`** do not arise. 6. **`deliverBuffered(ctx, row) error`** implements SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** It is called on the coordinating replica with the delivery lock held. Invariants:
   - **Order.** It delivers one entry at a time, the earliest `EnqueuedAt` across the inbox head and the DLQ head, and the inbox head on a tie. DLQ score order is acceptance order because every DLQ enqueue scores `EnqueuedAt + DefaultDLQTTL`: `messages_inbox.go` and `mcptools_register.go` pass a zero TTL, and `MigrateInboxToDLQ` keeps the original `EnqueuedAt` (SPEC-5z item 7). A later per-row TTL must preserve this order, or else `PeekOldest` and `DLQ.CountAcceptedAfter` (CODE-6 item 2) must order by `EnqueuedAt`.
   - **Removal.** For each entry: `executor.Send`, then `recordDeliveredResponse`, then removal. A removal that finds nothing is not an error. These three run on the server-lifetime context whatever the caller's `ctx`. The loop reads `ctx` only before taking the next entry, and it returns `ctx.Err()` with the untaken entries in place.
   - **Terminal stop.** Before it takes each entry, the loop reads the row with `Get` (the read pool, D4). When that row is terminal, the loop returns nil and leaves the entry in place. A read error is logged at WARN and the loop continues. A terminal state is absorbing, so a read-pool row that is terminal is final; a lagging row can only delay the stop (Edge cases **Held entry in flight at a terminal write.**).
   - **Failure.** A `Send`, audit-record, or removal error stops the loop and returns the error, leaving that entry at the head. A `Peek` or `PeekOldest` error is logged at WARN, stops the loop, and returns nil. An entry whose `Payload` does not decode as `MessagePayload` is logged at ERROR with the session ID and message ID and removed, and the loop continues. That is the only removal of an undelivered entry. The loop expires nothing, calls no `SweepExpired`, and writes nothing to the inbox or the DLQ.
   - **Policy.** Every held message runs the PostAgentOutput chain and its §16.7 audit row. A REJECT records the audit row, transcribes and publishes nothing for the entry, removes it, and continues.
   - **Encoding.** The inbox and the DLQ hold one encoding, `sessionserver.MessagePayload`. The MCP buffering block in `mcptools_register.go` marshals `MessagePayload{ID: messageID, Role: "user", Content: <the buffered content>}` in place of the bare `in.Message`. The projection to `executor.Message` is the one `parseMessageBatch` applies, extracted as a helper both call. `From` is set from the entry's `SenderSessionID` through `senderFrom`, which moves from `mcptools.go` to the executor package (§15.4).
   - **No coordinator.** With no messaging coordinator wired, it returns nil at once.
   Interfaces:
   - New `Inbox.Peek`, `Inbox.Ack`, `DLQ.PeekOldest(ctx, tenantID, sessionID)` (returning the decoded message and the raw member), and `DLQ.Remove(ctx, tenantID, sessionID, member)`. Each is exposed through a `Coordinator` method of the same name that returns an empty result and nil on a nil `*Coordinator`.
   - `runPostAgentOutput` and `recordDeliveredResponse` take a context and no writer, and return the parts, the `interceptor.Result` on REJECT, and the audit-record error.
   - `recordRouteRejection` (`route.go`) splits into an audit half that returns the error and an envelope half that writes the 500, 503, 400, or 403. The PreRoute and PostRoute phases and the REST callers call both.
   **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the Redis commands behind the four accessors. Bound: `Ack` and `Remove` remove only the named entry, and `PeekOldest` returns the lowest-scored entry.
   Callers: `completeResume` (item 5 **Post-commit tail.**), the takeover delivery (item 26), `resumeHeldAndDrain` (item 8), `handleStart` (item 21), `SessionDelivery.DeliverHeld` (CODE-6 item 7), and both message paths before every path-2 `executor.Send` (CODE-6 item 7). No call site is added for the path-3 and path-5 `ready_for_input` report, because the tree has no handler for it. Code that later handles the report for a `running` session calls `deliverBuffered` before any other delivery.
   Cite `// spec: §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6)`. Pinned by intents C4.28, C4.29, C4.30, and C4.32.
7. **`holdMessage(ctx, tenantID, sessionID string, msgs []sessioninbox.Message, ttl time.Duration)`** on `Server` is the one DLQ enqueue site of both message paths: `bufferIncomingMessages` for `bufferTargetDLQ`, and the MCP path through `SessionDelivery.HoldMessage`. It implements SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** It calls `EnqueueDLQ` for each message, then reads the row with `ReadPrimary`.
   - **Re-read error.** Remove each member it enqueued with `DLQ.Remove`, and return the error. The caller answers `error` with `inbox_unavailable`.
   - **Terminal row.** Remove each member it enqueued. Return `terminal` when any removal found its member, which the caller answers with `TARGET_TERMINAL` as `writeTargetTerminal` does, and `queued` otherwise. A removal that returns an error counts as found. `holdMessage` then calls `s.drainMessagingOnTerminal(ctx, row)` once with the re-read row, so a member the failed removal left is removed and reported `message_expired`.
   - **Any other row.** Return `queued`, or `dropped` on eviction.
   - **No messaging coordinator.** `EnqueueDLQ` returns `ErrMessagingUnavailable`, which both paths answer as `error` with `inbox_unavailable`.
   Cite `// spec: §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6)`. Pinned by intent C4.31.
8. **Store-only `POST /resume`** (SPEC-3). `EndpointResume` admits `StateAwaitingClientAction` and `StateSuspended`. Add `holdsPod(row) bool` on `Server`, true when `row.PodAssignment != ""` or no pod binder is wired. `handleResume` reads the row with `ReadPrimary` and branches:
   - From `awaiting_client_action`, or from `suspended` with `!holdsPod(row)`: call `enterResumePending` and answer `200` with the updated row.
   - From `suspended` with `holdsPod(row)` when D1 admits this replica: take the delivery lock, call `resumeHeldAndDrain`, and answer `200` with the row in `running`. On `errHeldDeliveryFailed`, answer `200` with `running`, and answer any other error through `writePreconditionError`. On `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended`, call `enterResumePending` with the read row's `State` set to `awaiting_client_action`; a guard miss of its `enterResumePending` is the precondition error.
   - From `suspended` with `holdsPod(row)` when D1 refuses this replica: write nothing and answer `TARGET_NOT_READY`, HTTP 409, with `currentState` in the details. This is the §29.3 held-pod `/resume` row, because no inter-replica carrier for a forward exists.

For each level it calls the start callback for every node that passes its tree, level, and resume-window checks, and only then calls `recoverFn` for those nodes. `recoverFn` gains a `context.Context` parameter, and `Recover` passes each call a context whose deadline is that node's `nodeDeadline`, the earliest of the level deadline, the tree deadline, and the end of the node's resume window. Today `Recover` computes `nodeDeadline` and discards it. The orchestrator in `pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treerecovery/treerecovery.go` derives each context from its own. The start callback calls `DriveResume` for the node, which applies the in-flight check, D1, and the age check (item 2). `ReattachNode` then reads the row with `ReadPrimary` once per poll interval until the row is in neither `resume_pending` nor `resuming`, or its context ends. It returns an error when the row is `awaiting_client_action` or terminal, and nil for any other state, because the restore committed. A read error is logged at WARN and the next poll retries. A restored node can leave `running` before the next poll, to `input_required` through `lenny/request_input` or to `suspended` through an interrupt, and neither state is a failed restore. A wait that the node's deadline ends returns an error whose text is the reason `Recover` records for that budget, so the orchestrator's level and tree timeout counters still count it. `recovery.Recover` records each error as a failed node, as it does for a failed restore today. Bound: no node of a level is waited on before every selected node of that level has been driven, and no wait outlives its node's deadline.

A node listed `resume_pending` that has since moved gets no write from `DriveResume`'s D1 check or from `attemptResume` step 1, and the wait reads its current state. A node coordinated by a peer replica waits for that replica's driver. A node that entered `resume_pending` after the listing is driven by its own coordinating replica's `sweepResumePending`.

The traversal drives no node this replica does not coordinate. It fails a node only when the node's restore ends in `awaiting_client_action` or a terminal state, or when a §8.10 budget or the node's resume window elapses inside the traversal. The deadline on each wait enforces those budgets.

22a. **Crash mark on the binding** (§5.2 **Recycle lifecycle**, §28.5.1 **Degradation.**). Invariant: a pod that serves one session and whose `CH-ATTACH` stream ended in a reported stream failure is retired by every release of that binding whose unbind follows `endConn`'s handling of the stream's end, whatever the session row's state and whatever disposition the release passes. A release whose unbind precedes that handling ends the stream itself, which §28.5.1 counts as no failure, so it keeps its disposition. SPEC-5b step 2 states the outcome for the suspension release.
   - `BindResult` (`bindrequest.go`) gains an unexported `crashed atomic.Bool`, `(*BindResult).MarkCrashed()`, and `(*BindResult).Crashed() bool`. No site clears the mark. A later binding of the session is a new `BindResult`. Every use of `BindResult` is through a pointer, so the field adds no copy.
   - `attachConn` (`podstream.go`) gains `bind *podsession.BindResult`, set in `newAttachConn` to the binding `streamFor` read under `e.mu`. In `endConn`, after the `closedByGateway` check and before `e.mu.Unlock()`, call `c.bind.MarkCrashed()` when `isReportedStreamFailure(err)` is true, whether or not a handler is wired. `isReportedStreamFailure` is a pure status-code check, and every release unbinds under `e.mu` (`PodExecutor.unbind`) before `Binder.Release` reads the mark, so a release whose unbind follows the conn's removal from `e.streams` reads the mark. A release whose unbind precedes it, because the reader has not yet returned from `Recv` into `endConn` or is blocked in the approval gate (`maybeGateToolCall`), evicts the conn and sets `closedByGateway`, so `endConn` sets no mark. A gateway-caused end (`closedByGateway`) and an unreported status code set nothing. The mark therefore reaches the binding through every early return of `ReportAttachStreamFailure`: a registry that no longer names the sandbox because a release already took the binding, a store read error, a peer takeover, a terminal row, and a compare-and-swap lost to a terminal or suspend write.
   - `Binder.Release` (`release.go`) takes the recycle branch only when `result.Recycle && disposition != dispositionFailed && !result.Crashed()`. Every other case takes the retire branch, which sends a plain `Shutdown` and drains. Rewrite the `Release`, `dispositionFailed`, and `shutdownAdapter` doc comments to name the mark as the second retire condition. No caller and no signature changes. The post-terminal hook, D6, the suspension release, `PodExecutor.Close`, and every other `releaseExecutor` caller get the retire disposition through this one read.
   - `ReleaseSlot` does not read the mark. Under §5.2 **Failure isolation:**, a crashed slot releases only its slot, and the occupancy-zero scrub decides the pod. The uncounted slot of a report that commits no failure edge is RECORDS-1 item 7(w).
   Cite `// spec: §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes), §28.5.1 (Gateway-to-pod)`. Pinned by intents C4.66, C4.67, and T1.37.
