   - `resume_driver.go` declares `type resumeExit int` with the values `resumeExitUncounted` (the zero value), `resumeExitReported`, `resumeExitCommitLanded`, `resumeExitCommitNotLanded`, and `resumeExitAbandoned`, and the method `(s *Server) recordResumeAttempt(pool string, exit resumeExit)`. The method calls `s.incSessionResumeAttempt(pool, "success")` for `resumeExitCommitLanded` and `s.incSessionResumeAttempt(pool, "failure")` for `resumeExitReported` and `resumeExitCommitNotLanded`. It does nothing for any other value, and nothing when `incSessionResumeAttempt` is nil.
   - Immediately after the item 3.1 read, `attemptResume` defers one call of `recordResumeAttempt` with the read row's `PoolRef` and the attempt's `exit` as it stands when the attempt returns. That deferred call is the only caller of `incSessionResumeAttempt`. `handleResume`, `resumeHeldAndDrain`, `ResumeSuspended` (CODE-6 item 7), `sweepResuming`, `ReportSessionFailure`, the CODE-5 adoption, and the binderless `completeResume` call of item 2 record nothing.
   - The exits:

     | Driver exit | Site | `exit` | Counted |
     |:--|:--|:--|:--|
     | Read error or a row that is not `resume_pending` | 3.1 | none recorded | No |
     | Lease acquire error | 3.3 | `resumeExitUncounted` | No |
     | Pre-claim failure, including a claim that the attempt bound, a `CancelResumeAttempt` call (item 2a), or the server-lifetime context cancels | 3.5 | `resumeExitUncounted` | No |
     | Hook returned `errResumeSuperseded` | 3.6 | `resumeExitUncounted` | No |
     | Hook store error, whether or not the write applied | 3.6 | `resumeExitUncounted` | No |
     | A step after the hook failed while the server-lifetime context is live, including expiry of the attempt bound, a cancel through `CancelResumeAttempt` (item 2a), and a report whose mutator finds the incarnation ended | 3.7 | `resumeExitReported` | `failure` |
     | A step after the hook failed after the server-lifetime context ended, so no report is sent | 3.7 | `resumeExitAbandoned` | No |
     | Server-lifetime context ended in the item 5.1 lock wait or the item 5.2 repeat before the commit's outcome is known | 5.1, 5.2 | `resumeExitAbandoned` | No |
     | Commit `Update` returned without error, including a later relinquish | 5.2, 5.4 | `resumeExitCommitLanded` | `success` |
     | Commit landed at `G` after a store error, including a later relinquish | 5.3, 5.4 | `resumeExitCommitLanded` | `success` |
     | Commit landed above `G` | 5.3 | `resumeExitCommitLanded` | `success` |
     | Commit not landed, including after the `resuming` watchdog, a mid-resume terminal transition, or a later incarnation ended this one | 5.3 | `resumeExitCommitNotLanded` | `failure` |
     | The `resuming` watchdog ends an incarnation | item 15 | none recorded by the watchdog | No. An attempt that is still running records its own row above when its next write finds the incarnation ended. |
     | Binderless commit | item 2 | none recorded | No. The binderless path writes no `resuming` incarnation. |
     | `POST /resume`, held-pod resume, or adoption | item 8, CODE-5, and CODE-6 item 7 | none recorded | No |

### CODE-5 · Sweeper adoption of the SPEC-2a states

Targets: `pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go`, in the 0091 version of `Sweep`; the `BindingRegistry` satisfiers `cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go`, `tests/testinfra/coordfixture/coordfixture.go`, and the fakes in `coordination_scoping_test.go` and `tests/tier2_component/coordination/sweep_test.go`. Locate edits by function and quoted text.

1. **Predicate.** Replace `isRunningPod` with two predicates.
   - `adoptedState(row)`: the row's state is one that SPEC-2a lists as adopted.
   - `podHeld(row)`: the state is `running`, `input_required`, or `suspended`, and `PodAssignment != ""`.
   Set `adoptable := leaseUnheld && adoptedState(row) && !inAdoptionBackoff`. `adoptedState` admits `running`, `input_required`, and `suspended` only when `PodAssignment != ""`, which is the never-bound exclusion of implemented proposal 0060. Add `podlessSuspended(row)`: the state is `suspended` and `PodAssignment == ""`. Directly after the `leases.Get` call and before the dead-connection branch, the Sweeper skips a `podlessSuspended` row with no `Acquire` and no `Release`, whatever `bound` and `priorHolder` say, so no replica renews or adopts its lease and the lease lapses on its TTL (SPEC-2a). When `priorHolder == s.replicaID` on that row, it first calls `releaseMirror`, so this replica's §10.1.8 barrier-target set stops naming a session with no pod. The call repeats on each sweep until the lease lapses, which also undoes a mirror upsert by a sweep that listed the row before the suspension release cleared its pod (`coordlease.Store.Upsert` clears `released_at`). It writes no lease `Release`, because the lapse ends the lease. A binding still present on the row is left to the suspension release, whose step 4 removes it. Cite `// spec: §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination), §10.1.8 (Rolling Updates and the CheckpointBarrier Protocol)`. A `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` row is never re-adopted onto a pod, whatever its `PodAssignment` says, because that pod failed.
2. **Takeover branch.** After a non-zero `RecordHandoff`:
   - when the locked-row `podHeld` value that `RecordHandoff` returns (item 2b) is true, run `readoptAndFence` and `publish` as today; before the dial, `readoptAndFence` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go`) resolves the session's pool with `podsession.ResolvePool` pinned to `row.PoolRef` (never the client pin `row.Pool`) through `sessionserver.NewPoolPolicyReader`, and the binding it publishes carries `SlotID` equal to the session ID when the match's `MaxConcurrentSessions > 1` and `Recycle`, `CleanupCommands`, and `CleanupTimeoutSeconds` as `checkpointResumeRequest` copies them, because every release dispatches on the binding alone; a resolve error takes the `releaseAfterReadoptFailure` path (`// spec: §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol)`);
   - otherwise skip both, and still call `clearAdoptionBackoff`, `upsertMirror` with the `RecordHandoff` generation, and `held++`.
   On both branches, call `upsertMirror` with the `RecordHandoff` result, never `row.CoordinationGeneration`, so the mirror row a takeover writes carries the post-handoff generation. A zero `RecordHandoff` keeps the existing release-and-continue path.
2a. **Peer takeover from the binding stamp.** Delete `renewedGeneration`, `recordRenewedGeneration`, `forgetRenewedGeneration`, every call to them, including in `evictBinding`, and the map's initialization in `NewSweeper`. Add `BindingGeneration(sessionID string) int64` to `BindingRegistry`. It returns the bound `BindResult.CoordinationGeneration`, or 0 when the session is unbound. `peerTookOver(row, bindGen)` returns `bindGen > 0 && row.CoordinationGeneration > bindGen`. The stamp is the generation the replica published the binding at (CODE-4 item 5.4, the readopt publish), so a resume that rebinds on this replica between two sweeps is compared against its own `G`. A per-replica record that only an unbound sweep clears would instead be compared against the earlier binding's value and would evict the live binding on a stale foreign lease. Update the `BindingRegistry` doc comment, the `Sweeper.mu` comment, the `evictBinding` doc comment (which states that it forgets the generation the session was last renewed at), and the `peerTookOver` doc comment. Cite `// spec: §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination), §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior)`.
2b. **`RecordHandoff` is the §10.1.2 step 1 compare-and-swap.** `RecordHandoff(ctx, tenantID, sessionID string, expected int64) (int64, bool)`. Rename `errHandoffSessionTerminal` to `errHandoffRefused`, and have the mutator also return it unless `row.CoordinationGeneration == expected` and `adoptedState(*row)` holds on the locked row. `RecordHandoff` returns the new generation and `podHeld(*row)` of the locked row. A row that the failure funnel moved to `resume_pending` at the listed generation is therefore not re-adopted onto its failed pod (Design **Session-row read authority.**). The Sweeper passes the `List` row's generation. A miss returns 0 and false, and the existing path releases the lease and continues, which is step 1's "discard its lease claim, and restart". A Sweeper that listed `resume_pending` before the driver's entry write therefore leaves the `resuming` incarnation alone, and the next sweep reads `resuming`, which `adoptedState` excludes. Update the callers in `tests/tier2_component/coordination/sweep_test.go` and `resume_fence_takeover_test.go` to pass the expected value. Cite `// spec: §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol)`.
2c. **Takeover delivery hook.** `Options` gains `HeldDeliverer`, an interface declared in `coordination.go` with one method, `DeliverHeldAfterTakeover(tenantID, sessionID string, generation int64)`. After `publish()` on the takeover branch (item 2), the Sweeper calls it once with the `RecordHandoff` generation. It makes no call on a readopt failure, on a zero `RecordHandoff`, on the branch where `podHeld` is false, or on a renew. A nil hook is skipped. CODE-4 item 26 implements and wires the hook, so this step lands with the hook unset. Cite `// spec: §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6)`. Pinned by intent C5.8.
2d. **Terminal release hook** (D6). `Options` gains `TerminalReleaser`, an interface declared in `coordination.go` with one method, `ReleaseTerminalBinding(row sessionstore.Session)`. In `Sweep`'s `if session.IsTerminal(row.State)` branch, after `releaseMirror`, the Sweeper calls it once when `boundHere(row.ID)` is true. It calls it for no unbound or non-terminal row, and a nil hook is skipped. The branch makes no lease write and no `EvictBinding` call. The generation check and the release belong to the hook, under one lock. CODE-4 item 27 implements and wires the hook, so this step lands with it unset. Rewrite the terminal-branch comment and the `Sweep` doc comment to state the handoff. Cite `// spec: §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model)`. Pinned by intents C5.9 and C5.10.
3. **Comments.** Rewrite the doc comment of the replaced predicate, the `Sweep` doc comment, and the comment above `eligible`. Each names the adopted states, states that a row with a held pod is re-fenced, and states that a podless `suspended` row is neither renewed nor adopted and has its mirror row released while this replica is its prior holder. Cite `// spec: §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol)`.

1. **Suspension reason and the locked-row endpoint checks.**
   - Add the exported constant `SuspendedReasonIdle = "Idle"`, and export the two interrupt values that `suspendReason` returns (`interrupt.go`). `sweepSuspended` and `SuspendIdle` compare against these constants.
   - `handleInterrupt` is the one write of an interrupt into `suspended`. `lenny/interrupt_session` registers in `client_tools.go` through `postByID(..., "/interrupt")`, as `lenny/resume_session` does. Delete its handler in `mcptools_register.go` and the comments in `registerSessionLifecycleTools` and `Register` that name it.
   - `handleInterrupt`, `handleTransition` (`POST /terminate`), and `handleDelete` (`DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}`) run `session.Validate` for their endpoint on the locked row inside the mutator, and take `fromState` from that row (D4). On a validation error they answer through `writePreconditionError`, with no status event, no re-arm, and no post-terminal hook, and a terminal claim taken for the write ends at once (D7). A restore that moved the row to `resuming` after the entry read therefore gets no terminal write (SPEC-2d item 2), and a delete of such a row bumps `coordination_generation` in its terminal write (D7 **Mark.**).
   - The tool result becomes the REST session body:
     - `MCPClient.InterruptSession` (`sdks/client/go/lenny/mcp.go`) decodes it into `Session`, and the `MCPSessionState` doc comment stops naming the tool;
     - `cmdSessionInterrupt` (`pkg/embedded/localcli/session.go`) prints `ID` and `State`;
     - the fakes in `mcp_test.go` and `session_verbs_test.go` return that body.
   Pinned by intents C6.41, C6.40, C6.37, and T1.5.
2. **`sweepSuspended`**, new and called from `Tick`, lists `suspended` rows and, for each row in order:
   1. **Lifetime** (SPEC-5g). When `now - SuspendedAt >= MaxSuspendedSessionSeconds`, a guarded `Update` (`State == suspended` and `SuspendedAt` unchanged) writes `expired` with `FailureReason = expired:idle` (`session.FailureExpiredIdle`). When the `Update` returns nil, the sweep calls `notifySessionExpiry` with `ExpiryReasonMaxIdleTime`, and `recordCompleted`, and does nothing more for the row (sub-items 2 and 3 are skipped). The write is a terminal writer on the `funnel` path (D7), and CODE-6 adds its row to the D7 **Terminal writer inventory.** table. On a guard miss the mutator returns `errSuspendedSuperseded` and changes nothing. On that sentinel, on `ErrNotFound`, or on a store error, which it logs with the session ID, the sweep does nothing more for the row in that tick.
   2. **Podless row.** When `BacklogResumer` is wired and its `HoldsPod(row) bool` (the server's `holdsPod`) returns false, do nothing more for the row. Item 16 owns its backlog.
   3. **Held pod.** Call `BacklogResumer.ResumeBacklog(ctx, row) bool` when it is wired. When it returns true, skip the release this tick. Otherwise, when `PodAssignment != ""` and `now - SuspendedAt >= MaxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds`, call `SuspendedPodReleaser.ReleaseSuspendedPod(ctx, row)` when it is wired. An `Idle` row names no pod (item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.**), so sub-item 2 takes it.
   **`ResumeBacklog`** (session server, SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**) returns false under D2 or when D1 refuses this replica. Otherwise it calls `HeldLen`.
   - On a `HeldLen` error it logs and returns true, so the row gets no release this tick.
   - On a zero count it returns false.
   - On a count above zero it returns true and starts `ResumeSuspended(ctx, row)` (item 7) asynchronously, on a context derived from the server-lifetime context, with at most one in flight per session on this replica.
   Invariant: `Tick` never waits on a delivery, because `Watchdog.Run` calls `Tick` serially.
   **`HeldLen(ctx, tenantID, sessionID string, withInbox bool, acceptedAfter time.Time)`** is new on `Coordinator`. With a zero `acceptedAfter` it counts every DLQ entry. Otherwise it counts the entries accepted after `acceptedAfter`, through the new `DLQ.CountAcceptedAfter(ctx, tenantID, sessionID, since)`, which relies on CODE-4 item 6's score order. When `withInbox` is true it adds the inbox `Len`. Every caller passes `withInbox = true` and a zero `acceptedAfter` for `SuspendedReasonIdle`, and `withInbox = false` with `acceptedAfter = SuspendedAt` otherwise.
   Cite `// spec: §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6)`. Pinned by intents C6.1, C6.2, C6.3, C6.42, C6.43, C6.44, C6.34, and C6.21.
3. **Hold source.** Correct the `Config.MaxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` comment that claims a per-tenant cap. Only the deploy-wide value exists, and RECORDS-1 files the tenant cap.
4. **`ReleaseSuspendedPod`** in `suspend_release.go` (SPEC-5b steps 1 and 2) releases the pod of an interrupt suspension. It returns at once under D2, when D1 refuses this replica, when a release or an idle suspension of the session is in flight on this replica, or when this replica holds an open terminal claim for the session (D7 **Claim.**). Otherwise it runs steps 1 to 5 asynchronously, as `ResumeBacklog` starts `ResumeSuspended`. It takes no delivery lock and makes no adapter seal call. Invariant: a session row never names a released pod. A stop between steps 3 and 4 leaves a podless row and, on an exclusive pool, an orphan claim for the orphan-claim GC. On a concurrent-workspace pool the slot stays counted until its pod terminates, because the GC skips a pod with live siblings. This fails closed. Owner: P1 (BUILD-GAPS F-10.1.21).
   1. Skip the session when its last checkpoint failure is under 60 s old. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the per-replica record.
   2. Checkpoint through the consumer interface `SuspensionCheckpointer` (`CheckpointWithTrigger(ctx, tenantID, sessionID, checkpoint.TriggerPeriodic)`), wired in `cmd/lenny-gateway` to the existing checkpoint service, under a context bounded by the 60 s of §4.4.3 **Checkpoint timeout and recovery:** (`// spec: §4.4.3 (Checkpoint Quiescence, Timeout, and Abort Handling)`). On failure, including that deadline, emit `session.suspension_checkpoint_failed`, increment `lenny_session_suspension_checkpoint_failed_total`, record the retry time, and stop. `lockFlight` (`checkpointer/uploaddriver.go`) honors the context and returns `ctx.Err()` when the context ends during its wait.
   3. Clear `PodAssignment` with a guarded `Update` (`State == suspended && SuspendedAt.Equal(row.SuspendedAt) && PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment`). A guard miss stops the release.
      - Then call `migrateInboxOnResumePending(ctx, row)`, whose doc comment names the release, and re-read with `ReadPrimary`. On a terminal row, call `s.drainMessagingOnTerminal(ctx, reread)` (SPEC-5z item 11). A re-read error is logged, and the release continues.
      - `migrateInboxOnResumePending` passes `sess.State` to `MigrateInboxToDLQ`, and `recordDrainFailure` uses it as the `session_state` label in place of the literal `"resume_pending"`.
      - Rewrite the `InboxDrainFailure` `Description` in `pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go` to the SPEC-5q §16.5 wording, and run `make generate`.
   3a. Check the backlog once (SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**): call `HeldLen` as item 2 states, and when the count is above zero call `enterResumePending`. Either error is logged, and the release continues with step 4.
   4. Under `s.slotAccountLocks` for `row.ID`, honoring `ctx`, call `releaseReportedBinding(ctx, row.ID, bind, executor.DispositionCompleted)` over the binding that D2 read. A lock wait that `ctx` ends takes the outcome of a stop between steps 3 and 4. Under the lock and before the release, stop as at a stop between steps 3 and 4 when this replica holds an open terminal claim for the session or step 3's re-read read a terminal row. D6 or the writer's hook then releases the binding with the terminal disposition. When the helper skips, or when it returns false after logging a release error, stop before step 5. Before releasing the lock, call `s.expireRaisedElicitations(ctx, row)` as item 19.3 states. Invariant: step 4 never releases a binding or a lease that a resume attempt created. Three facts hold it:
      - `releaseEarlierBinding` runs under the same lock before every attempt's claim, so a replacement binding is published after step 4 releases the lock, or fails the pointer-identity check of `releaseReportedBinding` (CODE-4 item 22) and is skipped;
      - a restore claims an idle pod or a scrubbed `reserved` hold, never the pod step 4 releases, because the step-2 checkpoint recorded `WorkspaceSnapshot` before step 3;
      - the credential release is generation-scoped (CODE-4 item 24).
   5. Emit `session.pod_released_during_suspension` and increment `lenny_session_pod_released_during_suspension_total`.
   **Orphan reconciler.** The mutator of `orphansession.failOrphan` returns the new sentinel `errOrphanSuperseded` unless `eligible(*s)` holds on the locked row and `s.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment`. This replaces its nil return on a terminal row. On that sentinel, `failOrphan` returns false, with no metric and no terminal hook. Cite `// spec: §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §10.1.4 (Coordinator-Loss Detection and Hold State)`.

   - intents C6.16, C6.17, and C6.18;
   - intents C6.19, C6.20, and C6.22;
   - the tier-7a intents T1.24 and T1.18;
   - the tier-8 intent T1.28.

   **`SuspendIdle`, the zero-hold release** (SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**). `sweepIdle` calls `IdleSuspender.SuspendIdle(ctx, row, idleSeconds)` (item 5). It returns at once under D2. It writes nothing when it found no binding or the binding carries no adapter connection. Otherwise it returns at once when D1 refuses this replica, when a release or an idle suspension of the session is in flight on this replica, when this replica holds an open terminal claim for the session, or when the session's last checkpoint failure is under 60 s old, using `ReleaseSuspendedPod`'s in-flight and retry records. Otherwise it runs these steps asynchronously, as `ReleaseSuspendedPod` does:
      1. Call `s.sealSession(ctx, bind, idleSeconds)` (**Session seal.**).
         - On `sealed == false`, call `s.activity.StampAt(row.TenantID, row.ID, at.Add(-idleAge))` with `at := s.clock()`, log at INFO with the session ID and `activity_recent`, and stop.
         - On an error whose message begins `activity_untapped:`, call `s.activity.Stamp(row.TenantID, row.ID)`, log at INFO with the session ID and `activity_untapped`, and stop.
         - On any other error, call `s.activity.Stamp(row.TenantID, row.ID)`, log at INFO with the session ID and `seal_unavailable`, and stop.

         No branch writes the session row or records a retry time. The session stays `running`, and the moved `last_agent_activity_at` schedules the next attempt. Cite `// spec: §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states)` on the `activity_untapped` branch.
      2. Checkpoint as `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 2 states, with its deadline, event, metric, and retry time. The seal precedes the checkpoint, so no delivered message changes the workspace after it. On failure, call `s.unsealSession(ctx, bind)` and stop.
      3. Capture `at := s.clock()` once. Run one `Update` whose mutator copies the locked row into `seen` and calls the pure function `classifyIdleWrite(seen, row sessionstore.Session, gen int64) idleWriteOutcome` with `gen = bind.CoordinationGeneration`. It returns `idleWriteStale` when `seen.CoordinationGeneration != gen`. Otherwise it returns `idleWriteApply` when `seen.State == running`, `seen.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment`, and `seen.LastAgentActivityAt.Equal(row.LastAgentActivityAt)`; `idleWriteLanded` when `seen.State == suspended`, `seen.SuspendedReason == SuspendedReasonIdle`, and `seen.PodAssignment == ""`, because at this generation only this incarnation writes that row; `idleWriteLift` when `seen.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment`; and `idleWriteStop` otherwise. On `idleWriteApply` the mutator sets `State = suspended`, `SuspendedAt = at`, `SuspendedReason = SuspendedReasonIdle`, and `PodAssignment = ""` and returns nil. On every other outcome it changes nothing and returns the package sentinel `errIdleWriteNotApplied`. The write follows the Design **Guarded-write repeat rule.**, with `seen` as its copied row, `errIdleWriteNotApplied` as its sentinel, and the same `at` on every try. `sessionstore.ErrNotFound` takes **Stop**. A repeat classifies a row its own landed commit left as `idleWriteLanded`, so no branch depends on an earlier error. Then:
         - **Apply or Landed.** Continue with step 4 over the stored row: the returned row on Apply, `seen` on Landed.
         - **Lift.** Call `unsealSession`, emit nothing, and stop.
         - **Stale.** Send the pod no RPC (no `unsealSession` and no release). The pod release is not generation-checked, so only this attempt's own check keeps it from releasing a pod an adopter serves. Call `s.evictStaleBinding(row.ID, bind)`, log WARN `coordinator_preempted` with the session ID, `bind.CoordinationGeneration`, and `seen.CoordinationGeneration`, and stop.
         - **Stop.** Emit nothing and stop. The pod is no longer the session's, and a podless row left by a landed write that a later transition moved goes to the orphan-claim GC, as a stop between steps 3 and 4 of `ReleaseSuspendedPod` does.
         - **Context end during the repeat.** Emit nothing and stop.
         - Step 2's failure and **Lift** are the only `unsealSession` call sites. Every other outcome leaves the seal, and SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** states how it ends.

7. **Message routing** (SPEC-5i, SPEC-5f).
   - **Classification.** `messagerouting.Classify` returns `ActionResumeAndDeliver` for every `suspended` target, whatever `immediate` is, and `isRecovering` admits `session.StateResuming`. Both message paths take the delivery lock, read the row with `ReadPrimary`, and classify that row (D3, D5).
   - **Path 2.** Before every `executor.Send` of a new message on `ActionDeliver`, either path calls `deliverBuffered` (on MCP, `SessionDelivery.DeliverHeld` with the classified row) and handles its error as an error of that send. When `deliverBuffered` or that `executor.Send` returns an error that satisfies `errors.Is(err, executor.ErrSessionSealed)` (item 4 **Session seal.**), the path, still holding the lock, reads with `ReadPrimary` and routes the batch's undelivered messages once from that row's classification. A refusal from that `executor.Send` records the messages `SealedError.Delivered` counts as delivered, and a refusal from `deliverBuffered` delivered none of the batch. The receipt is the outcome of the undelivered messages. A second refusal takes the held branch. The delegation child's first message and the single-shot translators keep their existing error handling, which reports a seal refusal as a delivery failure.
   - **Held branch.** Call `holdMessage` (CODE-4 item 7). Only on `queued` or `dropped`, call `enterResumePending` (CODE-4 item 9). A guard miss or a logged store error does not change the answer. The answers are `queued`, `dropped` with `dlq_overflow`, `TARGET_TERMINAL` for `terminal`, and `error` with `inbox_unavailable` for an error.
   - **REST, `ActionResumeAndDeliver`** in `deliverMessageBatch`:
     - When `holdsPod(row)` and D1 admits this replica, call `resumeHeldAndDrain` and then send the message as today.
     - On `errHeldDeliveryFailed`, take the held branch. The message waits in the DLQ behind the failed entry, and `enterResumePending` writes nothing because the row holds a pod.
     - Otherwise, or on any other `resumeHeldAndDrain` error, take the held branch.
     - Delete the `bufferTargetInbox` fallback.
     - `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` handles every error of the resumed message's `executor.Send`. It reads with `ReadPrimary`. A terminal row answers `TARGET_TERMINAL` and enqueues nothing. A `running` or `input_required` row buffers to the inbox. Any other row, or a re-read error, takes the held branch. Delete its inbox branch on a re-read error and its enqueue on the pre-resume row.
   - **MCP** (`mcptools_register.go`):
     - The `PhasePreMessageDelivery` block (the weakening-cooldown check, the `maxInputSize` bound, and the interceptor chain) runs before `Classify` on every branch. A refusal holds nothing, and a held or inbox entry carries the `MODIFY` result (§4.8).
     - An `ActionResumeAndDeliver` or `ActionBufferDLQ` decision calls `deps.Delivery.HoldMessage`. For `ActionResumeAndDeliver`, unless the hold returned `terminal` or an error, the path then releases the lock and calls `deps.Delivery.ResumeSuspended` with the classified row.
     - It answers as the held branch does. A `ResumeSuspended` error leaves the answer unchanged, and a held-pod resume also answers `queued`.
     - The MCP path never calls `EnqueueInbox` or `Executor.Send` for a `suspended` target.

With a nil `deps.Delivery`, the MCP path skips `DeliverHeld` and answers a held or resume decision with `error` and `inbox_unavailable`.
   - **`ResumeSuspended`** takes the delivery lock and branches on the row it is passed. It reads no row, and every write it makes is guarded.
     - When `!holdsPod(row)`, it calls `enterResumePending`.
     - When `holdsPod(row)` and D1 admits this replica, it calls `resumeHeldAndDrain`. On `errResumeHeldPodReleased` it calls `enterResumePending`, and on `errHeldDeliveryFailed` it returns nil.
     - When `holdsPod(row)` and D1 refuses this replica, it calls `enterResumePending`, which writes only when the pod was released after the read. Otherwise the coordinating replica's backlog resume acts.

     - call `DeliverHeld` only when the child is `running`;

Each page continues after the last `id` of the page before it, so a pass holds at most one page of rows. For each row of a page, `Pass` calls `HeldLen` as item 2 states, with at most `Concurrency` calls in flight (default 32, `errgroup.Group.SetLimit`), and calls `enterResumePending(ctx, row)` when the count is above zero.
      - It applies no lease gate and takes no delivery lock.
      - A list, `HeldLen`, or write error is logged with the tenant or session ID, and the pass continues; a page list error ends that tenant's pass for this tick. A row resumed and suspended again after the listing may be resumed once more than needed, which is accepted.
      - `Run(ctx)` calls `Pass` once at start, then on a ticker until `ctx` ends.

18. **Adapter activity clock and `busy`** (SPEC-5ab, SPEC-5ac). The clock is the state `SealSession` compares (item 4 **Session seal.**). Invariant: no state of the clock crosses a checkpoint, and every write of a session's `session_start` starts a fresh window. `SealSession` places no seal while a `CH-MCP-PLATFORM` or `CH-MCP-CONNECTOR` call for the session is in flight. Cite `// spec: §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK)` on each part.
   1. **Clock and inventory.** `Server` gains `clock func() time.Time`, which tests set, and a `now()` method that returns `time.Now()` when `clock` is nil, as `SessionUsageMeter.clock` (`usage.go`) does. `slotState` gains `lastActivity time.Time` and `toolCalls int`, guarded by `s.mu`. `createSlotStateLocked` (`slot.go`) creates both at zero, `deregisterSlotLocked` (`slotsession.go`), the only removal, takes them with the entry, and no site clears `lastActivity`. The table below is the complete set of activity setting sites on both clocks. The adapter rows (A) set `lastActivity` and `toolCalls`, which only `SealSession` reads (item 4 **Session seal.**), and are the SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** activity list. The gateway rows (G) advance `last_agent_activity_at` through `sessionidle.Stamper`, which `sweepIdle` reads (item 5), and are the SPEC-5e qualifying events and the SPEC-5f seal answer. A write of either clock at a site outside the table is a defect. `sweepIdle`'s fallback to `UpdatedAt` for a zero anchor (`watchdog.go`) is a reader and sets nothing.

      **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the lock structure of each adapter site, within these bounds: resolve the entry and stamp it in one hold of `s.mu`; a stamp never changes an entry other than the one it resolved, so a later stamp or decrement by the same site (a write's return, a call's return) acts on the entry pointer captured when it resolved; and no state crosses a checkpoint.

   2. **Tap.** `linefanout.Hub` gains `Tap(fn func(line []byte))`, set before `Serve`. The reader calls `fn` with each line before `broadcast`, whether or not a subscriber is registered, and `fn` does not block beyond a short-held mutex.

Rewrite the `Output` doc comments and the hub field comments (`InProcessRuntime.hub`, `subprocessSession.stdoutPipe`/`output`) in `embedded.go` and `subprocess.go`, which say the reader starts at the first `Output`. `MCPRuntime` calls `fn` with each frame it delivers to its output channel.
      - `NewGRPCServer` calls `s.installActivityTap()` once, which calls `TapOutput(s.observeRuntimeFrame)` and sets `s.activityTapped` when `s.Runtime` implements `outputTapper`. `RuntimeProcess` is unchanged.
   3. **Per-entry heartbeats** (OD-S8, Decision 16). Invariant: while heartbeats can reach the runtime, every registry entry whose `session_start` is written and whose `session_end` is not written has exactly one running heartbeat monitor, independent of `Attach`; no heartbeat for a session is written after its `session_end`; and no monitor outlives its entry.
      - `s.heartbeatsReachRuntime() bool` is `s.HeartbeatInterval > 0 && s.activityTapped && s.RuntimeKind != RuntimeKindMCP`. It gates the monitor start below. An untapped runtime gets no monitor, because its acks would reach none and every session would read as hung. A `type: mcp` runtime drops `heartbeat` (`mcpruntime.go`), so it gets none either. With no monitor, `SealSession` decides on frames and platform and connector calls alone (item 4 **Session seal.**).
      - `heartbeatMonitor` owns its context and gains a `done` channel that `run` closes on return, `cancel()`, which cancels the context once and returns at once, and `stop()`, which calls `cancel()` and then waits on `done`. The monitor goroutine never takes `s.mu`: its write is `WriteEnvelope`, and on a missed ack it closes `hung` and calls `s.onHeartbeatHung`, which takes no lock. `stop()` is therefore never called under `s.mu`, and a heartbeat write in flight when it is called has returned when it returns.
      - `slotState` gains `hb *heartbeatMonitor`, guarded by `s.mu`. `markSessionStartWritten` takes the session ID, which each of its callers in `sessionframes.go` holds. In its existing hold, when `written` is true, `entry.hb` is nil, and `s.heartbeatsReachRuntime()` holds, it builds `newHeartbeatMonitor(s.HeartbeatInterval, s.HeartbeatAckTimeout, sessionID, write, onHung)`, stores it in `entry.hb`, and starts its goroutine. That is the only start site. Clearing the flag starts and stops nothing, because every caller that clears it writes `session_end` next.
      - `writeSessionEnd` takes the entry as a second parameter (`writeSessionEnd(sessionID string, entry *slotState)`). Before it writes the frame, and before its `type: mcp` early return, it reads `entry.hb` under `s.mu`, releases `s.mu`, and calls `hb.stop()` when the monitor is not nil. Every caller holds the entry and calls it with `s.mu` released: `openRuntimeSession` and `failUnacknowledgedStart` (`sessionframes.go`, `claim.entry` and `entry`), `tearDownReclaimedSlot` (`session.go`, `r.st`), and `DemoteSDK` (`sdkwarm.go`, the entry it reads under `s.mu` before the `session_end` write, captured there). The `DemoteSDK` path, which writes `session_end` while the entry still stands and removes the entry only after `sw.DemoteSDK` returns, therefore writes no heartbeat after the frame, including when `sw.DemoteSDK` fails and the entry stays. Update the `sessionframes_test.go` call to the new signature.
      - `deregisterSlotLocked` (`slotsession.go`), the only removal, calls `hb.cancel()` because it holds `s.mu`, and leaves `hb` on the removed entry so that `tearDownReclaimedSlot`'s later `writeSessionEnd(sessionID, r.st)` joins the goroutine before its write. These are the only start and stop sites: `markSessionStartWritten` starts a monitor, `writeSessionEnd` stops and joins one, and `deregisterSlotLocked` cancels one.
      - `frame()` writes `{"type":"heartbeat","ts":N,"sessionId":"<id>"}`. `write` is `s.Runtime.WriteEnvelope(sessionID, frame)`, and a write error ends the monitor as it does today.
      - When the ack deadline passes, the monitor closes `hung` and then calls `s.onHeartbeatHung` once on its own context. The entry keeps the stopped monitor, so `hung` stays closed until the entry is removed.
      - `startHeartbeat` is deleted. `Attach`, after `checkSessionBound`, reads the entry's `hb.hung` under `s.mu` (nil when the entry has no monitor) and selects on it. A closed channel, whether it closed before or during the stream, ends the stream with `DeadlineExceeded` and makes no `Interrupt` call. The loop consumes every `heartbeat_ack` without relaying it, whether or not a monitor exists, and calls no `ack()`. Rewrite the `sessionScopedFrameTypes` and `demuxSessionOutput` comments, which say the demultiplexer keeps a per-stream monitor answering, because acks now reach monitors through the tap.
      - The JSONL schema edits for `heartbeat` and `heartbeat_ack` land earlier, at checklist step S8, as **Staged schema, chart, and migration changes** states.
      Cite `// spec: §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK), §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract)` on each part.
   4. **SDKs.** In each of the Go, Python, and TypeScript runtime SDKs, a session state carries a pending-message count and a hold count, and is busy while either is above zero. The frame loop increments the pending count when it routes a `message` to the session, before the handler can dequeue it, and the SDK decrements it when the handler for that message returns, after writing the `response` or error response it produced, if any, so no instant between reading the frame and finishing its handling reads idle. A handler that returns a Reply marked streaming and not final writes no `response`, and its return still decrements the count.
      - A hold API lets a handler report work after its handler returns, as §15.7 **Busy reporting.** (SPEC-5ac item 8) states: Go `func HoldBusy(ctx context.Context) (release func())` in `context.go`, reading the session from a new `ctxKeySession` value that `withSessionContext` sets; Python `HandlerTools.hold_busy()` (`runtime.py`) and `CreateRequest.hold_busy()` (`types.py`), each a context manager whose exit releases; TypeScript `HandlerTools.holdBusy(): () => void` and `CreateRequest.holdBusy(): () => void` (`types.ts`). The SDK sets the `CreateRequest` member to the same session-scoped hold before it invokes `on_create` or `onCreate`, so work a runtime starts when the session opens, before any `message`, takes a hold that outlives `on_create`; Go `OnCreate` reaches `HoldBusy` through the context `withSessionContext` supplies. Each increments the hold count; the release decrements it once, and a second call to it does nothing. A context or tools value naming no live session returns a no-op release. A session's `session_end` zeroes its counts.
      - A `heartbeat` naming a session the process holds is answered with `{"type":"heartbeat_ack","sessionId":<id>,"busy":<busy>}`, omitting `busy` when it is false.
      - A `heartbeat` naming no session, or a session the process does not hold, is answered with the bare `{"type":"heartbeat_ack"}`.
      - In Go, `outboundHeartbeatAck` (`types.go`) gains `SessionID string \`json:"sessionId,omitempty"\`` and `Busy bool \`json:"busy,omitempty"\``.
      - The SDK doc comments state that work after a handler returns is reported only through the hold, and that a release can end unreported work. Cite `// spec: §15.7 (Runtime Author SDKs), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK)`.
      - `cmd/runtimes/echo-concurrent`: the front loop routes a `heartbeat` whose `sessionId` names a held session to that session's echocore worker, and `slotWriter.stamp` stamps the resulting `heartbeat_ack` instead of forwarding it unchanged; a `heartbeat` naming no session or an unheld one keeps the front loop's bare ack, never `rejectUnheld`. Rewrite the comments in `dispatch.go`, `slot.go`, and `main.go` that say `heartbeat` carries no per-session identifier. Cite `// spec: §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK)`.
   5. **Compliance.** `cmd/lenny-compliance/main.go` adds `{"heartbeat_busy", "15.4", checkHeartbeatBusy}`. The check, in the new `cmd/lenny-compliance/heartbeatbusy.go`, implements the §15.4.6 **heartbeat busy** row (SPEC-5ac item 5), reading frames until it holds both the `response` and the `heartbeat_ack`. `cmd/lenny-ctl/runtimescaffold/probe.go` `checkLevel` gains `"heartbeat_busy": compliance.LevelBasic`, because `failuresAtOrBelow` skips a check name the map does not know.

19. **Elicitation expiry when a session leaves its pod** (SPEC-5p2). Invariant: after each call site in item 19.3 commits and `ExpireRaisedBy` succeeds, no `interactions` row with `kind = elicitation`, `phase = pending`, and `detail.originPod` equal to that session exists, except one a later restored runtime of the session raises. A respond or dismiss for an expired elicitation is refused with `ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` through the existing `ErrNotFound` paths of `resolveInteraction` and `ResolveElicitation`. The item adds one index migration and no phase, handler branch, or resolution-path change.
   1. `interactionstore.Store` gains `ExpireRaisedBy(ctx context.Context, tenantID, originSessionID string) (int, error)`. It deletes every row with `kind = elicitation`, `phase = pending`, the given tenant, and `detail["originPod"] == originSessionID`, and returns the count. The key is the raising session, which the `request_elicitation` handler writes to `detail["originPod"]` (`mcptools_register.go`). It is never the row's `session_id`, which names the chain resolver, so a child's elicitation held under §8.2 for this session is not matched. Migration `0185_interactions_pending_elicitation_origin_index` (the next free number at implementation) adds `CREATE INDEX idx_interactions_pending_elicitation_origin ON interactions (tenant_id, (detail->>'originPod')) WHERE kind = 'elicitation' AND phase = 'pending';`, with the matching `DROP INDEX` in the down file. pgstore runs one `DELETE FROM interactions WHERE tenant_id = $1 AND kind = 'elicitation' AND phase = 'pending' AND detail->>'originPod' = $2` in `pgtenant.InTx`; `kind` and `phase` are literals so the planner matches the index predicate, and the delete reads only the tenant's pending elicitations with that origin. `idx_interactions_tenant_user` is not used, because resolved rows stay until erasure and a per-user scan grows with the user's history. `Memory` deletes under its mutex. A §8.6 lease-extension row carries no `originPod` and is never matched. `interactionstore.Memory` and `pgstore.Store` are the only satisfiers. Cite `// spec: §9.2 (Elicitation Chain)`.
   2. New `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/elicitation_expiry.go`: `(s *Server) expireRaisedElicitations(ctx context.Context, row sessionstore.Session)`. It returns at once when `s.interactions` is nil. Otherwise it calls `s.interactions.ExpireRaisedBy(row.TenantID, row.ID)` on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` bounded by 5 s, and logs an error at WARN with the session ID. It never fails its caller. Cite `// spec: §9.2 (Elicitation Chain), §7.3 (Retry and Resume)`.
   3. The only call sites:
            - The post-terminal hook, step 7 (Design **Terminal transition.** (D7)), after every terminal write that runs the hook.
            - `ReportSessionFailure` (`failure.go`): after `applyFailureFromActive` or `applyFailureFromResuming` returns a nil error and `disp.To` is `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action`. A `failed` disposition expires through the hook that `transitionToFailed` runs, and a no-op or conflict disposition expires nothing.
      - `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 4 (item 4): under `s.slotAccountLocks`, after `releaseReportedBinding` returns or skips, on every path past step 3's committed clear, including a skipped or failed release. The call follows the pod release, so the runtime on the released pod cannot raise again after it, and it precedes the lock release, so no resume attempt has claimed a pod. `SuspendIdle` step 4 runs that step, so the idle release expires on Apply and Landed. A checkpoint failure, a guard miss, Lift, Stale, and Stop expire nothing, because the session keeps its pod or another incarnation owns the row. A lock wait that `ctx` ends expires nothing, as a stop between steps 3 and 4.
   4. The `request_elicitation` handler's terminal-state check reads the row with `sessionstore.ReadPrimary(ctx, deps.Store, deps.PrimaryReadTimeout, tenant, sessionID)` in place of `deps.Store.Get`, and a read error takes the existing `errSessionLookup` path. A raise whose check runs after a terminal write commits is refused on any replica, so the expiry misses only a raise whose check passed before the write (D7 hook step 7). Both resolution paths are unchanged. A handler still polling an expired row gets `ErrNotFound` from `Get` and returns through its `elicitation lookup` branch, which calls `recordTerminal`.

Targets: `migrations/0184_sessions_counted_by_tenant_index.up.sql` and `.down.sql`; `tests/tier2_component/migrations/prod_columns_test.go`; `pkg/api/v1/session/session.go`; `pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go`, `pgstore/pgstore.go`, and `memstore/memstore.go`; `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go`, `sessionserver.go`, `resume.go`, `resume_held_pod.go`, `failure.go`, `messages_delivery.go`, `suspended_backlog.go`, and the file that holds `ResumeBacklog` (CODE-6 item 2); `pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools.go` and `mcptools_register.go`; `cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go`, `metrics_exporters.go`, and `httpsurface.go`; `charts/lenny/values.yaml` and `charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml`, with the regenerated `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml`.

Spec: SPEC-6, SPEC-5i **Admission.** and **Backlog resume.**, SPEC-3b, SPEC-5j. CODE-7 lands after CODE-4 and CODE-6 and amends functions they add: `handleResume` (CODE-4 item 8), `enterResumePending` (CODE-4 item 9), `resumeHeldPod` (CODE-6 item 6), `ResumeBacklog` (CODE-6 item 2), the message paths and `SessionDelivery` (CODE-6 item 7), and `BacklogRechecker.Pass` (CODE-6 item 16).

1. **Count predicate.** Add `session.CountsTowardConcurrencyCaps(s State) bool`, true when `!IsTerminal(s) && s != StateSuspended`, cited `// spec: §11.1 (Concurrent-session count)`. The `pgstore` predicate of `CountActiveSessions`, `CountActiveSessionsByUser`, `CountActiveSessionsByRuntime`, and `CountActiveSessionsGlobal` becomes `state NOT IN ('completed', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'expired', 'suspended')`, Migration `0184_sessions_counted_by_tenant_index` (the next free number after CODE-6 item 16's 0183) replaces the migration-0102 index, whose predicate no longer equals the count predicate. Under the old index each count would read a heap tuple for every `suspended` row of the tenant, and those rows are not bounded by any cap and persist for up to `gateway.maxSuspendedSessionSeconds`. The up file is `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS idx_sessions_active_by_tenant;` followed by `CREATE INDEX idx_sessions_counted_by_tenant ON sessions (tenant_id, user_id) WHERE state NOT IN ('completed', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'expired', 'suspended');`. The down file drops `idx_sessions_counted_by_tenant` and recreates `idx_sessions_active_by_tenant` with the migration-0102 definition. The index predicate and the four count predicates are the same text. The tenant and per-user counts read it index-only, the per-runtime count reads it by tenant prefix, and the global count scans it. `CountActiveDelegatedChildrenByUser` keeps the 4-state predicate and `idx_sessions_tenant_user`. Rewrite the `CountActiveSessions` pgstore doc comment, which names migration 0102, to name `idx_sessions_counted_by_tenant` and migration 0184, and cite the migration header `-- spec: §11.1 (Concurrent-session count)`. The four `memstore` methods call the helper in place of `!session.IsTerminal`. Rewrite the four `sessionstore.go` interface comments and the pgstore and memstore doc comments to say "non-terminal sessions other than `suspended`" and name the helper. `CountActiveSessionsInRecoveryGlobal`, `CountActiveDelegatedChildrenByUser`, `PoolDrainStats`, and `exportHPAGauges` are unchanged. `exportSessionAvailabilityRatio` divides by `CountActiveSessionsGlobal`, so the §16.5 ratio's denominator stops counting `suspended` sessions, which run no turn and are in no recovery state; rewrite its doc comment and the `CountActiveSessionsInRecoveryGlobal` interface comment to state that. Cite `// spec: §16.5 (Alerting Rules and SLOs), §11.1 (Concurrent-session count)` on `exportSessionAvailabilityRatio` (SPEC-6e). The wrapping test stores (`countingStore`, `slowAncestorStore`) delegate and are unchanged.
2. **Shared cap check** in `quota.go`. Extract the counting from `requireSessionQuota` and `requireConcurrencyLimits`:
   - `CapRefusal{Scope string; Limit, Active int}`, exported because `mcptools` reads it, with `Error()`, `Message() string`, and `Details() map[string]any`. For `Scope == "tenant"`, `Message` is "the tenant has reached its concurrent-session limit" and `Details` is `{"limit", "active"}`. For `user`, `runtime`, and `global`, `Message` is "the <scope> concurrent-session limit was reached" and `Details` is `{"scope", "limit", "active"}`. These are the bodies creation writes today.
   - `checkTenantCap(ctx, tenantID string) error` and `checkScopeCaps(ctx, tenantID, userID, runtimeRef string) error` run today's logic in today's order (tenant; then per-user, per-runtime, and global, each only when enabled) and return nil, a `*CapRefusal`, or a wrapped lookup or count error.
   - `writeCapRefusal(w, *CapRefusal)` writes `429 QUOTA_EXCEEDED` with `Message()` and `Details()`.
   `requireSessionQuota` and `requireConcurrencyLimits` keep their signatures and call sites and become wrappers that answer a refusal through `writeCapRefusal` and any other error with today's `500 INTERNAL_ERROR`. `admitResume(ctx context.Context, row sessionstore.Session) error` runs `checkTenantCap(row.TenantID)` and then `checkScopeCaps(row.TenantID, row.UserID, row.RuntimeRef)`; with every cap disabled it issues no count query. Cite `// spec: §11.1 (Resume admission), §11.1 (Concurrent admissions)`.
3. **Writes out of `suspended`.** `enterResumePending`, when its expected source state is `suspended`, and `resumeHeldPod` call `admitResume` on the row they were passed before their `Update`, and on an error return it unchanged and write nothing. `enterResumePending` from `awaiting_client_action` does not call it, because that session already counts. The `Update` guards are unchanged, so the check and the write are not atomic, as SPEC-6a **Concurrent admissions.** states. `applyFailureFromActive` does not call `admitResume`.
4. **Request paths.**
   - `handleResume`: a `*CapRefusal` from `enterResumePending` or `resumeHeldAndDrain`, on any branch, is answered through `writeCapRefusal`, and any other `admitResume` error with `500 INTERNAL_ERROR`; the row stays `suspended`. The off-holder held-pod branch that answers `TARGET_NOT_READY` writes nothing and runs no check.
   - REST `deliverMessageBatch`, `ActionResumeAndDeliver`: first call `admitResume` on the classified row. A `*CapRefusal` is answered through `writeCapRefusal` and any other error with `500 INTERNAL_ERROR`, and nothing is held, sent, or written. A `*CapRefusal` that `resumeHeldAndDrain` or `enterResumePending` returns afterwards takes the held branch as CODE-6 item 7 states for their other errors, does not change its answer, and is logged at INFO with the session ID and scope.
   - MCP `lenny/send_message`: `SessionDelivery` gains `AdmitResume(ctx context.Context, row sessionstore.Session) error`, which `*sessionserver.Server` satisfies with `admitResume`; its fakes gain the method. On `ActionResumeAndDeliver` the path calls it after the `PhasePreMessageDelivery` block and before `HoldMessage`. A `*sessionserver.CapRefusal` (`errors.As`) returns `mcp.NewToolError("QUOTA_EXCEEDED", r.Message(), r.Details())`, any other error is returned as the tool's Go error, and nothing is held. A refusal from `ResumeSuspended` leaves the answer unchanged, as its other errors do.
5. **Backlog resume.** `ResumeBacklog` calls `admitResume` synchronously after a `HeldLen` count above zero; on any error it logs at INFO with the session ID, and the scope for a `*CapRefusal`, and returns false, so the hold timer decides the release this tick. `BacklogRechecker.Pass` and `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 3a log a `*CapRefusal` from `enterResumePending` at INFO with the session ID and scope, leave the row `suspended`, and continue; the next pass or evaluation tries again.
6. **Flag help and comments.** The help of `--max-concurrent-sessions-global`, `--max-concurrent-sessions-per-user`, and `--max-concurrent-sessions-per-runtime` replaces "live non-terminal sessions" with "non-terminal sessions other than suspended ones" and adds "A resume of a suspended session is checked against the cap." Rewrite the `maxConcSess*` field comment in `sessionserver.go`, the `requireSessionQuota` and `requireConcurrencyLimits` doc comments, and the `httpsurface.go` comment that names `requireSessionQuota` to match. Rewrite the `gateway.concurrentSessions` comment in `charts/lenny/values.yaml`, which counts "live (non-terminal) sessions admitted at POST /v1/sessions" and says "excess creates get 429 QUOTA_EXCEEDED", and the cap comment in `charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml`, which says "live non-terminal session counts", to say non-terminal sessions other than `suspended` ones and that a resume of a `suspended` session is also refused with `429 QUOTA_EXCEEDED`; then regenerate `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml`, which carries the rendered template comment.

Pinned by the CODE-7 intents C7.1 through C7.14.

## Staged schema, chart, and migration changes

The active-age migration is part of CODE-1, the podless `suspended` index migration is part of CODE-6 item 16, the counted-session index migration 0184 is part of CODE-7, the pending-elicitation index migration 0185 is part of CODE-6 item 19, the `SealSession` and `UnsealSession` proto edit, with `idle_seconds` and the `SealSessionResponse` fields, is part of CODE-6 item 4, each `openapi.json` edit and its regenerated MCP schemas are part of the CODE item that names it, and the chart values and the `deadline_approaching` schema and proto edits are part of CODE-6. Each lands in the step of the code that reads it.

**Heartbeat frames.** The `heartbeat` and `heartbeat_ack` edits below land at checklist step S8, after TEST-0 at S7 and ahead of CODE-6 and DOCS-1b. The tier-11 `TestFrameIdentifierAgreesAcrossSchemaSpecAndReference` (`tests/tier11_docs/frame_identifier_schema_reconciliation_test.go`) requires the published JSONL schema, the §28.5.3 frame cards, and the frame sections of `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md` to agree, frame by frame, on whether `sessionId` is declared, so the schema and the reference change in one step, and the test fails from S5, where SPEC-5ac changes the frame cards, until S8. DOCS-1b owns the rest of `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md`.
- In `schemas/lenny-adapter-jsonl.schema.json`, `heartbeat` gains optional `sessionId` (string); `heartbeat_ack` gains optional `sessionId` (string) and `busy` (boolean), with an `if` clause requiring `sessionId` when `busy` is `true`; and both descriptions replace "it carries no per-session identifier and is outside the session addressing rule" with the SPEC-5ac wording.
- In `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md`, the `heartbeat` section's example and the `heartbeat_ack` section's example gain `sessionId` and `busy` as SPEC-5ac items 1 and 2 state, the `heartbeat_ack` sentence "No other fields." is replaced with the SPEC-5ac field table and rule, step 7 of the annotated trace replaces `{"type":"heartbeat","ts":1717430410}` with `{"type":"heartbeat","ts":1717430410,"sessionId":"sess_abc"}`, matching the `sess_abc` the trace's other steps use, and the **Multiple Outstanding Tool Calls** trace's heartbeat `{"type":"heartbeat","ts":1717430420}` becomes `{"type":"heartbeat","ts":1717430420,"sessionId":"sess_abc"}`, and the same trace's ack `{"type":"heartbeat_ack"}`, written while two tool calls are outstanding, becomes `{"type":"heartbeat_ack","sessionId":"sess_abc","busy":true}`.
- In `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md`, the `heartbeat` section's sentence "If no ack is received, the adapter treats the process as hung and ends that session's stream to the gateway, and the runtime process receives no signal." becomes "If no ack is received, the adapter treats the runtime as hung for the session the heartbeat names: it ends the session's stream to the gateway when one is open and refuses each later stream for the session until the adapter releases the session, and the runtime process receives no signal." (SPEC-5ac item 7). The sentence that follows it stays.
- In `tests/tier3_contract/adapter_jsonl/session_scoped_frame_address_test.go`, `TestSessionScopedFramesDeclareSessionAddress` removes `heartbeat` and `heartbeat_ack` from `protocolFrames` and deletes that loop and the `protocolFrames` variable with its comment, which state that a protocol-level frame carries no per-session address. In their place it asserts that both frames declare `sessionId` as an optional string (absent from `required`) and declare no `slotId`. Without this change the schema edit fails the test at the first later step that runs tier 3.

Content: State the retry accounting that SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** gives. On the client guide, describe automatic recovery first and present `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/resume` as the client override, which returns the session in its new state while the restore's outcome arrives on the event stream. Idle suspension (no activity for the idle window, a runtime's `busy` report counting as activity, default 900 s, the workspace checkpointed and the pod or slot released as the session is suspended, a session whose adapter observed activity in the window left `running`, and a session that cannot be idle-suspended (a `type: mcp` runtime or an `executionMode: service` pool) expired instead), resume on any message with no `delivery: "immediate"` condition, the suspended-session lifetime with its default and the `gateway.maxSuspendedSessionSeconds` setting, active-time session age, the renamed gateway flag, and the budget-key TTL default with its constraint that it exceed the suspended-session lifetime. The `maxClientIdleSeconds` half of each `session_expired` description, and the `max_idle_time` description, cover the idle expiry of a session that cannot be idle-suspended (a `type: mcp` runtime or an `executionMode: service` pool) and the suspended-session lifetime expiry. A message to a `suspended` session resumes it; a REST message that the coordinating replica can deliver at once to a held pod is delivered, and every other message that the concurrent-session check admits is held in the session's dead-letter queue until the resume delivers it, and the `deadline_approaching` trigger values are `session_age` and `budget`.

Both parts: reference tables keep their structure and change only their rows. Cite no section numbers, state no counts, and re-read each table against the prose on its page.

No `sessions` index keys `state`, so each watchdog per-state list, `sweepSuspended` included, reads at best every non-terminal row of the tenant. Filed under §10.1. The gateway leader's backlog re-check reads the partial index `idx_sessions_podless_suspended` (CODE-6 item 16) and is outside this finding.

The replacement claim is referenced by no row until the commit writes `PodAssignment`, so the orphan-claim GC drains it after `claimOrphanTimeout` (5 min). A client message therefore advances the clock only through the agent output it causes. A fix stamps a message only after the adapter writes it to the runtime, as SPEC-5e states, so a message the seal refuses does not stamp. Filed under §6.2.

Filed under §10.1.7.

The F-7.3.19 resolution describes a GET normalisation that the tree does not implement. Filed under §15.1.

Every terminal writer commits the terminal state before the seal runs. `Binder.Release` (`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go`) releases the credential leases first; on the retire path it then sends `Shutdown` and deletes the claim, and on the recycle path it patches the claim `bound → recycling` and then sends the recycle-disposition `Shutdown`; on both paths `Shutdown` follows the terminal mark, the terminal event, and the lease release. The terminal write stays first because every writer's guard and terminal claim key on it. §29.4 step 12 also places the recycle-disposition `Shutdown`, which carries the whole-pod scrub parameters, before step 17's `bound → recycling` patch, while §5.2 **Lenny scrub procedure.** runs the scrub on a claim already `recycling` and the tree patches first. A lease that no releaser returns therefore stays counted until the assigner's process restarts, and so does a spent renewal lease, which nothing releases. Owned by proposal P1 (release ownership for pods, slots, and credential leases). Filed under §4.9.
   - (w) A `CH-ATTACH` stream-failure report that commits no failure edge runs no `accountFailedSlot`, so a concurrent pod's failed slot is not counted toward the §5.2 whole-pod replacement trigger. This covers a report that reads a terminal row (`ReportSessionFailure`), one whose compare-and-swap loses to a terminal write or to a suspend write (an interrupt, or CODE-6 item 4's idle suspension), one that CODE-4 item 22's suspended-source guard turns into `errReportConflict` (`failure.go`), and one that `ReportAttachStreamFailure` drops on a store read error (`stream_failure.go`). The later terminal or suspension release frees the slot through `ReleaseSlot`, which counts nothing. A fix must not count again a slot whose winning write was a sibling failure report that already counted it. CODE-4 item 22a retires a crashed pod that serves one session and leaves the slot count unchanged. Owned by proposal P1. Filed under §5.2.

## Testing

Tests are stated as intents. The implementor chooses test names, fixtures, and file placement; each intent must be pinned by at least one test at each named tier, annotated with its `// spec:` sections. A failure that a test exposes during implementation is fixed or filed as **Failures the tests find.** below states.

Every tier-2-and-higher test also carries a `// diagnosis:` comment that states what a failure means. Each intent listed under a code deliverable lands in that deliverable's step, at each tier it names, and so does each update to an existing test that the deliverable's code breaks or whose subject the deliverable deletes. The step that adds a test at tier 2 or above maps it in `tests/spec-map.json` under the sections its `// spec:` annotation names, and the step that deletes or renames a test at any tier updates its entries there, because tier-0 `validate-maps` fails on an unmapped tier-2-and-higher test file and on an entry naming a missing file or function. TEST-1 holds the other intents at tier 2 and above, and the cross-cutting tier-0 and tier-11 checks. **Step placement.** under **Expansive lifecycle tests** places the cells, operations, and pairs of the X intents, whatever heading lists the intent. The bracketed label on each intent is the anchor that the design, the code items, the inventory tables, and RECORDS-1 cite.

**Failures the tests find.** The tests follow the owner's expansive-test instruction of 2026-10-12 in `gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md` §10.2, so they can fail on defects this proposal did not anticipate. The step whose run exposes a failure classifies it once, applying these items in order:

1. **Not a defect.** A failure that the environment preflight in `.claude/rules/test-coverage.md` attributes to the environment follows that rule. A failure of an existing test that a deliverable of this proposal breaks or whose subject it deletes, as the Testing preamble states, is a test update, which the Testing preamble assigns to the deliverable that changes the behavior. Neither is classified further.
2. **A defect of this proposal.** Fix the defect in the step when the faulty code is a line this proposal's diff adds or changes, or when the failing case passes at the merge base. Fix it even when an OPEN `BUILD-GAPS.md` finding or a `gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md` §10 member records the same defect or its category for a later step. The one exception is a defect that this proposal records as an accepted gap, in RECORDS-1 item 7, the D6 **Accepted gaps.**, an **Edge cases and accepted failure modes** bullet, or Decision 40's `revokeFinalizeLease` departure, which is filed under item 6. When the fix needs a spec change, take the item 6 actions and stop the step for a human decision.
3. **Owned by a later step.** This item applies only to a defect that neither item 2 nor item 4 makes relevant to this proposal. Search `BUILD-GAPS.md` for an OPEN finding that names the failing function or the symptom. Search `gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md` §10 for the same defect: the R-steps in §10.1, and in §10.2 the direct fixes (including F-15.1.43, F-10.1.29, and F-10.1.31, which land after this proposal), the members of P1 to P5, and the recycling follow-up. File an owned defect under item 6, and do not fix it in this proposal.
4. **Relevant to this proposal.** Fix a pre-existing defect that stops an invariant or intent of this proposal from holding and that is either in a file a `Targets:` line of this proposal names or on a path that code this proposal adds calls into, such as a release that a deliverable of this proposal invokes. Fix it even when a later step owns it, except a defect this proposal records as an accepted gap (item 2), which is filed under item 6. When the fix needs a spec change, file the defect under item 6.
5. **Straightforward.** Otherwise, fix the defect when all of these hold:
   - the fix changes no spec text, schema, proto, migration, flag, metric, or chart value;
   - it stays in one Go package and in one function or a few closely related functions, in files that **Files touched on application** names;
   - it adds no new mechanism, cross-component contract, or per-replica state.
6. **File.** Otherwise, take all three of these actions:
   - Add a `BUILD-GAPS.md` finding in that file's format, with the next free `F-<section>.<n>` identifier. Its **Evidence:** names the failing test, the case, and the seed or interleaving that reproduces it. Its **Suggested resolution:** names the owning plan step, or `unassigned`. When an OPEN finding already describes the defect, append the failing test to that finding's **Evidence:** and add no new finding.
   - Skip only the failing case, at the narrowest subtest that contains it, with a `t.Skip` reason that opens with the `blocked:` category and the F-ID, for example `blocked: F-6.2.31 (owner: P3) <one clause stating the defect>`. When the failing check is not its own subtest, split it into one so that the rest of the test keeps running. The tier-0 skip-reason classifier accepts a reason that opens with a category. `tests/registers/skip-reasons.yaml` is a downward-only baseline of uncategorized skips and rejects new entries, so filing makes no register edit.
   - Record a deviation in the step result's `deviations` field naming the intent or test, the F-ID, and the owner. The run writes it to this proposal's deviations file.

A fix under item 2, 4, or 5 lands in the step together with the test that exposed it, and that test fails against the pre-fix code. When an OPEN finding describes only a defect fixed under item 2, 4, or 5, the step marks that finding RESOLVED. When the finding covers more, the step appends the fix and its test to the finding's **Evidence:** and leaves it OPEN. No step weakens, narrows, or deletes a test, its assertions, its cells, its seeds, its fault points, or its timeouts to make it pass, other than such an update. The cost bounds of **Expansive lifecycle tests** decide which cells a pull-request run executes and which only the nightly run executes. They never permit removing a cell from the nightly run, moving a failing cell out of the pull-request run, or relaxing an assertion to make a failure pass.

### Expansive lifecycle tests

The owner expects further unknown defects in the terminal, release, resume, idle, and suspension paths (owner instruction, 2026-10-12), so the intents labelled X1 to X5 exercise those paths exhaustively, and the checker below runs after each of their cells. Each X intent iterates a table this proposal already states, so a row added to that table gains its cells. The expected outcome of every cell comes from the staged spec, these tables, and the **Edge cases and accepted failure modes** bullets, never from a recording of the tree's current behavior. A tier's time budget below is the per-package `go test` timeout that `lenny-test` applies to that tier. Each pull-request run executes every X1, X3, and X5 cell at tier 1 within the tier-1 time budget, and at tier 2 executes the floor that the intent's **Cost floor.** names, each value of each dimension listed there at least once, within the tier-2 time budget. The nightly run executes every X1, X3, and X5 cell at tier 2. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** which tier-2 cells a pull-request run executes above the floor.

**Harness.** The harness is test-only code under `tests/testinfra/` and adds no production seam. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** its package, names, and parameters, within these constraints:
- It runs two or more session servers over one session store, coordination lease store, credential lease store, Redis, and Kubernetes client, under one stepped clock, with a runtime behind each pod that records each message it receives.
- It records every call those servers make to the shared stores, the slot counter, the DLQ and inbox, the Kubernetes client, and the adapter client as one ordered trace, and each trace entry names the call and its call site (the gateway function and the call within it that issued it). At any trace index it can inject one fault of each kind X4 names, or a read-pool read that returns the row one write stale. A crash ends the replica's server-lifetime context, drops its in-memory records, and lets its coordination lease lapse. The same call points are the scheduling points of X2.
- The credential lease TTL and renewal buffer keep their provider defaults, so Edge cases **Renewal racing a release by identifier.** is unreachable. The harness sets every timer a cell fires, and the checker's horizon, below the credential lease TTL.
- **Invariant checker.** It runs on the harness only. A pass advances the stepped clock to the next tick of a loop in the tick set, calls each loop in the tick set once on each surviving replica, and waits for every goroutine that the calls and the cell, X2 run, or X2a sequence started to return, including D6 releases, post-terminal hooks that a writer runs on a goroutine, and resume attempts. The tick set is the coordination Sweeper's `Sweep` and the watchdog's `Tick` on each surviving replica, and the gateway leader's backlog re-check on the replica that holds leadership, each from the step that lands it (**Step placement.**). The checker ticks no other loop. It does not tick the controller's orphan-claim GC, whose one-pass sweep is unexported. It does not tick the orphan-session reconciler or the orphan-cleanup job, whose terminal writes are X1 rows, the created-session sweeper, which hard-deletes expired `created` rows and writes no terminal state, or any credential lease renewal or expiry. The horizon is the latest of the last crash or lease release plus the coordination lease TTL, the last write to a `resuming` row plus `maxResumingSeconds`, and the last write to a `resume_pending` row plus the window `sweepResumePending` applies, each plus one interval of the loop that acts on it. The checker stops once the clock has passed the horizon and two consecutive passes change no state the invariants read, with lease expiry times excluded. The harness sets every other timer a ticked loop applies (idle, session age, suspended lifetime, suspended pod hold, `awaiting_client_action`, and DLQ TTL) longer than a cell, X2 run, or X2a sequence and its horizon run, except the timer the cell's row or edge fires, which the cell fires before the checker runs. Where a shared store expires keys on its own clock, the harness advances that clock with the stepped clock or sets the TTL short enough to wait out in real time. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the loop intervals and timer values within these constraints. A crashed replica is outside the cluster from its crash onward. After the last pass the checker asserts each invariant below as its own subtest for every session the cell, X2 run, or X2a sequence created.

**Invariants.**
- **I1 Pods and claims.** Every `SandboxClaim` in the `bound` state names a pod that the `PodAssignment` of a non-terminal row names. A claim in `recycling` or `reserved` is a pool-owned hold on a recycling pool and is excluded.
- **I2 Slots.** On a concurrent-workspace pod, `REG-SLOTCOUNT` equals the number of non-terminal rows that name the pod in a state other than `resume_pending`, `resuming`, and `awaiting_client_action` (the I2 count). A row in one of those states names a failed pod and holds no slot.
- **I3 Credential leases.** `LeasesBySession` is empty for a terminal session and for a session in `created`, podless `suspended`, `resume_pending`, or `awaiting_client_action`. Every lease that an attempt minted in an incarnation that did not commit has been returned. Every lease that a live binding's `MintedLeaseIDs` names is still recorded.
- **I4 Coordination.** For a terminal or podless `suspended` session, no checker pass that starts after the session's last row write records an `Acquire` of its `REG-COORDLEASE` key. No unreleased `REG-COORDMIRROR` row exists for a terminal session, and none that names a surviving replica exists for a podless `suspended` session. At most one surviving replica binds each session.
- **I5 Replica records.** No surviving replica's pod registry binds a terminal row, and its resume-attempt records, terminal claims, delivery locks, and D6 goroutines are empty. After the last checker run of a cell, X2 run, or X2a sequence, the harness closes every replica and store client, and `goleak.VerifyNone` passes.
- **I6 Queues.** The DLQ and inbox are empty for a terminal session. For a `running` session, each held entry is one that the coordinating replica's last delivery of held messages did not consume, as SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** permits: an entry its read did not return, an entry enqueued after that read, or the entry whose delivery failed and every entry behind it.
- **I7 Client-visible effects.** Each message answered `delivered` or `queued` reaches the runtime exactly once, is still held in a `running` session as I6 permits, or, when the session ended first, has its sender receive exactly one `message_expired`. A landed commit whose **Post-commit tail.** terminal re-check reads a non-terminal row emits one `session.resumed` and one `children_reattached`, and one whose re-check reads a terminal row emits neither. A terminal session emits one `session_complete` and one terminal `status_change` when its writer's path runs `emitTerminalLifecycle` (the `funnel` path, or a `hook` path whose `signals` runs it, as D7 **Paths.** and the writer's **Terminal writer inventory.** row state), and emits neither otherwise. `RetryCount` never exceeds `maxRetries`.

**Invariant relaxations.** This register lists every recorded gap under which a cell may leave an invariant clause unmet. In a cell that a row names, the checker asserts the row's outcome in place of the clause the row relaxes, and asserts every other clause. Every other cell asserts the invariants in full. A gap that this proposal newly records as unfixed and that relaxes an invariant adds its row here. A claim is GC-selectable when it is `bound` and names a pod that no non-terminal row names and no surviving replica binds, which is the claim-orphan GC's selection. The checker does not tick that GC. A row's outcome is the cited Edge-cases bullet's, and the column states only what the checker asserts.

| Gap | Owner | Relaxes | Cells | Asserted outcome |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| Edge cases **Driver goroutine at shutdown.**, **Credential leases with no binding releaser.**, and **Terminal write without its hook.**, for a crash or a context end that takes the **Post-commit tail.** **Context ended** column | P1 | I1, I2, and I3, for the claim, slot, and leases that the crashed replica's or the ended attempt's binding held | X4 crash cells, and X4 context-cancel cells at a `completeResume` call that take the **Context ended** column | Each leftover claim is GC-selectable. A concurrent pod's `REG-SLOTCOUNT` exceeds its I2 count by at most the slots those bindings held. Each leftover lease belongs to a binding or attempt that the trace shows no releaser reached. |
| Edge cases **Held message delivered again after a coordinator change.** | R20 | I7, "reaches the runtime exactly once" | X4 cells whose fault index lies from a held entry's `Send` through its removal; X2 runs that schedule a takeover or lease lapse during a held-message delivery | The bullet's bound, with coordinator changes and injected faults counted from the trace. |
| Edge cases **Credential leases with no binding releaser.** | P1 | I3, for the leases of a binding or attempt that no releaser reached | X1 cells with placement none on a pre-state that names a pod; X3 Relinquished and Above `G` exits followed by a terminal write before the adopter publishes, and Not landed with a reply other than `RECLAIMED`; X4 peer-takeover cells whose readopt fails | Each leftover lease belongs to a binding or attempt that the trace shows no releaser reached. |
| Edge cases **Terminal row with no binding at its generation.** and **Uncommitted replacement pod disposition.** | P1 | I1 for an exclusive pod's claim, and I2 on a concurrent-workspace pod | The previous row's cells, and X4 store-error cells at a slot release | Each leftover claim is GC-selectable. The pod's `REG-SLOTCOUNT` exceeds its I2 count by at most its unreleased slots and is never below it. |
| Edge cases **Terminal binding reclamation latency.** (D6 **Accepted gaps.**, a release error after the unbind) | P1 | I1, for that binding's claim | X4 store-error and context-cancel cells at a `Binder.Release` call (its claim patch, Sandbox `Get`, or drain) reached from the post-terminal hook, D6, or a suspension release | The claim is GC-selectable. |
| Edge cases **Terminal write without its hook.** | P2 | I6, and I7 for that session's held messages and terminal events | X4 crash cells between a terminal write and its hook, and X4 store-error cells at a terminal write that applied | The session's held entries remain, their senders receive no `message_expired`, and the session emits no `session_complete` and no terminal `status_change`. |
| Edge cases **Held entry in flight at a terminal write.** and **Held-message outcomes that overlap.** | P2 | I7, the exactly-once and `message_expired` clause | X2 runs that order a terminal write against a held-message enqueue or delivery; X4 store-error cells at the held-message re-read | No message reaches the runtime twice, and no sender receives both a `delivered` receipt and a `message_expired` or `error` outcome for one message. |
| Edge cases **Message held by a non-coordinator replica during a delivery.** | R19 | I6 and I7, for that message | X2 sends to a peer replica, and X5 cells served by a peer, in which the serving replica buffers in its own inbox | The message is in the serving replica's inbox or has reached the runtime once. |
| Edge cases **Pre-running claim at a `none` terminal write.** | P1 | I1 for an exclusive pod's claim, and I2 on a concurrent-workspace pod | X1 cells of the `terminateUserSessions` row on a `created`, `finalizing`, or `ready` pre-state whose row names a claim that no registry holds; X2 runs and X2a steps that apply the `full_revoke` write to such a session | The claim is GC-selectable. The pod's `REG-SLOTCOUNT` exceeds its I2 count by at most that session's reservation. |

The checker runs after every X1, X3, X4, and X5 cell, after every X2 run, and after every X2a step. A scenario at tier 4, 5, or 8 that runs gateway processes asserts its own intent and runs no checker, because the checker steps the clock and calls each replica's loops in process.
