No other test is retrofitted. A failing cell follows **Failures the tests find.** above.

**Step placement.** An X1, X3, or X5 cell lands in the step that lands the last of the code it exercises: the writer, attempt exit, or edge its row names, and the code that produces its pre-state. An X4 cell lands with the scenario it re-runs. An X2 or X2a operation lands in the step that lands the last of its code, or in the harness's step when that is later, and a required pair lands in the step that lands the later of its two operations. The harness and the checker land in the first step that lands a cell. The checker's tick set at each step holds the loops of **Invariant checker.** that exist at that step. A later step that changes code that a landed cell, operation, or pair exercises updates it as the Testing preamble states for any test, and runs its tiers.

**Seeds and counts.** An X2 run takes its seed from outside the test, so a run can vary it, and logs the seed on failure. A failing run replays exactly from that seed alone. Each pull-request run schedules every required pair in both orders and every X2 operation at least once, within the tier-7a time budget. The nightly run varies the seed. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the seed source and the schedule count.

### TEST-0 · Tier-11 spec-text tests updated for SPEC-2 and SPEC-5

- **[T0.1]** **Intent:** The tier-11 register-writer check reads the inter-replica message link from the §28.5.4 sentence that names the `ForwardMessage` RPC, which SPEC-5v keeps, rather than from the §7.2 forwarding sentences that SPEC-5i removes. **Tier:** 11. **Spec:** §28.5.4 (Inter-replica), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[T0.2]** **Intent:** The tier-11 off-holder matrix check pins the SPEC-2d §29.3 events JSON row as served from the shared session-event relay `CH-EVENTRELAY`, applies its deferral-phrase check only to the part of the row before its relay-absence clause, and pins the preamble's forwarding list as the interrupt, terminate, delete, resume, interaction-resolution, and upload rows. **Tier:** 11. **Spec:** §29.3 (Interactive message send).

### Tests that land with CODE-1

- **[C1.1]** **Intent:** Active-time accrual adds the `starting`, `running`, and `input_required` intervals of a row's history and no others, a write that keeps the state at `running` accrues the interval since the previous write, and the pgstore column carries the same accrual. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxSessionAge timer behavior across states). **Discriminates:** an accrual that counts time spent in `suspended`, `resume_pending`, or `resuming`.
- **[C1.2]** **Intent:** The watchdog expires a session, and warns of its expiry, by active age rather than wall-clock age. A `suspended` row whose accumulated age is over the cap is not expired by `sweepMaxAge`. A listed row whose locked row is `suspended`, is terminal, or is at or under the cap is not expired, and an over-cap `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` row is expired as before (F-6.2.28, P3). **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxSessionAge timer behavior across states). **Discriminates:** a session older than the cap by wall clock and under it by active time surviving.
- **[C1.3]** **Intent:** A playground session's cap counts active time, so time in `suspended` does not expire it, and it expires once its active time passes the cap after it returns to `running`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §27.6 (Session lifecycle and cleanup), §6.2 (maxSessionAge timer behavior across states).
- **[C1.5]** **Intent:** Migration 0182 applies and rolls back per step, and its `CHECK` rejects a negative accumulated age. **Tier:** 2. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxSessionAge timer behavior across states).
- **[C1.6]** **Intent:** `sweepMaxAge` gives a listed over-cap row whose stored row is already terminal no expiry side effect and no `UpdatedAt` advance. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxSessionAge timer behavior across states).

### Tests that land with CODE-2

- **[C2.1]** **Intent:** A retry is spent only on the spend edges: a retryable failure followed by a successful resume leaves `RetryCount` at 1 and `RecoveryGeneration` advanced by one, a second failure under `maxRetries` 2 still enters `resume_pending`, and the retry metric and audit fire once per spend edge and never on resume success. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C2.2]** **Intent:** No runtime or pool store, admin handler, or API schema sets or expects `maxSessionRetries`, and a runtime create that carries the key is accepted without persisting it. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C2.3]** **Intent:** `ValidTransitions` holds every edge of the §7.2 **Session state machine:** block, including the CODE-2 item 5 edges, and `IsValid` admits each. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).

### Tests that land with CODE-3

- **[C3.1]** **Intent:** On each binder path (`Resume` exclusive, `Resume` slot, `Bind`, and `BindSlot`), the post-claim hook runs after the claim and any reservation and before `NegotiateVersion`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C3.2]** **Intent:** On each binder path, a hook error deletes the claim or releases the slot reservation exactly once, issues no compensation RPC and no session-wide credential release, and returns the hook's own error rather than a `*SlotBindError`, and on `Resume` slot a sibling slot reserved earlier keeps the claim and the counter at one. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C3.3]** **Intent:** A failure after the claim (a `NegotiateVersion` failure on every path including a nil-hook `Claim`, and an adapter `Resume` failure on an exclusive pool through `failResume`) deletes the claim or releases the reservation. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C3.4]** **Intent:** The release after a claim runs on a detached context (CODE-3 item 3 **Detached release.**), so a hook that cancels the request context before failing, a `Launch` failure after the request context ends, and an adapter `Resume` failure after the request context ends each still delete the claim or release the reservation. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume). **Discriminates:** a release issued on the request context.
- **[C3.5]** **Intent:** `Bind` releases only the leases its own `Prepare` minted when `Launch` fails after the hook, keeping a lease another attempt holds for the same session; a failed `Prepare` reconnect releases no lease; and a `Launch` on a request `Bind` did not build still releases the session. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §4.9 (Credential Leasing Service).
- **[C3.6]** **Intent:** `applySlotRetryPolicy` stops after one `BindSlot` call that returns an error other than `*SlotBindError` and releases nothing. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).

### Tests that land with CODE-4

A fixture that writes a terminal state marks the row terminated in its mutator, as every D7 terminal writer does.

Watchdog and primary reads:

- **[C4.1]** **Intent:** The watchdog calls the resume driver for every `resume_pending` row inside the window, and its window-expiry write applies only when the locked row is still `resume_pending` with `UpdatedAt` past the window; a superseded row keeps its `UpdatedAt` and gets no timeout count and no notification. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §7.3 (Resume driver).
- **[C4.2]** **Intent:** `sweepResuming` exits only the incarnation it listed, so a row re-entered at a higher `coordination_generation`, or already moved to `resume_pending` by a failure report, is left unchanged with no notification. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine). **Discriminates:** a sweep that spends a retry beyond `maxRetries`.
- **[C4.3]** **Intent:** `ReadPrimary` returns the stored row without writing it (no `UpdatedAt` or accumulated-age change, and `ErrNotFound` for a missing row and another tenant's row), returns `context.DeadlineExceeded` within its timeout, applies `DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout` to a zero timeout, honors an earlier caller deadline, and on pgstore does not block a concurrent `Update`. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §12.3 (Postgres HA Requirements).
- **[C4.4]** **Intent:** The `--session-primary-read-timeout` flag defaults to `sessionstore.DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout`, its environment variable overrides it, and a non-positive value fails startup. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §12.3 (Postgres HA Requirements).
- **[C4.5]** **Intent:** Each one-shot decision that D4 moves to the primary decides on the primary row when a lagging replica read differs: `handleResume` branches on the primary row, `attemptResume` writes its entry at the primary generation, `ReportSessionFailure` applies a retryable report to a row the replica still shows as `resuming`, `ReportAttachStreamFailure` drops a report superseded by a peer's takeover and evicts the binding, and `persistDeriveFailure` fences on an advanced source generation and records the `fenced` outcome. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §7.1 (Normal Flow). **Discriminates:** a replica read that drops the retryable report.
- **[C4.6]** **Intent:** A derive admitted from a `resume_pending` source whose row enters `resuming` before the failure write persists no row and counts `fenced`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.1 (Normal Flow).

Resume driver:

- **[C4.7]** **Intent:** `DriveResume` does nothing on a non-holder or on a lease read error, does not start a second attempt for a session already in flight on the replica, and starts no attempt for a row whose active age exceeds its cap, whether the watchdog or `ReattachNode` calls it. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver).
- **[C4.8]** **Intent:** A pre-claim failure writes nothing and sets the next-attempt time within `[now+backoff, now+1.5·backoff)`; `DriveResume` starts no attempt before that time and one at it; the window still elapses to `awaiting_client_action`; and the attempt's deadline is the remaining window before the claim and `MaxResumingSeconds` after the hook. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver).
- **[C4.9]** **Intent:** The hook write advances `coordination_generation` by exactly one, a `cancelled` write or a `RecordHandoff` bump after the item 3.1 read makes the hook return `errResumeSuperseded` and release the claim, and a slot retry after a post-hook failure invokes the hook again without `errResumeSuperseded`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver).
- **[C4.10]** **Intent:** Every exit of an attempt is conditioned on its entry generation `G`: a generation advance between the hook and the commit makes the commit release the pod and publish nothing; an attempt overtaken by a second attempt at `G+1` fails its commit while the second commits, including on a concurrent pool with both slots on one pod; an `AdvanceLastSeq` write does not fail the commit; and each commit, on the rebuild and the checkpoint branch, advances `recovery_generation` by exactly one. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver).
- **[C4.11]** **Intent:** A failure report on a `resuming` row writes only when its `ExpectedCoordinationGeneration` matches the row, so a report with the field unset or behind a bump writes nothing, a committed exit through `applyFailureFromResuming` leaves `coordination_generation` unchanged, a post-claim failure takes the `applyFailureFromResuming` exits with a setup `FailedPrecondition` landing in `awaiting_client_action` as `setup_command_failed`, and a stream failure on a binding the commit published at `G` is reported rather than dropped as superseded. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[C4.12]** **Intent:** A lost commit, on the checkpoint branch and on the rebuild branch with an exclusive and a concurrent pool, publishes no binding, writes no `PodAssignment`, tears down the adapter session, sends one reclaim naming the attempt's token, releases the claim or the slot with `leaked` taken from that reply, and releases exactly the `MintedLeaseIDs` the attempt minted with no session-wide release. After a store error, a reply other than `RECLAIMED` releases nothing, while a lease-store error after a landed commit still publishes the binding. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod). **Discriminates:** a release that revokes a lease another attempt minted for the session, as `rollbackBinding` does.
- **[C4.13]** **Intent:** An ambiguous commit is classified once: a commit `Update` that applies and then errors advances `RecoveryGeneration` exactly once and, on the binderless path, runs the **Landed** column once; one that errors without applying commits on the repeat; `ErrNotFound` ends the repeat after one try and runs **Not landed**; and a binderless guard miss with no store error on a row another commit already moved runs **Not landed**. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver). **Discriminates:** two `session.resumed` events across two binderless commits.
- **[C4.14]** **Intent:** `classifyCommit` returns the outcome of every row of the CODE-4 item 5.3 table, for the pod-binder and the binderless baselines. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver). **Discriminates:** a terminal `R` at `G` naming the result's sandbox with an advanced `RecoveryGeneration` read as other than Landed, the same `R` above `G` read as other than Above `G`, a pod-binder row above `G` read as Landed, a binderless row at the next recovery generation with no store error read as Landed, and a binderless row with an advanced `CoordinationGeneration` read as Above `G`.
- **[C4.15]** **Intent:** Each exit runs exactly the effects of its column of the CODE-4 item 5 **Post-commit tail.** table (Landed, Relinquished, Above `G`, Landed with `R` `suspended`, Landed with a failed re-check read, Not landed, and Context ended), and a hook store error makes the attempt call no binder step after the hook and record the next-attempt time. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §8.10 (Delegation Tree Recovery). **Discriminates:** a Landed exit reporting a `resumeMode` other than `partial_workspace`, which shows cleanup ran before classification, and a Not landed exit that deletes the partial manifest's chunk objects.
- **[C4.16]** **Intent:** An external drain of the pod between the hook and the restore RPC makes the attempt report `runtime_crash` at `G`, write `resume_pending` with `RetryCount + 1`, publish no binding, and release the missing claim without error. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §4.6.1 (Warm Pool Controller (Pod Lifecycle)).
- **[C4.17]** **Intent:** A deadline that fires after the commit, with `MaxResumingSeconds` shorter than a held message's turn, still lets the delegation-tree recovery traversal run and the turn complete, and the held entry is removed and delivered once. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §8.10 (Delegation Tree Recovery).
- **[C4.18]** **Intent:** `--resume-driver-backoff-seconds` defaults to 10 and reads `LENNY_RESUME_DRIVER_BACKOFF_SECONDS`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver).
- **[C4.19]** **Intent:** `completeResume` emits `session.resumed` before `children_reattached`, runs the delegation-tree recovery traversal, emits no `inbox_cleared`, and clears no inbox. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §8.10 (Delegation Tree Recovery), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C4.20]** **Intent:** Table-driven over every non-terminal node state × present in or absent from the local pod registry × traversal started by the root's, a sibling's, or a descendant's commit. Tree recovery selects exactly the `resume_pending` nodes and routes each through `DriveResume`, calls no binder, writes nothing itself, and calls `FailNode` for no unselected node. `ReattachNode` returns only after the node leaves `resume_pending` and `resuming`: nil for any state the committed restore can reach (`running`, `input_required`, `suspended`), and an error for `awaiting_client_action` or a terminal state. A selected node that a peer replica coordinates waits for that replica's driver. With two selected siblings at one depth whose restores each take more than half of `maxLevelRecoverySeconds`, both are driven before either wait and neither is failed. A wait that outlives its node's deadline returns at that deadline with the budget's reason, and no later node is failed because of it. A `ReadPrimary` error on a poll fails no node. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §8.10 (Delegation Tree Recovery). **Discriminates:** selection by registry absence, a node counted recovered before its restore's outcome, a restored node that left `running` before the poll counted as failed, a serial drive-and-wait per node, a read error failing a node, and a wait that no budget bounds.

`POST /resume` and the held-pod paths:

- **[C4.21]** **Intent:** `EndpointResume` admits `suspended` and `awaiting_client_action` and rejects every other state. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C4.22]** **Intent:** `POST /resume` is a store write: from `awaiting_client_action`, and from `suspended` without a held pod, it writes `resume_pending` with `RetryCount` unchanged and no retry metric, answers `200`, claims no pod, and runs no post-commit effect; from a held-pod `suspended` row on the lease holder it answers `200` with `running`, including with no binder wired; a row moved after the read answers `200` with `resume_pending` when it reached `awaiting_client_action` and 409 `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` when it reached `running`; and with no binder wired, with or without a lease store, the binderless resume reaches `running` with no claim. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C4.23]** **Intent:** A `POST /resume` on a held-pod `suspended` row served by a replica whose lease read names another replica, or fails, answers 409 `TARGET_NOT_READY` and leaves the row and its DLQ entries unchanged. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §29.3 (Interactive message send).
- **[C4.24]** **Intent:** A failure report on a `suspended` row whose pod was released (cleared after the report's read, or podless before it) is a no-op for every mutator outcome (retries left, retries exhausted, and non-retryable), while a report on a `suspended` row that still names the reported pod takes its edge and releases the binding once. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine). **Discriminates:** a guard that blocks the report on a row that still holds the pod; the case runs with a pod binder wired.
- **[C4.25]** **Intent:** A report racing a same-replica resume on an exclusive and on a slot pool always leaves the session holding exactly the leases the resume attempt assigned and no lease from before the failure. **Tier:** 7a. **Spec:** §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod), §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes).
- **[C4.26]** **Intent:** A pod loss on a checkpointed session restores the workspace on a fresh pod with no client call, and a stream failure that retires an exclusive pod is followed by a restore onto another warm pod within the resume window, with the retired pod and the session's end still observed as before. **Tier:** 5. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §28.5.1 (CH-ATTACH).
- **[C4.27]** **Intent:** A concurrent pool still drains at its stream-failure threshold when the driver restores reported sessions onto the pool's second warm pod before the drain completes. **Tier:** 5. **Spec:** §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes).

Held messages:

- **[C4.28]** **Intent:** `deliverBuffered` delivers held inbox and DLQ entries in `EnqueuedAt` order in both inbox modes, delivers a DLQ entry whose score has passed, writes nothing to either store, decodes a payload without a role as a `user` message with its ID and text, and stamps an entry's `from` as the sending agent when the entry names a sender. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C4.29]** **Intent:** `deliverBuffered` keeps delivery exactly-once and ordered under failure: a failed `Send` leaves that entry and later ones at the head for a later call; a caller cancelled mid-turn lets the turn complete and removes only that entry; a remove miss continues; an undecodable head is removed with one ERROR log; a missing messaging coordinator is a no-op; and a read error returns nil, removes nothing, and lets a path-2 message proceed. A read-pool row that reads terminal before an entry stops the loop with that entry in place, and a row read error continues the loop. A Relinquished or Above-`G` tail whose re-check reads terminal emits nothing and still closes its connection. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6). **Discriminates:** a held entry delivered twice after a caller cancellation.
- **[C4.30]** **Intent:** A post-agent-output REJECT on a held entry records one audit row, leaves no transcript entry or event, removes the entry, and lets later entries and a following path-2 message proceed; with the audit sink failing the entry stays at the head; and the REST caller still answers 403 for a REJECT. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §4.8 (Gateway Policy Engine).
- **[C4.31]** **Intent:** `holdMessage` removes its own entries and answers `TARGET_TERMINAL` when its primary re-read finds the session terminal (or `queued` when the entries were already drained), removes them and answers `error` when the re-read fails, and leaves them when the re-read finds `running`. A removal error answers `TARGET_TERMINAL` and drains the session once. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6). **Discriminates:** a stale `suspended` read that leaves the entries in the DLQ of a `cancelled` session.
- **[C4.32]** **Intent:** The DLQ peek-oldest and remove operations act on one entry, the inbox peek and acknowledge remove only acknowledged entries in both modes, and the durable migration to the DLQ leaves no TTL on the inbox key. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C4.33]** **Intent:** `POST /start` delivers messages held before `running` after its commit, in acceptance order, and a failed held `Send` still answers `200` with `running` and leaves the entry at the head for a later delivery. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C4.34]** **Intent:** `DeliverHeldAfterTakeover` delivers the held entries once for a `running` row at the passed generation, delivers nothing for another generation, an `input_required` row, a `suspended` row, or a primary read error, and returns at once when the delivery lock is held, delivering after its release. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol). **Discriminates:** a hook that blocks the Sweeper.
- **[C4.35]** **Intent:** The coordination options the gateway builds carry a non-nil `HeldDeliverer` and `TerminalReleaser` that reach `DeliverHeldAfterTakeover` and `ReleaseTerminalBinding` with the passed arguments, and a call before the session server is set does nothing and returns without blocking. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol), §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).

Credentials on resume:

- **[C4.36]** **Intent:** `Binder.Resume` runs the hook, `NegotiateVersion`, `AssignCredentials` keyed by every named provider, and `Resume` in that order under one bind-attempt token, returns the minted lease IDs, and sends no `AssignCredentials` when no provider is named. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod), §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract).
- **[C4.37]** **Intent:** A resume that fails at minting, at the adapter's `AssignCredentials`, or at the adapter's `Resume`, on an exclusive and a concurrent pool, releases exactly the leases it minted and no others, sends one compensating `Shutdown` under its token, returns a `*SlotBindError` at stage `resume` (with a `*CredentialAssignmentError` for a minting failure), and deletes the claim on an exclusive pool. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod). **Discriminates:** a session-wide lease release.
- **[C4.38]** **Intent:** The checkpoint branch resolves the credential pools and the user before the claim, and a resolution error or a credential-isolation rejection returns before the binder's `Resume`, writes nothing, and sets the next-attempt time. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod).
- **[C4.39]** **Intent:** The adapter admits a `Resume` into the entry an `AssignCredentials` under the same token created, so the session's credential file holds the lease at `session_start`, and refuses a `Resume` under another token with `SLOT_BIND_ATTEMPT_SUPERSEDED` and an `AssignCredentials` after `Resume` with `SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §6.1 (What a Pre-Warmed Pod Looks Like).
- **[C4.40]** **Intent:** A credential assignment rejected after the hook for a cause `isCredentialRejection` admits ends the row in `awaiting_client_action` with `credential_rejected` and `RetryCount` unchanged, and a transient cause (pool exhausted, token service unavailable, gRPC `Unavailable`, or a deadline) ends it in `resume_pending` with `RetryCount + 1` and `runtime_crash`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver; Credential leases on the replacement pod), §6.2 (Pod State Machine). **Discriminates:** a rejection that spends a retry.
- **[C4.41]** **Intent:** `credential_rejected` classifies non-retryable under the platform defaults, whose non-retryable list has three entries, unknown under a client non-retryable list that omits it, and retryable under a client retryable list that names it. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[C4.42]** **Intent:** Every lease records its coordination generation: `Bind`, `BindSlot`, and `Resume` pass the request's generation to minting, the user-credential mint stores it, a renewal or a fallback rotation keeps the spent lease's generation, and the pgstore lease row round-trips it. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod), §4.9 (Proactive Lease Renewal). **Discriminates:** a minted lease read back at generation 0.
- **[C4.43]** **Intent:** `ReleaseThrough` on `Service` and `Client` releases a session's leases at or below the generation and keeps later ones, including a lease at or below it recorded during the release, and the pod release (`Release` and `ReleaseSlot`) returns the binding's minted leases and the session's leases through its stamp with no session-wide release. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod).
- **[C4.44]** **Intent:** A released lease is never renewed: after a release, on the same or a second `Service` sharing the lease store, or by an assigner that releases the spent lease after `Assign`, a renewal tick past every `RenewBefore` leaves no lease for the session, the pool's active count at its post-release value, and nothing tracked or reported exhausted. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.9 (Proactive Lease Renewal). **Discriminates:** a renewal that recreates a released lease.
- **[C4.45]** **Intent:** Renewal error handling keeps its counts: with the breaker open, a direct-mode lease extended across an expired-lease sweep stays tracked and reaches the extension cap once; an assigner that removes the spent lease and returns token-service-unavailable leaves nothing tracked and sends no extension in direct and proxy mode; a lookup error before or after `Assign` keeps the spent lease tracked with one retry counted and restores the pool's active count; and a renewal with no pool binding fails with `credrenewal.ErrLeaseReleased`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.9 (Proactive Lease Renewal).
- **[C4.46]** **Intent:** The pgstore lease store's `Lookup` reports an absent lease as not found and an unreadable blob as an error, `Advance` of a removed lease writes nothing, and `Advance` of a present lease moves its expiry so that `DeleteExpired` at a cutoff between the two expiries keeps the row. **Tier:** 2. **Spec:** §4.9 (Proactive Lease Renewal).
- **[C4.47]** **Intent:** `ReleaseSession` on `Service` and `Client` releases a lease recorded for the session during the release, and `Client` revokes it. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.9 (Proactive Lease Renewal).

Terminal transition and binding release:

- **[C4.48]** **Intent:** `MarkTerminated` stamps `TerminatedAt` and the state's reason once, keeps the stamp on a later write, rewrites the reason on a relabel to `failed`, leaves a non-terminal row unchanged, and advances `CoordinationGeneration` by one for every terminal state written from `resuming` and for no other source state. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §8.8 (TaskRecord and TaskResult Schema).
- **[C4.49]** **Intent:** The set of functions under `pkg/` and `cmd/` that assign a terminal state equals the D7 **Terminal writer inventory.** table; every terminal assignment is followed by `MarkTerminated` in the same mutator (for `transitionTerminate`, in the `handleTransition` mutator); and the claim-and-hook function of every `funnel` and `hook` row calls `BeginTerminal` before its `Update` and its hook after it. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §8.8 (TaskRecord and TaskResult Schema). **Discriminates:** a new terminal writer added without a table row.
- **[C4.50]** **Intent:** A terminal writer whose snapshot reads `resume_pending` while the stored row is `resuming` (`DELETE`, `cascadeToChildren`, `lenny/cancel_child`, and `lenny/cancel_session`) makes exactly one `Update`, which writes the terminal state at the next generation, and leaves the generation unchanged on a `running` row. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C4.51]** **Intent:** The post-terminal hook runs cancel, seal, drain, the `signals` callback, the lease release, and the pod release in that order; each writer's `signals` emits `status_change` and `session_complete`, and runs the cascade or archives a settled child, before the release, with billing after it; the drain runs once; a seal timeout relabels the row `failed` before a `failed` release; and a session held by a D6 release delays the seal until that release exits, and a context end during the wait skips the seal and the release and still runs the drain and `signals`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.1 (Normal Flow), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §9.2 (Elicitation Chain).
- **[C4.52]** **Intent:** A terminal write from a pre-running state with no local binding reclaims the claim by name exactly when a claim exists: the finalize failure paths that prepared a claim delete it once, those that did not delete none, a store failure on `failFinalizing` still deletes a prepared claim once and runs no hook, and a cascade over a child the locked row shows `running` deletes no claim by name. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.1 (Normal Flow), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C4.53]** **Intent:** `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases a bound terminal session with the disposition of its state, waits for `slotAccountLocks`, evicts a binding stamped below the row's generation or at 0 without a binder call, does nothing for an unbound session, a non-terminal row, or an open terminal claim, starts one release for two calls, releases only leases at or below the stamp and the binding's minted leases, and while a writer's hook is sealing or signalling releases nothing, leaving the hook's release as the only one after a `sealed` snapshot. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior), §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod). **Discriminates:** a release taken before the slot-accounting lock, and a release that runs before the hook's seal and export.
- **[C4.54]** **Intent:** Every terminal writer ends its terminal claim on every path that runs no hook (a `handleDelete` write that applies and then errors, `transitionToFailed` on `errReportConflict`, an `expireSession` store error, the watchdog pre-running sweep on an `Update` error, `sweepMaxAge` on an already terminal row, `failOrphan` on a stored terminal row, an idempotent `ForceTerminate`, and, from CODE-6 in S15, a `handleDelete` or `handleTransition` (`POST /terminate`) that `session.Validate` refuses on the locked row), so a later `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases the binding. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C4.55]** **Intent:** Every binding publish site is reaped after a racing terminal write: a terminal write after the `completeResume` re-check (each branch), between the row commit and the publish in `handleCreateAndStart`, and in `MaterializeDelegatedChild` leaves a binding that `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases with the terminal disposition. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §7.3 (Resume driver), §8.2 (Delegation Mechanism).
- **[C4.56]** **Intent:** The commits of `POST /start` and of a delegated child's running write are guarded: a peer's `cancelled` write before the `POST /start` commit answers the precondition error and leaves the row `cancelled` for the reaper, a racing second `POST /start` that committed first answers the precondition error with `running` and runs no status event or held delivery, and a child cancelled between its bind and its running commit returns an error with the row `cancelled`, no registry binding, and the binder's rollback release. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §8.2 (Delegation Mechanism). **Discriminates:** an unguarded commit that overwrites the terminal row or answers `200`.
- **[C4.57]** **Intent:** A terminal write on a `resuming` row through every `funnel` and `hook` path of the D7 inventory cancels a same-replica resume attempt's context by the time the writer returns (or the descendant's claim ends, for a cascade), and the attempt then exits with no commit, no published binding, its pod released, a `reported` exit, and empty attempt and claim records; a write on `resume_pending` ends the claim uncounted; `CancelResumeAttempt` does nothing with no attempt in flight and leaves a landed commit's tail whole; a cancel during `releaseEarlierBinding` still completes that release; and a `DELETE` during a post-commit fence emits no `session.resumed` and leaves no binding or minted lease. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §7.3 (Resume driver). **Discriminates:** the same-replica re-check row of D6, through the post-commit `DELETE` case.
- **[C4.58]** **Intent:** A terminal writer racing `DriveResume` on a `resume_pending` row never leaves a claim after the attempt exits, because the attempt either reads the terminal row before claiming or has its claim context cancelled. **Tier:** 7a. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §7.3 (Resume driver). **Discriminates:** an attempt that registers its cancel function after its item 3.1 read.
- **[C4.59]** **Intent:** `lenny/cancel_session` and `lenny/cancel_child` take each session's terminal claim before the `Update`, run one post-terminal hook per cancelled session with `fromState` from the locked row, and end the claim, with only the mark when no hook is wired; `terminateUserSessions` cancels each session's same-replica attempt and drains it once right after the write that cancelled it, with and without a pod registry wired, and the fan-out neither cancels nor drains. `lenny/cancel_session` completes its hook after its request context is cancelled and answers the state of the row the hook returned, `failed` after a seal relabel. `lenny/cancel_child` archives each node with the state its hook returned. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §11.4 (User invalidation).
- **[C4.60]** **Intent:** A partial `full_revoke` whose second cancel fails answers 500 and has cancelled the attempt of and drained only the sessions already cancelled, and runs the fan-out for those IDs only. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §11.4 (User invalidation).
- **[C4.61]** **Intent:** The production wiring connects every terminal writer's seam (the MCP tools, the watchdog, the orphan sweeps, `ForceTerminate`, and the budget terminator) to the session server's terminal claim and hook, and connects `terminateUserSessions` to the session server's `AfterRevokeWrite`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §11.4 (User invalidation). **Discriminates:** production writers that skip the hook while every other test injects a recorder.
- **[C4.62]** **Intent:** `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` records exactly the outcome that each row of the CODE-4 item 25 table assigns, with the row's pool, and a nil recorder still completes the commit. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §16.1 (Metrics), §7.3 (Resume driver). **Discriminates:** a relinquished landed commit counted other than one `success`, an applied hook store error counted at all, a shutdown after the hook counted or reported while the attempt-bound expiry counts one `failure`, and a setup failure or a commit that misses after the watchdog ended the incarnation counted other than one `failure`.
- **[C4.63]** **Intent:** `FenceAt` fences at its generation and relinquishes on a stale rejection without retrying at a higher re-read value, and `fenceResumedPod` passes `G` on both branches, so a binding's stamp equals the generation its pod accepted. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol), §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior).
- **[C4.64]** **Intent:** The callers under `pkg/` and `cmd/` of `CredentialAssigner.Release`, `ReleaseSession`, and `ReleaseThrough`, and of `releasePodCredentials` and `releaseAttemptCredentials`, are exactly the releasers that Design D6 **Credential lease return.** names, including `credRenewalWiring.Renew`'s release of its replacement and the Token Service's `RotateCredentials`, together with `revokeFinalizeLease` as its recorded departure. The assigners' internal calls, including the CODE-4 item 24.7 error returns, are excluded. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.1 (Normal Flow), §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod). **Discriminates:** a terminal writer, sweep, or handler that returns leases by session ID for a binding it does not hold.
- **[C4.65]** **Intent:** `AssignProto` on `Service` and `Client`, and `MintProto`, leave no recorded lease, no counted pool slot, and no renewal-tracked lease when the wire conversion fails. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.9 (Credential Leasing Service).
- **[C4.66]** **Intent:** On a recycling pool, `Binder.Release` of a binding marked crashed sends a plain `Shutdown`, deletes the claim, and neither patches the claim `recycling` nor arms the recycle boundary, for the dispositions `completed`, `cancelled`, `expired`, and empty. An unmarked binding with each of those dispositions still recycles. A marked slot binding's `ReleaseSlot` behaves as an unmarked one does. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes). **Discriminates:** a crashed pod returned to a recycling pool after a clean terminal.
- **[C4.67]** **Intent:** `endConn` marks the conn's binding crashed on a `DeadlineExceeded` or `Internal` end before the stream-failure handler runs, including with no handler wired. It does not mark on an `EvictStream` end, on `io.EOF`, or on `Unavailable`, `FailedPrecondition`, or `InvalidArgument`. A `PodExecutor.Release` whose unbind follows `endConn`'s unlock of `e.mu` reads the mark and retires the pod. An `Internal` end that `endConn` handles after the conn's eviction sets no mark, and a `PodExecutor.Release` whose unbind precedes it recycles the pod on a recycling pool. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.1 (Gateway-to-pod), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).

Expansive tables (Testing **Expansive lifecycle tests**):

- **[X1]** **Intent:** The cells are every row of the D7 **Terminal writer inventory.** (the table the C4.49 test holds) × every non-terminal state of `pkg/api/v1/session` (`created`, `finalizing`, `ready`, `starting`, `running`, `input_required`, held-pod and podless `suspended`, `resume_pending`, `resuming` with an attempt in flight, and `awaiting_client_action`) × binding placement (this replica, a peer replica, and none; `resuming` takes only this replica, the replica that runs the attempt) × pool kind (exclusive, exclusive recycling, and concurrent). Each cell reaches its pre-state through the production calls that produce it, never by writing the row, and a combination that no production path reaches is not a cell. Each writer runs through its production caller with that caller's collaborators wired; the `terminateUserSessions` row runs through `handleInvalidateUser` in `full_revoke` mode. In each cell the writer writes the terminal state exactly when its own guard, as its D7 inventory row and its CODE item state, admits the cell's pre-state, and otherwise writes nothing. A cell that writes runs the effects of its `funnel`, `hook`, or `none` path, and every cell leaves a state the invariant checker passes. **Cost floor.** Every writer row on every pre-state, and every placement and pool kind. **Tier:** 1, 2 (pgstore and Redis). **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §8.8 (TaskRecord and TaskResult Schema), §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior). **Discriminates:** a writer and pre-state pair that leaks a binding, lease, or claim that no single-path intent above names.
- **[X3]** **Intent:** The cells are every exit of an attempt × pool kind (exclusive, exclusive recycling, and concurrent) × branch (checkpoint and rebuild) × binder (wired and binderless). The exits are every `DriveResume` refusal of CODE-4 item 2, every row of the CODE-4 item 25 table, and every column of the item 5 **Post-commit tail.** table, with **Landed** split by `R` `suspended` and by a failed re-check read, and **Not landed** split by a `RECLAIMED` and any other reclaim reply. Each cell runs exactly its column's effects and its item 25 counter outcome, and leaves a state the invariant checker passes. **Cost floor.** Every exit, and every pool kind, branch, and binder value. **Tier:** 1, 2 (pgstore and Redis). **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).

### Tests that land with CODE-5

- **[C5.1]** **Intent:** The Sweeper adopts each state CODE-5 item 1 admits once its lease lapses and no other non-terminal state, never adopts a never-bound session, bumps the generation once without readopting a `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` row that names the failed pod, and re-fences a held-pod `suspended` row. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[C5.2]** **Intent:** A podless `suspended` session holds no coordination lease: the sweep makes no `Acquire` and releases the mirror row whether its connection is live or dead, writes no mirror row and adopts nothing once the lease lapses, and adopts the row with one generation bump and no readopt after it moves to `resume_pending`; and the dead-connection branch releases a bound `resume_pending` session's lease so a later sweep adopts it the same way. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[C5.3]** **Intent:** `readoptAndFence` publishes the binding the pool resolves (a slot binding keyed by the session ID on a concurrent pool, and a recycle binding with no slot on an exclusive recycling pool), and a resolve error publishes nothing and releases the lease. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol). **Discriminates:** a concurrent-pool readopt that publishes no slot ID.
- **[C5.4]** **Intent:** `BindingGeneration` returns 0 for an unbound session or a nil registry and the stamp of the current binding otherwise, following a re-put. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior).
- **[C5.5]** **Intent:** The peer-takeover check compares the row with the binding's stamp, so a session released and rebound on the same replica at a new generation keeps its binding under a foreign lease at that generation, and a row advanced past the stamp evicts it. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior).
- **[C5.6]** **Intent:** `RecordHandoff` re-checks the locked row: it returns 0, leaves the row, releases the lease, and writes no mirror row when the stored generation has advanced past the listing; it makes no readopt when the stored row is no longer the listed `running` row; it bumps nothing and releases the lease when a listed held-pod row is podless; and a takeover's mirror row carries the `RecordHandoff` generation on both branches. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol).
- **[C5.7]** **Intent:** The tier-11 BUILD-GAPS probe for F-10.1.26 reports the gap while the adoption predicate does not admit `starting`. **Tier:** 11. **Spec:** §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[C5.8]** **Intent:** The Sweeper calls the takeover delivery hook once, after the publish, with the `RecordHandoff` generation, makes no call on a readopt failure, a zero `RecordHandoff`, a takeover that holds no pod, or a renew, and skips a nil hook. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol).
- **[C5.9]** **Intent:** The Sweeper hands each bound terminal row to the terminal releaser once, hands it no unbound terminal row and no bound non-terminal row, skips a nil hook, and keeps processing and renewing later rows. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[C5.10]** **Intent:** A readopt that publishes a binding for a listed `running` or held-pod `suspended` row that turned terminal between `RecordHandoff` and the publish is reaped by the next sweep's terminal releaser. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).

### Tests that land with CODE-6

Suspended-session lifetime and the watchdog:

- **[C6.1]** **Intent:** `sweepSuspended` expires a row at `MaxSuspendedSessionSeconds` and not one second before, with `expired:idle` and `ExpiryReasonMaxIdleTime`, counted as `max_idle_time`, and calls the terminal and expiry hooks once each; it writes nothing and calls no hook when the stored row is no longer the listed suspended row; it releases an interrupt row's pod only after the hold and never releases a podless row; and `sweepIdle` calls the idle hook only for `running`. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §16.1.1 (Attribute Naming).
- **[C6.2]** **Intent:** `sweepSuspended` runs no expiry hook, no backlog resume, and no release for a row whose lifetime write returned a store error, returns no error from `Tick`, and expires the row on a later tick when the write did not apply. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[C6.3]** **Intent:** A podless `suspended` row gets only the lifetime check (no backlog check and no release before it is due), while a row whose pod-holding check answers true with an empty `PodAssignment`, as on a gateway with no pod binder, reaches the backlog check. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[C6.4]** **Intent:** With no policy set, `EffectiveMaxIdleSeconds` returns 0 and the idle firing and the suspended-session expiry each fire at the CODE-6 item 9 default and not one second before; the idle sweep runs only for `running`; and a playground idle stamp under the playground cap ignores the runtime's session-age cap. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §27.6 (Session lifecycle and cleanup).
- **[C6.5]** **Intent:** An over-idle `running` row that cannot be idle-suspended (a service-mode session or a `type: mcp` runtime, as `IdleSuspendable` answers) is written `expired` with `expired:idle` and counted `max_idle_time` without reaching the suspender, a suspendable row reaches the suspender and is not written, and a resolver error skips the row. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §11.3 (Timeouts and Cancellation), §5.1 (Runtime), §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes).
- **[C6.6]** **Intent:** The gateway wires the idle suspender only when a pod binder is wired, so a binderless gateway expires an idle session that would otherwise be suspended (Decision 30). **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states).
- **[C6.7]** **Intent:** The DLQ expiry sweep covers `resuming` rows and skips `suspended` rows, and for a `resume_pending` row it expires an entry only once `UpdatedAt + DefaultDLQTTL` has passed. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model). **Discriminates:** an entry expired by its score while its row's `UpdatedAt` is inside the TTL.

Idle suspension:

- **[C6.8]** **Intent:** `classifyIdleWrite` returns the outcome CODE-6 item 4 step 3 assigns to each combination of generation, state, pod, and activity (Apply, Landed, Lift, Stale, and Stop). **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination). **Discriminates:** a row at a later generation classified as anything other than Stale.
- **[C6.9]** **Intent:** `SuspendIdle` seals before it writes: a not-sealed answer, an `activity_untapped:` refusal, an error, or a seal call past its 5 s deadline leaves the row `running` with no checkpoint and no write; a guard miss or a checkpoint failure unseals once and leaves the row `running` with its pod, and a checkpoint failure also emits one `session.suspension_checkpoint_failed`, increments its counter, and suppresses the next seal for 60 s. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §4.4.3 (Checkpoint Quiescence, Timeout, and Abort Handling), §16.1 (Metrics).
- **[C6.10]** **Intent:** `SuspendIdle` writes nothing when the replica does not hold the session (with a pod binder wired), when the binding has no adapter, or when activity moved after the listing (unsealing once), and after a generation advance past the checkpoint it makes no unseal, no executor release, and no release event. It seals, checkpoints, and writes nothing while the replica holds an open terminal claim for the session, and it releases nothing at step 4 when such a claim opened during the checkpoint or the step-3 re-read reads a terminal row. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[C6.11]** **Intent:** `SuspendIdle` decides each try of an ambiguous suspend write with `classifyIdleWrite`: a write that applied and then errored runs the release without unsealing and keeps refusing messages with `session_sealed`; a repeat that finds the row `running` on another pod, or `suspended` and podless at a higher generation, makes no unseal and no release; a write that did not apply, followed by moved activity or an interrupt, unseals once and writes nothing; a store that keeps failing until the context ends makes no unseal; and `ErrNotFound` is tried once with no unseal and no release. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[C6.12]** **Intent:** A successful `SuspendIdle` returns before its work runs, runs at most one suspension per session at a time, and records the seal, the checkpoint, one `suspended` write with `Idle` and no pod, the executor release, and one `session.pod_released_during_suspension` in that order with no unseal, leaving the row in `resume_pending` when an inbox entry was present at the write. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §16.1 (Metrics).
- **[C6.13]** **Intent:** A seal that cannot be lifted after a guard miss leaves the row `running` and sealed, a following REST message is held in the DLQ and answered `queued`, and the next `SuspendIdle` completes the suspension and leaves the row `resume_pending`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.14]** **Intent:** A seal answer moves the gateway idle clock: not sealed with an idle age sets `LastAgentActivityAt` to now minus that age, a refusal or an error sets it to now, neither checkpoints nor unseals, and the seal receives the idle seconds `sweepIdle` passed; and `StampAt` only advances the stored time and persists without coalescing. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states).
- **[C6.15]** **Intent:** `resumeHeldPod` and the `completeResume` commit set `LastAgentActivityAt` to now on entry to `running`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states).

Suspension release:

- **[C6.16]** **Intent:** `ReleaseSuspendedPod` runs off the caller with one checkpoint per session at a time under a 60 s deadline, clears `PodAssignment` before the release, keeps the pod and retries after 60 s on a checkpoint failure, and emits `session.suspension_checkpoint_failed` at the deadline when a concurrent checkpoint holds the flight lock; and an interrupt suspension's release makes no seal call. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §4.4.3 (Checkpoint Quiescence, Timeout, and Abort Handling).
- **[C6.17]** **Intent:** `ReleaseSuspendedPod` releases nothing when the row moved during the checkpoint (to `running`, or to `running` and back to `suspended` with a new `SuspendedAt`), and starts nothing when the replica does not hold the lease or the lease read fails, or while it holds an open terminal claim for the session. It releases nothing at step 4 when such a claim opened during the checkpoint or the step-3 re-read reads a terminal row. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[C6.18]** **Intent:** A successful release emits one `session.pod_released_during_suspension` and increments its counter with the §16.1 `pool` and `tenant_id` labels; a checkpoint failure increments only the checkpoint-failure counter; an executor release error clears `PodAssignment` and emits nothing; and a lock wait the context ends releases nothing and emits nothing. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §16.1 (Metrics).
- **[C6.19]** **Intent:** The release checks the backlog after it clears the pod: an entry accepted after `SuspendedAt` moves the row to `resume_pending`, no entry leaves it podless `suspended`, a backlog read error still releases the pod, an interrupt row's earlier inbox entry moves to the DLQ, a failed inbox migration records its drain failure under `suspended` with the release completing (and, through `enterResumePending`, under `resume_pending` with the row left `resume_pending`), and a terminal write after the clear leaves the DLQ empty and sends the sender `message_expired` with `target_terminated` (keeping the entry when the primary read fails). **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §16.1 (Metrics).
- **[C6.20]** **Intent:** A stale replica's pod release after a peer adopted and restored the session releases only the leases of its own pod and keeps the leases the peer assigned. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior), §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod).
- **[C6.21]** **Intent:** With a pod binder wired, the suspension release, the idle suspension, and the backlog resume make no lease read and no held-length read for a session with no local binding, and one lease read and no held-length read for a bound session whose lease another replica holds (D2). **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.22]** **Intent:** `failOrphan` leaves a listed `suspended` row whose stored row became podless unchanged with no hook call, does not count or hook a row another replica already failed, and fails a row that still names the terminated pod. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §10.1.4 (Coordinator-Loss Detection and Hold State).
- **[C6.23]** **Intent:** The checkpoint freshness gauge counts a `suspended` row only while it names a pod. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.4 (Event / Checkpoint Store).

Message routing for `suspended` sessions:

- **[C6.24]** **Intent:** `messagerouting.Classify` returns resume-and-deliver for a `suspended` target without `immediate` and routes a `resuming` target to the DLQ, and `resumeHeldPod` refuses a podless `suspended` row when a binder is wired and admits it when none is wired. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.25]** **Intent:** On a held-pod `suspended` target, path 6 delivers held DLQ entries in acceptance order before the triggering message (and a held-pod `POST /resume` delivers them), and a `resumeHeldPod` error inside `resumeHeldAndDrain` holds the message in the DLQ with the inbox empty, the row `suspended`, and the answer `queued`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.26]** **Intent:** On a podless `suspended` REST target, the enqueue precedes the `resume_pending` write, and for every `holdMessage` result (CODE-4 item 7) the answer and whether `enterResumePending` writes match CODE-6 item 7's REST bullet. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.27]** **Intent:** When the pod is released between the message's read and `resumeHeldPod`, the message lands in the DLQ, the row moves to `resume_pending`, and the answer is `queued`, and a `POST /resume` in the same race answers `200` with `resume_pending`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.28]** **Intent:** `ResumeSuspended` returns the error, leaves the row state, and leaves the DLQ head that each branch of CODE-6 item 7's `ResumeSuspended` bullet and CODE-4 item 8's `errHeldDeliveryFailed` case state, including the pod-released race. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.29]** **Intent:** A held message on REST and MCP always attempts the guarded `resume_pending` write: off the holder of a held-pod row the guard misses with `queued` and no change, a row made podless between the read and the write reaches `resume_pending`, and a failed write still answers `queued` on REST with the row `suspended` and the entry held. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.30]** **Intent:** A batch the adapter refuses as sealed (`*executor.SealedError` with `Delivered` 0), on REST and MCP, is routed to the DLQ through path 6 and never answers `EXECUTOR_FAILURE`; a second refusal after the row reads `running` again is still routed once to the DLQ with `queued`; and a partial refusal records the delivered messages and holds the rest in acceptance order. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[C6.31]** **Intent:** When the update after a sealed refusal fails, a successful `holdMessage` re-read on a podless `suspended` row holds the batch with `queued` and reaches `resume_pending`, and a failed re-read leaves the DLQ empty and answers `error` with `inbox_unavailable`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.32]** **Intent:** A resumed `Send` that fails holds the message in the DLQ with the inbox empty: on a fresh `suspended` row, on REST path 6 when the following primary read fails and the re-read finds `running`, and after a sealed refusal on a stored `suspended` row with one `Send` and one held-pod resume. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.33]** **Intent:** Held entries are delivered before each new path-2 message on REST and MCP: a held `Send` error answers as the new message's `EXECUTOR_FAILURE`; a sealed refusal of a held or new message leaves the refused held entry at the head and routes the new message once from a primary re-read; and on REST a held-pod synchronous resume whose held `Send` fails after the commit leaves the row `running`, holds the new message behind the failed entry with `queued`, and answers a `POST /resume` with `200` and `running`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.34]** **Intent:** `HeldLen` counts the DLQ alone, adds the inbox when asked, and counts only DLQ entries accepted after a given time when one is passed. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.35]** **Intent:** `lenny/send_message` to a `suspended` target holds the message, releases the delivery lock, and then calls `ResumeSuspended` with the classified row, with no inbox enqueue and no executor send; it answers `queued` whether the resume succeeds or fails; for every `HoldMessage` result its answer and its `ResumeSuspended` call match CODE-6 item 7's MCP bullet; and the held payload decodes as a session-server message payload with the message ID, role `user`, and the content, for string and part-array input. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §15.4 (Runtime Adapter Specification). **Discriminates:** a held payload in a format `deliverBuffered` cannot decode.
- **[C6.36]** **Intent:** `lenny/send_message` runs the pre-message policy before it holds or enqueues, so a REJECT, a cooldown refusal, or an oversize body answers its tool error with nothing held or enqueued, and a MODIFY holds or enqueues the modified body; and with no delivery dependency wired, a message to a `suspended` target answers `error` with `inbox_unavailable` and sends and enqueues nothing. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §4.8 (Gateway Policy Engine), §15.4 (Runtime Adapter Specification).
- **[C6.37]** **Intent:** `lenny/interrupt_session` dispatches `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/interrupt`, is registered as a client tool, and is absent when no session service is wired. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §15.2.1 (REST/MCP Consistency Contract).
- **[C6.38]** **Intent:** `lenny/delegate_task` sends the task input, then takes the delivery lock and delivers the child's held messages with the child row read from the primary after the task input; a delivery error leaves the tool result a success; a task with no inline text still delivers held messages; a terminal child, a failed or missing primary read, or a missing delivery or materializer dependency delivers nothing and still succeeds. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §8.2 (Delegation Mechanism).
- **[C6.39]** **Intent:** Message-path decisions use the caller's or the primary's row as D5 states: `ResumeSuspended` and `DeliverHeld` act on the row passed without re-reading; a message whose snapshot is podless `suspended` and whose primary row is `running` is delivered on path 2 on REST and MCP; a classification read failure falls back to the entry row and delivers held then new messages; a row deleted after the entry read answers `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` with nothing delivered or held; and a resumed send failure on a row now `cancelled` answers `TARGET_TERMINAL` with nothing enqueued. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6). **Discriminates:** a snapshot-classified message held in the DLQ of a `running` session.
- **[C6.40]** **Intent:** `POST /interrupt` on a row the primary shows as `resume_pending`, `resuming`, or podless `Idle` `suspended` answers 409 `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` and leaves the row, emits no status event, and re-arms no budget key, while on a `running` row it writes `suspended`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.41]** **Intent:** `POST /terminate` and `DELETE` re-validate on the locked row: a terminate on a row that became `resuming` answers the §15.1 precondition error with no hook, and on `resume_pending` completes with `fromState` `resume_pending`; a delete on a row that became `resuming` writes `cancelled` with a generation bump and `fromState` `resuming`; and a delete on a row that became `expired` answers the precondition error with no hook. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §15.1 (REST API).

Backlog resume:

- **[C6.42]** **Intent:** On an interrupt suspension, the backlog check starts a resume and skips the release when the DLQ holds an entry accepted after `SuspendedAt`, and keeps the pod until the hold ends when only the inbox or earlier DLQ entries hold messages; a held-length error keeps the pod and still evaluates later rows. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.43]** **Intent:** A backlog resume runs off the watchdog tick, so `Tick` returns while the delivery's `Send` blocks, a second tick starts no second resume for the session, and the held message is delivered once. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.44]** **Intent:** On a replica that does not hold the lease of a bound session, or whose lease read fails, the backlog check returns false without reading the held length. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.45]** **Intent:** The leader's backlog re-check moves a podless `suspended` row to `resume_pending` when it holds an inbox entry (for `Idle`) or a DLQ entry accepted after `SuspendedAt`, leaves other rows and rows that hold a pod by the write, continues past a row's held-length error and a tenant's list error, runs once before its first interval, and pages through each tenant by `id` with bounded concurrency. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C6.46]** **Intent:** The backlog re-check job runs only while the replica is leader, is not registered without a pod binder or messaging, and its flag rejects a value below 30. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §12.5 (Artifact Store).
- **[C6.47]** **Intent:** The podless-suspended listing returns only the tenant's `suspended` rows with no pod after the given `id`, at most the limit, in `id` order, served by `idx_sessions_podless_suspended` with no sort, and migration 0183 creates the index and its down file drops it. **Tier:** 2. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).

Delegation budget keys and erasure:

- **[C6.48]** **Intent:** The tree-budget Reserver arms created keys with the configured TTL, and `RearmTree` sets the TTL of the root's tree-wide, high-watermark, grant, and named parents' keys without creating absent keys or touching another root's keys. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §8.3 (Delegation Policy and Lease).
- **[C6.49]** **Intent:** Every CODE-6 item 14 re-arm site re-arms once after its commit with the distinct parents its child rows name, and not on a guard miss; a tree with no delegating parent calls no rearmer; a derived session re-arms its source root's keys; a rearmer error leaves the row in its new state; and startup refuses a budget-key TTL that does not exceed the suspended-session lifetime. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §8.3 (Delegation Policy and Lease). **Discriminates:** a re-arm that scans the keyspace when no parent is named.
- **[C6.50]** **Intent:** The gateway's rearmer constructor returns an untyped nil for a nil Reserver and re-arms a `{root}:dlg:*` key otherwise. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §8.3 (Delegation Policy and Lease). **Discriminates:** a typed nil that the server treats as wired.
- **[C6.51]** **Intent:** `PurgeRoot` on a Redis cluster deletes every `{root}:dlg:*` key and no other key, scanning only the node that owns the `{root}` hash slot. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §8.3 (Delegation Policy and Lease), §12.8 (Compliance Interfaces).
- **[C6.52]** **Intent:** Session erasure deletes the session's DLQ and durable inbox in durable mode and clears them in non-durable mode, leaves other sessions' and tenants' keys, returns the count and 0 on a repeat, wraps a server error, runs in the canonical erasure order, and is registered exactly when messaging is wired. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §12.8 (Compliance Interfaces).

Elicitation expiry:

- **[C6.53]** **Intent:** `ExpireRaisedBy` deletes only the pending elicitations the session's pod raised in the tenant, keeping one it resolves for a child, a responded one, a lease-extension elicitation, a tool-use row, and another tenant's row, after which `Resolve` of the deleted row returns `ErrNotFound`, on memory and pgstore. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain), §8.2 (Delegation Mechanism).
- **[C6.54]** **Intent:** The pgstore delete uses `idx_interactions_pending_elicitation_origin` on a tenant with many resolved elicitations, and migration 0185 creates the index and its down file drops it. **Tier:** 2. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain).
- **[C6.55]** **Intent:** Every failure edge out of `running` and `resuming`, and the post-terminal hook as its last step, expires the session's raised elicitations; a no-op report expires none; and an `ExpireRaisedBy` error is logged at WARN without changing the disposition or stopping the hook from returning. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain), §7.3 (Retry and Resume), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[C6.56]** **Intent:** The suspension release expires raised elicitations after its pod release, including when the release helper skips, and including one raised after the clear; a checkpoint failure, a guard miss, or a lock wait the context ends leaves it answerable; and `SuspendIdle` expires them on Apply and Landed and keeps them on Lift, Stale, and Stop. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[C6.57]** **Intent:** A runtime waiting on `lenny/request_elicitation` gets an error within two poll intervals of the row's expiry, with the pending gauge decremented once and no `ELICITATION_TIMEOUT`, and a respond or dismiss of the expired elicitation answers `ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` on MCP and `404 ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` on REST. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain), §15.1 (REST API).
- **[C6.82]** **Intent:** `lenny/request_elicitation` refuses a raise when the primary row is terminal while the read pool still reads it non-terminal, records nothing, and answers `errSessionLookup` on a primary read error. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain).

Adapter activity clock and session seal:

- **[C6.58]** **Intent:** Every row A1 through A5 of the CODE-6 item 18.1 activity inventory stamps the entry for each event in its counts column and stamps no entry for any event in its does-not-count column, including co-tenant isolation on a two-slot server. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK, CH-RUNTIMEOPS).
- **[C6.59]** **Intent:** `SealSession` seals exactly when the entry's last activity is at least the requested window old, answers not sealed with the idle age otherwise, keeps an existing seal when newer activity arrives, seals each co-tenant independently, seals one window after the last frame whatever the heartbeat interval, refuses an entry without `session_start` with `FailedPrecondition` and a zero window with `InvalidArgument`, and starts a fresh window at each `session_start` write, including on `Resume`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[C6.60]** **Intent:** Bare `busy: true` acks with no `sessionId` keep no session unsealed, so a session seals one window after its last traffic with or without heartbeats, and a tapped `response` moves the seal. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK), §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states). **Discriminates:** unattributed `busy` treated as activity.
- **[C6.61]** **Intent:** A sealed session refuses every `message` write (on `Attach`'s first frame, its receive loop, and `SendMessage`) with `FailedPrecondition` `session_sealed:` and no activity stamp, still writes `tool_result`, `heartbeat`, and `session_end`, leaves a co-tenant unaffected, and is unsealed by `UnsealSession` or by the entry's removal and recreation. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.1 (CH-ATTACH), §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract).
- **[C6.62]** **Intent:** The seal RPCs refuse a missing session ID, an unbound session, an untapped runtime (`activity_untapped:`), hold state (`coordinator_hold`), and a generation other than the entry's last fenced one (`coordinator_handoff_stale`), and a seal survives a later `CoordinatorFence` until a seal or unseal at the new generation. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.1 (CH-ATTACH), §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §10.1 (Horizontal Scaling).
- **[C6.63]** **Intent:** The executor's `Send` returns an error satisfying `ErrSessionSealed` with the delivered count and the delivered turns' response when the adapter ends the stream as sealed, calls no stream-failure handler, and opens a new stream on the next `Send`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.1 (CH-ATTACH).
- **[C6.64]** **Intent:** Every runtime transport (socket, in-process, MCP, and the subprocess executor) and the line hub deliver every output frame to the activity tap with no `Output` subscriber, across a restart that creates a new hub, and a write the MCP runtime refuses still stamps the entry. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[C6.65]** **Intent:** A platform or connector tool call in flight (rows A6 and A7) keeps its session unsealed with idle age 0 for as long as it blocks, the window restarts at its return, concurrent calls of either kind keep the count above zero until all return, a call on a two-slot server is refused with no entry changed, a call that outlives its entry changes no recreated entry, and `List` is not activity. **Tier:** 1, 3. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MCP-PLATFORM, CH-MCP-CONNECTOR), §9.2 (Elicitation Chain).
- **[C6.66]** **Intent:** A gateway idle firing during a platform call the runtime is waiting on leaves the row `running`, moves `LastAgentActivityAt` to the sweep time, and checkpoints and releases nothing, while a session with no call in flight is suspended by the same tick. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §9.2 (Elicitation Chain).
- **[C6.67]** **Intent:** Tool calls racing `SealSession`, and racing the entry's removal and recreation, never let a seal be placed while a call is in flight, and every entry's call count returns to zero and never goes negative. **Tier:** 7a. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MCP-PLATFORM, CH-MCP-CONNECTOR). **Discriminates:** resolving and counting a call outside the seal's lock.
- **[C6.68]** **Intent:** The gateway client and the adapter agree on the `SealSession` and `UnsealSession` wire fields against `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto`, an empty session ID is refused, an `Attach` message to a sealed session ends the stream with `session_sealed:`, and the fence-site and claim-register checks count both requests as generation-fenced. **Tier:** 0, 3. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.1 (CH-ATTACH).

Heartbeats and `busy`:

- **[C6.69]** **Intent:** The adapter's heartbeat frame names the session, as `{"type":"heartbeat","ts":<ts>,"sessionId":"<id>"}`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[C6.70]** **Intent:** Heartbeats for a session run from its `session_start` write with no `Attach` open and stop at `session_end` or deregistration, with the monitor exiting; no heartbeat follows `session_end` on the `DemoteSDK` path even when the entry stays; and no monitor runs on an MCP runtime, an untapped runtime, or with heartbeats disabled. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK), §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract). **Discriminates:** a heartbeat after `session_end` (OD-S8).
- **[C6.71]** **Intent:** A tapped `heartbeat_ack` with `busy: true` naming the session keeps `SealSession` answering not sealed with no `Attach` open, and the session seals one window after the last busy ack. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[C6.72]** **Intent:** A missed ack deadline interrupts the runtime once and ends the open `Attach` and every later `Attach` for the entry with `DeadlineExceeded`, a re-registered session heartbeats afresh, and acks reach the monitor through the tap and are never relayed to an `Attach` stream. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK), §4.7.1 (Role and Gateway RPC Contract).
- **[C6.73]** **Intent:** A stream failure between turns and mid-turn on the integration stack still triggers the heartbeat hang escalation with monitors started at `session_start` and acks routed through the tap. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §28.5.1 (CH-ATTACH), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[C6.74]** **Intent:** Each runtime SDK answers a heartbeat naming a session with `busy: true` and that `sessionId` while the session has a message routed and not finished (a running handler, a blocked `response` write, a queued message, or an unreleased busy hold, including one taken in `OnCreate`), omits `busy` after the response, an error response, a streaming non-final return, a release, or `session_end`, and answers an unaddressed heartbeat with the bare ack. **Tier:** 1, 3. **Spec:** §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK), §15.7 (Runtime Author SDKs).
- **[C6.75]** **Intent:** The reference concurrent echo runtime acks a heartbeat naming a held session from that session's worker after its `response`, and answers a heartbeat naming an unknown or ended session, or no session, with one bare ack and no drop diagnostic. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[C6.76]** **Intent:** The `heartbeat_busy` compliance check passes a runtime that reports `busy` with the `sessionId` while a message is outstanding and a serial runtime whose ack follows its response, fails one that answers a bare ack while handling, and is a basic-level category in the scaffold probe. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §15.4.6 (Conformance Test Suite), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).

Gateway idle-clock sites:

- **[C6.77]** **Intent:** Row G3: `lenny/await_children` stamps the calling session once per poll round while it blocks and not after it returns. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states).
- **[C6.78]** **Intent:** Row G4: the LLM proxy stamps a proxy-mode lease's session after each relayed stream line and before the stream ends, stamps nothing for a direct-mode lease or a lease with no session, relays unchanged with no stamper, and the gateway wires its stamper into the proxy handler. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states).
- **[C6.79]** **Intent:** Row G5: `RecordUsage` advances the idle clock of a proxy-mode lease's tenant and session and leaves it for a direct-mode lease or a lease with no tenant or session. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states).

Expansive tables (Testing **Expansive lifecycle tests**):

- **[X5]** **Intent:** The cells are every `suspended` edge that SPEC-5a and SPEC-5h stage × pod held or released × pool kind × serving replica (coordinator and peer) × held message (none, DLQ, and durable inbox) × runtime `busy` (true and false). The edges are idle suspension, interrupt timeout, `suspended → running`, `suspended → resume_pending`, the `maxSuspendedSessionSeconds` and `perChildMaxAge` expiries, the release at `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` with its step 3a, and the leader's backlog re-check.
