Each cell takes the staged edge or writes nothing, delivers each held message once, and leaves a state the invariant checker passes. **Cost floor.** Every edge, and every value of each other dimension. **Tier:** 1, 2 (pgstore and Redis). **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[X2a]** **Intent:** A `rapid` state machine applies random sequences of the X2 operations to the harness's replicas one at a time, runs the invariant checker after each step, and shrinks any failing sequence. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §7.3 (Resume driver), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).

Flags and chart:

- **[C6.80]** **Intent:** Every flag CODE-6 item 10 names parses with its default and environment variable, the renamed flag parses, and its former name is rejected. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §27.6 (Session lifecycle and cleanup).
- **[C6.81]** **Intent:** Each CODE-6 item 11 chart value renders into the gateway deployment from values. **Tier:** 0. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine).

### Tests that land with CODE-7

- **[C7.1]** **Intent:** Every non-terminal state counts toward the concurrent-session caps except `suspended`, and the memstore and pgstore counts agree with that predicate for every scope, counting `resuming`, `resume_pending`, and `awaiting_client_action` rows. **Tier:** 1, 2. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count). **Discriminates:** a SQL predicate that diverges from `session.CountsTowardConcurrencyCaps`.
- **[C7.2]** **Intent:** The tenant and per-user count queries use `idx_sessions_counted_by_tenant` on a tenant with many terminal and `suspended` rows, and migration 0184 replaces `idx_sessions_active_by_tenant` with it and its down file reverses the swap. **Tier:** 2. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.3]** **Intent:** The resume admission check returns nil, a cap refusal with its scope, limit, and count, or a non-refusal error for each scope at, below, and without its limit, for an unknown tenant, and for a count error, and makes no count call when every cap is disabled. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.4]** **Intent:** Session creation keeps its admission behavior, its refusal body equals the shared cap refusal for each scope, and a creation is admitted while the cap is full of `suspended` sessions. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.5]** **Intent:** `POST /resume` from a `suspended` row at a limit answers 429 `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` with the creation body and leaves the row and an empty DLQ, from `awaiting_client_action` at the same limit answers `200` with `resume_pending`, and on a count error answers 500 and writes nothing. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.6]** **Intent:** A message to a `suspended` session at a limit is refused with `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` on REST and MCP and is neither held nor delivered, and a count error answers 500 `INTERNAL_ERROR` on REST and a Go error on MCP with the same outcome. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.7]** **Intent:** A message admitted at the entry check whose resume write the cap then refuses is held with `queued` and the row stays `suspended`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C7.8]** **Intent:** A failure edge out of a held-pod `suspended` row at a limit is not admission-checked and writes `resume_pending`. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.9]** **Intent:** The backlog check returns false and starts no resume when the cap refuses or its count fails, and `sweepSuspended` then releases the pod when the hold ends. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6). **Discriminates:** a count error treated like a held-length error, which returns true.
- **[C7.10]** **Intent:** The leader's re-check leaves a podless row at a limit `suspended` and continues, and writes `resume_pending` on the first pass after a slot frees. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[C7.11]** **Intent:** The release step 3a at a limit, from `ReleaseSuspendedPod` and from `SuspendIdle` with an inbox entry, releases the pod with one release event, returns no error, leaves the row podless `suspended`, logs the refusing scope, and writes no `resume_pending` until a later re-check pass after a slot frees. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.12]** **Intent:** The session-availability ratio excludes `suspended` sessions from its population and is 0 when only `suspended` sessions exist. **Tier:** 1. **Spec:** §16.5 (Alerting Rules and SLOs), §11.1 (Admission and Fairness). **Discriminates:** a population that counts `suspended`.
- **[C7.13]** **Intent:** The cap refusals of REST `POST /messages` and `POST /resume` and of MCP `lenny/send_message` and `lenny/resume_session` to a `suspended` session at a limit carry the same code, category, and `details`. **Tier:** 3. **Spec:** §15.2.1 (REST/MCP Consistency Contract), §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).
- **[C7.14]** **Intent:** On compose Postgres and Redis at a tenant limit of one, a `suspended` session does not hold a slot, a message or `POST /resume` to it is refused with `limit` and `active` and nothing held until the other session ends, after which a message resumes it, and a podless `suspended` row with a held entry stays `suspended` under the re-check until a slot frees. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §11.1 (Concurrent-session count).

CODE-7 has no tier-7a intent: it adds no lock, reservation, or ordering, and SPEC-6a states that concurrent admissions can overshoot, as the creation path already does.

### TEST-1 · Tests at tier 2 and above, and the tier-0 and tier-11 checks

- **[T1.1]** **Intent:** The regenerated `openapi.json` and `generated_tools.go` match their generators. **Tier:** 0. **Spec:** §15.1 (REST API).
- **[T1.2]** **Intent:** In the harness that wires no pod binder, `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/resume` answers `200` with `resume_pending` from `awaiting_client_action` and `running` from `suspended`, and `RESUME_FAILED` is absent from the error catalog and `openapi.json`. **Tier:** 3. **Spec:** §15.1 (REST API), §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[T1.3]** **Intent:** `schemas/runtime-ops-events.schema.json` and the runtime-ops contract accept only `session_age` and `budget` as `deadline_approaching` triggers. **Tier:** 3. **Spec:** §28.5.3 (CH-RUNTIMEOPS).
- **[T1.4]** **Intent:** `openapi.json` and the generated MCP schema list no `sessionPolicy.maxSessionRetries`, and a runtime create that carries it does not persist it. **Tier:** 3. **Spec:** §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[T1.5]** **Intent:** `lenny/resume_session` and `lenny/interrupt_session` produce the same transition and session body as `POST /resume` and `POST /interrupt` from each admitted state, and the Go SDK's MCP `InterruptSession` returns the row's `id` and `state`. **Tier:** 3. **Spec:** §15.2.1 (REST/MCP Consistency Contract).
- **[T1.6]** **Intent:** The `heartbeat` and `heartbeat_ack` JSONL schema accepts `sessionId` on `heartbeat`, accepts a bare ack, `busy: false`, and `busy: true` with `sessionId`, rejects `busy: true` without `sessionId` and a non-boolean `busy`, and an adapter heartbeat read from a socket runtime carries the session's `sessionId`. **Tier:** 3. **Spec:** §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[T1.7]** **Intent:** A pod crash restores the session to `running` with no client call, and a `POST /resume` served by a replica that does not hold the session leads to `running`. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[T1.8]** **Intent:** With two replicas, killing the holder in `resume_pending` lets the peer adopt and drive the restore after the lease lapses, and deleting the live holder's lease key lets the peer adopt while the original replica renews nothing, writes nothing, and leaves no pod claimed. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination), §7.3 (Resume driver).
- **[T1.9]** **Intent:** A podless `suspended` session needs no coordinating replica: after idle suspension and the release on one replica, its lease key is absent and its lease row released, `GET /events` returns the same items on every replica, and `POST /terminate` on another replica completes it and drains its DLQ with no lease acquired. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination), §29.3 (Interactive message send).
- **[T1.10]** **Intent:** Held messages reach the runtime in order with no client call on the compose stack: a message to a podless `suspended` session is delivered after the restore and before a later message; a message to a `ready` session is held in the shared DLQ and delivered after `POST /start` before a later message; and a durable inbox entry present at `resume_pending` keeps no TTL and is delivered before DLQ entries enqueued later. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model).
- **[T1.11]** **Intent:** A message to a held-pod `suspended` session whose `running` write fails waits in the shared DLQ, is delivered by the coordinating replica's next suspended sweep once the write succeeds, and otherwise expires so that a `DELETE` sends its sender `message_expired` with `target_terminated`; and a message served by the non-holder is answered `queued` and delivered once after the holder's next tick. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[T1.12]** **Intent:** The leader's re-check moves a podless `suspended` row with a stranded DLQ entry to `resume_pending`, and the driver delivers the message. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[T1.13]** **Intent:** The user-erasure job deletes the erased user's session DLQ and durable-inbox keys and keeps another user's. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §12.8 (Compliance Interfaces).
- **[T1.14]** **Intent:** A `DELETE`, a `POST /terminate`, and `lenny/cancel_session`, each served by a replica other than the coordinator, in `running`, held-pod `suspended`, `resume_pending`, and `awaiting_client_action`, write the terminal state on the serving replica (the recorded F-10.1.33 departure). The coordinator's binding is then released with the state's disposition within two Sweeper intervals (D6). The session's credential leases are returned by the coordinator's release while the serving replica returns none, and no pod, slot, or credential lease remains. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[T1.15]** **Intent:** After an interrupt whose hold release ends the pod, an elicitation the runtime raised answers `404 ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` on REST and `ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` on MCP, and the session's resumed runtime receives no elicitation result. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain), §6.2 (Pod State Machine).
- **[T1.16]** **Intent:** On Kind, an idle session is suspended and its pod released with the recycle disposition on a recycling pool and resumed on a fresh pod by a later message; on a concurrent pool the release frees one slot and the other session keeps answering; and a held-pod `suspended` session's adopter fences the pod after its coordinator is killed. **Tier:** 5. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol).
- **[X2]** **Intent:** Randomized interleaving. Concurrent goroutines on two or three replicas issue operations whose recorded calls the seeded scheduler releases in an order taken from the seed, and the invariant checker runs at quiescence. The operations are:
  - terminal writes through each D7 path class (`DELETE`, terminate, failure report, the watchdog sweeps, `lenny/cancel_session`, the cascade, `ForceTerminate`, and the budget terminator);
  - `DriveResume`, `POST /resume`, and `POST /start`;
  - message sends to the coordinating replica and to a peer replica;
  - a delegated child's binding publish;
  - Sweeper takeover and lease lapse;
  - `SuspendIdle`, `ReleaseSuspendedPod`, and the backlog re-check;
  - `ReattachNode`;
  - the `full_revoke` write and fan-out.
  Concurrent-session cap operations are excluded (CODE-7 has no tier-7a intent). Every run also schedules each required pair below through a rendezvous in both orders, and each pair keeps its own expected outcome:
  - **[T1.17]** **Intent:** Two drivers on one `resume_pending` row produce one entry write and one commit and leave no pod claimed, and two watchdogs ticking on one store exit each `resuming` incarnation at most once with `retryCount` never above `maxRetries`.
  - **[T1.18]** **Intent:** A message to an idle-suspended session, on the coordinating or another replica, racing the zero-hold release always ends with the session in `resume_pending` or `running` and the message delivered. **Discriminates:** a podless `suspended` session left holding the message.
  - **[T1.19]** **Intent:** `ReattachNode` racing the watchdog driver on one descendant claims exactly one pod, and `POST /resume` racing `DELETE` ends `cancelled` with no pod claimed.
  - **[T1.20]** **Intent:** Message sends racing `SuspendIdle` never end a turn: each `message` the runtime received has its `response` written before the release `Shutdown`, and each message is answered once or held in the DLQ. **Discriminates:** a delivery that starts between the seal and the release.
  - **[T1.21]** **Intent:** A stale coordinator's idle suspension after a peer took the session over at a later generation never ends a turn: the row stays `running` on its pod at the new generation, the stale replica neither unseals nor sends `Shutdown`, and the peer's turn completes.
  - **[T1.22]** **Intent:** Held messages are delivered exactly once and in order under concurrency: a message arriving during `deliverBuffered` is delivered after the buffered ones; messages held against `resume_pending` on one replica while another commits and delivers each reach the runtime once with nothing left queued; and a relinquished resume whose session a same-replica sweep adopts delivers the held entry once and publishes `children_reattached` once.
  - **[T1.23]** **Intent:** The leader's re-check racing `enterResumePending` from a message hold and from release step 3a records exactly one `suspended → resume_pending` transition.
  - **[T1.24]** **Intent:** A suspension release racing a resume started after its clear leaves the session `running` with exactly the leases the resume assigned and none from before the suspension, and on a slot pool a release held until the resume published its replacement binding leaves that binding, its slot, and its leases intact with no release event. **Discriminates:** a release that skips the pointer-identity check under `slotAccountLocks`.
  - **[T1.25]** **Intent:** Concurrent terminal releases of one bound session (two `ReleaseTerminalBinding` calls and the writer's hook) release the pod once with the cancelled disposition, release each lease once after a `sealed` snapshot, and leave no binding and no open terminal claim.
  - The pairs of intents C4.25 and C4.58.
  **Tier:** 7a. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §8.10 (Delegation Tree Recovery), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §10.1.5 (Stale Replica Behavior), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol), §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod), §6.2 (maxClientIdleSeconds clock behavior across states), §28.5.1 (CH-ATTACH). **Discriminates:** the bugs the folded intents name, a lease returned by a component that D6 **Credential lease return.** does not name, and any ordering among the operations that no pair names.
- **[T1.26]** **Intent:** A respond racing `ExpireRaisedBy` on pgstore has exactly one outcome: the respond wins and the delete counts 0, or the delete counts 1 and the respond finds nothing. **Tier:** 7a. **Spec:** §9.2 (Elicitation Chain).
- **[T1.27]** **Intent:** Killing the coordinator in `resume_pending` and in held-pod `suspended`, with a message held in the DLQ before the peer adopts, recovers the session on the peer with the message delivered at least once and the DLQ empty. **Tier:** 8. **Spec:** §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[T1.28]** **Intent:** A coordinator killed after the entry write leaves a claim the controller's orphan GC deletes (marking the Sandbox and counting the orphan), and the session reaches `running` on another pod through the watchdog and the peer's driver; and a crash between the suspension's binding clear and the release leaves an orphan claim and an intact checkpoint from which the session resumes. **Tier:** 8. **Spec:** §7.3 (Resume driver), §4.6.1 (Warm Pool Controller (Pod Lifecycle)).
- **[T1.29]** **Intent:** A runtime handling a message is not idle-suspended across a coordinator kill: the adopter leaves the row `running` until the handler returns, then idle-suspends within one idle bound, one heartbeat interval, and one watchdog tick without opening `Attach`, and a session with no handler running is suspended within the same bound. **Tier:** 8. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol).
- **[T1.30]** **Intent:** A held-message read outage (a Redis partition) does not stall or refuse delivery to a `running` session, and after it heals the held entry precedes the next message and the DLQ empties. **Tier:** 8. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6). **Discriminates:** a read that hangs rather than fails.
- **[T1.31]** **Intent:** A Postgres primary failover or partition does not refuse or stall a REST or MCP message to a `running` session beyond the primary-read deadline, and delivery continues after recovery. **Tier:** 8. **Spec:** §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6), §12.3 (Postgres HA Requirements). **Discriminates:** a primary read that hangs during a partition.
- **[T1.32]** **Intent:** During a primary failover, a message classified on a stale `running` row of a session an idle suspension has sealed reaches no runtime and answers `error` with `inbox_unavailable`, and after recovery the suspension completes and a later message resumes the session. **Tier:** 8. **Spec:** §6.2 (Pod State Machine), §7.2 (Message delivery routing — seven paths, path 6).
- **[X4]** **Intent:** Fault injection at every step. For each scenario of X1, X3, and X5 that writes a session row, a clean run records the call trace. The scenario then re-runs once for each trace index × fault kind (store error, context cancel, crash between that call and the next, and peer takeover at that call). After each re-run, the surviving replicas reach quiescence, the invariant checker passes, and the session reaches the state the staged spec gives for that interruption. Two re-runs of one scenario are duplicates when their traces agree call by call, with call sites, through the fault index and they inject the same fault kind, and a run executes one re-run of each duplicate set. The harness runs on compose Postgres and Redis. **Cost.** Each pull-request run injects each fault kind at each distinct call site that the clean traces reach, within the tier-8 time budget. The nightly run executes every deduplicated re-run within the tier-8 time budget. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the re-run that covers each call site in the pull-request run, and the sharding of the nightly run. **Tier:** 8. **Spec:** §10.1.2 (Coordinator Handoff Protocol), §7.3 (Resume driver), §7.2 (Interactive Session Model), §12.3 (Postgres HA Requirements). **Discriminates:** a path that leaks a claim, slot, lease, or queue entry when it is interrupted between two effects.
- **[T1.33]** **Intent:** On Kind with a proxy-delivery credential pool, an idle-suspended session's old proxy token is rejected, and the session resumed on another pod holds a new lease whose token authenticates. **Tier:** 9. **Spec:** §7.3 (Credential leases on the replacement pod), §4.9 (Credential Leasing Service).
- **[T1.34]** **Intent:** The `heartbeat_busy` check passes on every reference runtime, every SDK scaffold, an SDK fixture that delays its response, and an always-busy fixture, and fails on a fixture that answers a bare ack while handling. **Tier:** 10. **Spec:** §15.4.6 (Conformance Test Suite), §28.5.3 (CH-MSGSOCK).
- **[T1.35]** **Intent:** The docs and the flag help are consistent with the staged spec, and no reader page names `RESUME_FAILED` or `maxSessionRetries`. **Tier:** 11. **Spec:** §15.1 (REST API), §7.3 (Retry and Resume).
- **[T1.36]** **Intent:** With two replicas, a delegated child running on replica B, and a sibling in `awaiting_client_action`, a resume of the root or of the sibling committed on replica A leaves the child `running` on its pod. No second claim is made, no tree-recovery failure is recorded, and the child's `lenny_delegation_tree_recovery_*` outcome is not `partial_failure`. **Tier:** 4. **Spec:** §8.10 (Delegation Tree Recovery), §10.1.1 (Stateless Replicas and Per-Session Coordination).
- **[T1.37]** **Intent:** On a recycling pool with `maxConcurrentSessions: 1`, a session whose `CH-ATTACH` stream fails with `Internal` has its pod retired, with the claim deleted and never patched `recycling`. This holds when the row was already `completed` or `cancelled` and the post-terminal hook or D6 releases the binding, and when an idle suspension's write won the race against the failure report and the suspension release ran with `DispositionCompleted`. A control session with no stream failure recycles. At tier 7a, a stream failure concurrent with a terminal release is `-race` clean, and a failure whose `endConn` has run before the terminal write always retires the pod. **Tier:** 4, 7a. **Spec:** §5.2 (Pool Configuration and Execution Modes), §28.5.1 (Gateway-to-pod).

## Edge cases and accepted failure modes

A bullet below that names a BUILD-GAPS finding and a follow-up owner is a recorded departure. A test that reaches its path asserts the outcome the bullet states, and this proposal changes no code to close it. A bullet that leaves an invariant of the Testing **Expansive lifecycle tests** checker unmet has a row in Testing **Invariant relaxations.**, which states the cells and the asserted outcome.

- **Hung pre-claim wait.** A claim that waits on an exhausted pool is bounded by the remaining window, after which the attempt context is cancelled and `sweepResumePending` moves the row to `awaiting_client_action`.
- **Driver goroutine at shutdown.** The server-lifetime context cancels an in-flight attempt. An attempt cancelled before the hook writes nothing. One cancelled after the hook leaves either a `resuming` row for `sweepResuming` whose claim the binder's detached release deletes (CODE-3 item 3 **Detached release.**), or that the claim-orphan GC deletes when the process exits first, or a `running` row whose commit landed unacknowledged. The peer's adoption takes that row's pod and its held messages (CODE-5 item 2, CODE-4 item 26), and its tail takes the **Context ended** column of the CODE-4 item 5 **Post-commit tail.** table (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**).
- **A resolution the expiry does not catch.** SPEC-5p2 states the outcomes. In code, the misses are an `ExpireRaisedBy` error (logged at WARN), the watchdog's `sweepResuming` write, a suspension release whose lock wait the context ends, a terminal write that runs no post-terminal hook (Design **Terminal transition.** (D7) states which), and an elicitation raised by a single-shot session that a translator completes, because the translators run no hook (P2, BUILD-GAPS F-15.1.46 and F-15.1.47).
- **Message held by a non-coordinator replica during a delivery.** The delivery lock does not reach it. SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** states when the entry is delivered. A message to a `running` target follows the §29.3 `running` row (RECORDS-1 item 7(f)). When the serving replica buffers it in its own session inbox, it is answered `queued` and stays in that inbox until that replica coordinates the session or the session ends, and no other replica's delivery reaches it. Owner: R19.
- **Idle-suspended session with a delegation tree.** Children keep running. The parent's suspension releases only its own pod or slot.
- **Seal refused, unavailable, or not lifted.** SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** states the outcome of a refusal, and SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** states who lifts the seal and how a seal that is not lifted ends. An `unsealSession` error is logged with the session ID.
- **`busy` unattributed, and sessions that cannot be idle-suspended.** SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** and SPEC-5f state the outcomes; CODE-6 items 4, 5, and 18 implement them.
- **Takeover racing the entry write.** A Sweeper whose `List` read a row in `resume_pending` can acquire the lease after the driver's entry write. Its `RecordHandoff` compare-and-swap at the listed generation misses, it releases the lease, and the incarnation continues (CODE-5 item 2b).
- **Terminal transition during an attempt.** SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** and CODE-4 item 2a state the outcome, and Decision 21 records why.
- **Held entry in flight at a terminal write.** The **Terminal stop.** (CODE-4 item 6) reads the read pool. After a terminal write, `deliverBuffered` can therefore still deliver the entry whose `Send` is in flight, and entries it takes within the read pool's lag. Such an entry can reach the runtime and also be reported `message_expired` by the terminal drain. The resumer's `session.resumed` and `status_change(running)` can follow the terminal events when the write lands after the tail's re-check. BUILD-GAPS F-7.2.36 (TT-85, P2) tracks emissions after a terminal write.
- **Watchdog anchor.** `sweepResuming` measures from `UpdatedAt`, which every write in the incarnation advances, including the asynchronous `AdvanceLastSeq`. The watchdog therefore fires `MaxResumingSeconds` after the last write in the incarnation, while §6.2 states a watchdog set on entry. This predates the proposal and is accepted. `sweepResumePending` and the **`attemptResume`** step 2 bound measure the `maxResumeWindowSeconds` cap from the same `UpdatedAt`, so a later write that leaves a `resume_pending` row in `resume_pending` restarts the cap, while §6.2 starts it on entry to `resume_pending`. This also predates the proposal and is accepted.
- **Lease mirror lag.** A barrier sent within one Sweeper interval of a resume commit carries a stale generation (RECORDS-1 item 7(g)).
- **Held message whose `resume_pending` write does not follow.** SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** states the outcome (CODE-6 items 4 step 3a, 7, and 16).
- **Message during a §12.3 primary failover.** D5 **Message-path read failures.** states the outcome for an entry row that reads `running`, and CODE-7 item 4 the outcome for one that reads `suspended`.
- **Snapshotless rebuild with long setup.** The 300 s watchdog and `claimOrphanTimeout` bound the rebuild below the setup cap a runtime may declare (RECORDS-1 item 7(m)).
- **Pod release on a replica that lost the lease.** The release returns the attempt's minted leases and the session's leases at or below its binding's stamp (CODE-4 item 24), so a peer restore's leases survive. SPEC-1f item 3 states the outcome.
- **Credential leases with no binding releaser.** A lease is returned only by its binding's releaser or its minting attempt (Design D6 **Credential lease return.**). When neither runs, the lease stays recorded and counted against its pool until a later pod release of the session whose binding stamp is at or above the lease's generation returns it (CODE-4 item 24), or until it expires. This happens when:
  - the replica that holds the binding exits before it releases the binding, including between a terminal write and its hook on that replica;
  - the session row is hard-deleted while bound;
  - a takeover leaves a binding that no replica publishes, or a terminal row has no binding at its generation (**Terminal row with no binding at its generation.**);
  - `ReleaseUncommitted` finds its reclaim unconfirmed (CODE-4 item 5.3).

  This is an accepted departure from §7.1 step 23. Its fallback is lease expiry. The LLM proxy and the adapter stop honoring the lease at its `expiresAt`, which `leaseTTLSeconds` bounds (3600 s under the provider defaults), and the §4.9 expired-lease sweep deletes its record. Two tree defects weaken the fallback, and proposal P1 owns both:
  - the renewal worker on the replica that tracks the lease re-mints it at each `renewBefore` without checking session liveness (F-4.9.32), so the lease expires only after that replica's process exits;
  - expiry deletes the record without returning the pool slot (RECORDS-1 item 7(v)).

  With the in-memory per-replica lease store, a releaser on a replica other than the minter finds no record, so a deployment that runs more than one replica uses the Postgres lease store. No metric names the condition. A pool whose utilization does not fall, and `CREDENTIAL_POOL_EXHAUSTED` on later sessions, reveal it.
- **Held message delivered again after a coordinator change.** No code implements the adapter's `delivered_message_ids` suppression (RECORDS-1 item 7(i)). A held entry whose delivery a coordinator change or an injected fault interrupts between the runtime's consumption and the entry's removal is delivered again at the next delivery point. The message reaches the runtime at least once and at most once more per coordinator change or interruption, its sender receives no `message_expired`, and the entry is removed after its last delivery. Owner: R20.
- **Terminal binding reclamation latency.** Design **Terminal binding reclamation.** (D6) states the bound and its accepted gaps.
- **Terminal write without its hook.** Design **Terminal transition.** (D7) states the outcomes.
- **Renewal racing a release by identifier.** `ReleaseUncommitted` and `failResume` release an attempt's leases by identifier (CODE-4 items 5.3 and 20.4). When a lease's renewal passes its post-check before that release, the replacement stays held by the session and keeps renewing, because the release names only the spent identifier. The replacement records the attempt's generation `G` (CODE-4 item 24), so the session's next pod release returns it, because that binding's stamp is at or above `G`, and the release lists the session's leases twice (CODE-4 item 23.1). Until then it counts against its pool. The case is reachable only when `leaseTTLSeconds` minus `renewBeforeBufferSeconds` is shorter than one attempt, which `maxResumingSeconds` (default 300 s) bounds, and the provider defaults (3600 s and 300 s) exclude it.
- **Uncommitted replacement pod disposition.** `ReleaseUncommitted` and `failResume` retire an exclusive pod by deleting its claim. They release a slot reservation without the occupancy-zero recycle, whatever state ended the incarnation. §7.2 snapshot-close step 3 instead releases the pod through the standard path, which recycles a recycling pool's pod after `cancelled` or `completed`. The warm pool replaces the retired pod. A reservation release that fails leaves the slot counted until its pod terminates, which fails closed. Owner: P1 (BUILD-GAPS F-10.1.21).
- **Erasure racing a live session.** A message enqueued to one of the user's sessions, in its DLQ or its durable inbox, between CODE-6 item 17's key delete and step 17's row delete stays in Redis with no TTL, and no sweep reaches a key whose row is gone. §12.8 applies the same in-flight-session posture to every session-scoped store. P5 makes erasure end the user's sessions and wait for their hooks before the erasers run, and BUILD-GAPS F-7.2.32 (TT-81, P2) tracks keys written after the drain.
- **Cap full while held messages wait.** A podless `suspended` session with held messages stays `suspended` while a limit it counts toward is full, and the gateway leader retries it on each pass; its messages wait until the check admits the resume or the session expires (CODE-7 item 5).
- **Terminal row with no binding at its generation.** A terminal write can land when no replica holds a binding stamped at the row's generation. This happens after a **Relinquished** or **Above `G`** exit whose adopter has not published, after a takeover whose readopt failed (CODE-5 item 2), or after `ReleaseUncommitted` left an unconfirmed pod. D6 evicts a binding stamped below the row's generation without a binder call, and the attempt sends no RPC at a generation it does not hold (§10.1.5). No replica releases the pod. The claim-orphan GC reclaims an exclusive pod. A concurrent-workspace slot stays counted until its pod terminates, which fails closed. A lease the attempt minted follows **Credential leases with no binding releaser.** Owner: P1 (BUILD-GAPS F-10.1.21, TT-14).
- **Pre-running claim at a `none` terminal write.** `terminateUserSessions` takes the `none` path (Design **Terminal transition.** (D7)) and runs no post-terminal hook. A `created`, `finalizing`, or `ready` row it cancels whose `PodAssignment` names a claim that no registry holds therefore has no releaser, because D6 acts only on a registry binding and the `full_revoke` fan-out sends `Shutdown` only to registry bindings. The claim-orphan GC reclaims an exclusive pod. A concurrent-workspace reservation stays counted until its pod terminates, which fails closed. The `full_revoke` lease revocation removes the session's leases. Owner: P1 (RECORDS-1 item 7(x)).
- **Suspension release racing a terminal write on one replica.** `ReleaseSuspendedPod` and `SuspendIdle` check this replica's terminal claims at entry and again under `slotAccountLocks` before the release. Suppose a writer's claim opens after that second check and its hook's seal reads the registry before the release's unbind. The seal then exports a workspace being scrubbed, or finds no binding and records success. Owner: P2 (BUILD-GAPS F-7.1.22, TT-3).
- **Over-cap row entering `running`.** §6.2 evaluates the age timer on every transition into `running`. CODE-1 item 5 states which writes into `running` check the cap. The departure is a held-pod `suspended` row that paused over its cap within one watchdog interval of crossing it: `resumeHeldPod` resumes it, and it runs for up to one watchdog interval before `sweepMaxAge` expires it. An over-cap `resume_pending` row is not driven; it moves to `awaiting_client_action` at its window, unless `sweepMaxAge` expires it first, which is pre-existing behavior. The expiry of paused states other than `suspended` belongs to P3 (BUILD-GAPS F-6.2.28).
- **Crash not yet handled at a release's unbind.** A release whose unbind precedes `endConn`'s handling of the stream's end ends the stream itself, which §28.5.1 counts as no failure, and SPEC-5b step 2 states the outcome for the suspension release. CODE-4 item 22a states which releases read the crash mark.
- **Held-message outcomes that overlap.** A batch the drain took in part answers `TARGET_TERMINAL`, and the drained members also get `message_expired`. A re-read error answers `error` for members the drain already reported. Each overlap reports a message as not consumed twice, never as both consumed and not consumed. An enqueue that evicts another sender's entry gives that entry no outcome. Owner: P2 (BUILD-GAPS F-7.2.34 and F-7.2.32).
